这位精妙艺术的大师不过是一个人物而已；但正如我所说，在我所认识的那些精妙艺术的大师们中间，性情的多样性是无穷的。有些是伟大的印象派。他们将对上帝与无限的敬畏印在你心上——或者换句话说，对在一切恐怖而壮丽的场景中被淹死的畏惧。或许有人认为，你因水中窒息而逝去的地点其实并不太要紧。我对此不那么确定。也许我过于敏感了，但我承认，那种在黑暗与喧嚣中突然被抛入狂怒大海的想法，总让我产生一种退缩的厌恶感。死在池塘里，尽管无知者或许会称之为可耻的命运，但与我在心灵深处战栗的、另几种尘世生涯的结局相比，仍是一种明亮而宁静的终结。

===Original===
But let that pass. Some of the masters whose influence left a trace upon my character to this very day, combined a fierceness of conception with a certitude of execution upon the basis of just appreciation of means and ends which is the highest quality of the man of action. And an artist is a man of action, whether he creates a personality, invents an expedient, finds the issue of a complicated situation.

===Chinese===
但此事姑且搁下。有些大师的影响至今仍在我性格中留下痕迹，他们将凶猛的构想与基于对手段与目的恰切把握之上的执行确定性相结合——这是行动者（man of action）的最高品质。而艺术家正是行动者，无论他是创造一个性格、发明一种权宜之计，还是为一个复杂局面找到出路。

===Original===
There were masters, too, I have known, whose very art consisted in avoiding every conceivable situation. It is needless to say that they never did great things in their craft; but they were not to be despised for that. They were modest; they understood their limitations. Their own masters had not handed the sacred fire into the keeping of their cold and skilful hands. One of those last I remember specially, now gone to his rest from that sea which his temperament must have made a scene of little more than a peaceful pursuit. Once only did he attempt a stroke of audacity, one early morning, with a steady breeze, entering a crowded roadstead. But he was not genuine in this display which might have been art. He was thinking of his own self; he hankered after the meretricious glory of a showy performance.

===Chinese===
我也曾认识一些大师，他们的艺术恰恰在于回避一切可以想象的情境。不必说，他们在自己的行当里从未成就过伟业；但因此便看不起他们是不应该的。他们谦逊，他们明白自己的局限。他们自己的师傅未曾将那神圣的火种交到他们那双冷峻而灵巧的手中。我记得其中最后一位特别清楚，他如今已从那片海洋安息——他的性情本已将那片海化为不过是一场平静追寻的场景。只有一回，他曾尝试过一次大胆之举，在一个清晨，伴着稳定的微风，驶入一个拥挤的锚地。但他在这本可成为艺术的展示中并不真诚。他只想着自己；他渴求那浮华表演的虚荣荣光。

===Original===
As, rounding a dark, wooded point, bathed in fresh air and sunshine, we opened to view a crowd of shipping at anchor lying half a mile ahead of us perhaps, he called me aft from my station on the forecastle head, and, turning over and over his binoculars in his brown hands, said: “Do you see that big, heavy ship with white lower masts? I am going to take up a berth between her and the shore. Now do you see to it that the men jump smartly at the first order.”

===Chinese===
当我们的船绕过一个林木幽深的海角，沐浴在清新的空气和阳光中，前方约半英里处，一群锚泊的船只展现在眼前时，他从我所在的艏楼船头将我唤到船尾，在棕色的双手中反复转动着双筒望远镜（binoculars），说道："你看到那条下桅刷成白色的大船了吗？我要在她和岸边之间找一个泊位。你留神着，让伙计们听到第一道命令就机灵点跳动起来。"

===Original===
I answered, “Ay, ay, sir,” and verily believed that this would be a fine performance. We dashed on through the fleet in magnificent style. There must have been many open mouths and following eyes on board those ships﻿—Dutch, English, with a sprinkling of Americans and a German or two﻿—who had all hoisted their flags at eight o’clock as if in honour of our arrival. It would have been a fine performance if it had come off, but it did not. Through a touch of self-seeking that modest artist of solid merit became untrue to his temperament. It was not with him art for art’s sake: it was art for his own sake; and a dismal failure was the penalty he paid for that greatest of sins. It might have been even heavier, but, as it happened, we did not run our ship ashore, nor did we knock a large hole in the big ship whose lower masts were painted white. But it is a wonder that we did not carry away the cables of both our anchors, for, as may be imagined, I did not stand upon the order to “Let go!” that came to me in a quavering, quite unknown voice from his trembling lips. I let them both go with a celerity which to this day astonishes my memory. No average merchantman’s anchors have ever been let go with such miraculous smartness. And they both held. I could have kissed their rough, cold iron palms in gratitude if they had not been buried in slimy mud under ten fathoms of water. Ultimately they brought us up with the jibboom of a Dutch brig poking through our spanker﻿—nothing worse. And a miss is as good as a mile.

===Chinese===
我答道："是，是，先生"，并且真心相信这将是一场精彩的表演。我们以壮观的姿态冲过船队。那些船上一定有许多张开的嘴和追随的目光——荷兰船、英国船，间或几艘美国船和一两艘德国船——他们在八点钟都升起了旗帜，仿佛是为我们的到来致敬。倘若表演成功，那本该是场精彩的表演，但它没有。因为一丝自私的念头，那位谦逊而实力不凡的艺术家背离了自己的性情。于他而言，这不是为艺术而艺术：这是为他自己而艺术；他为这最大的罪过付出的代价，是一场惨败。或许代价本该更重，但碰巧，我们没有让自己的船搁浅，也没有在那条下桅刷成白色的船上撞出一个大洞。不过，奇迹是我们没有拽断两根锚链（cables），因为可以想见，当他颤抖的嘴唇发出那颤抖、陌生的声音下达"松锚！"（Let go!）的命令时，我没有丝毫犹豫。我以至今令我自己记忆犹新的敏捷将它们都放了下去。没有哪艘普通商船的锚，曾以如此神奇的速度被放下去过。而它们都抓住了底。如果它们不是埋在十英寻（fathom）深水下的淤泥里，我真想亲吻它们那粗糙、冰冷的铁掌以示感激。最终，它们让我们停住了，只是荷兰双桅横帆船（Dutch brig）的艏斜桅（jibboom）戳穿了我们的后桅纵帆（spanker）——仅此而已。失之毫厘，差之千里。

===Original===
But not in art. Afterwards the master said to me in a shy mumble, “She wouldn’t luff up in time, somehow. What’s the matter with her?” And I made no answer.

===Chinese===
但在艺术中不是这样。事后，大师害羞地咕哝着对我说："她没能及时迎风偏转（luff）上来。她出什么毛病了？"我没有回答。

===Original===
Yet the answer was clear. The ship had found out the momentary weakness of her man. Of all the living creatures upon land and sea, it is ships alone that cannot be taken in by barren pretences, that will not put up with bad art from their masters.

===Chinese===
然而答案是清晰的。船已经看穿了她的主人那片刻的软弱。在陆地和海上所有的生灵中，唯有船舶不会被空洞的伪装欺骗，不会容忍主人拙劣的艺术。

## Cobwebs and Gossamer / 蛛丝与轻纱

### X / 第十篇

===Original===
From the main truck of the average tall ship the horizon describes a circle of many miles, in which you can see another ship right down to her waterline; and these very eyes which follow this writing have counted in their time over a hundred sail becalmed, as if within a magic ring, not very far from the Azores—ships more or less tall. There were hardly two of them heading exactly the same way, as if each had meditated breaking out of the enchanted circle at a different point of the compass. But the spell of the calm is a strong magic. The following day still saw them scattered within sight of each other and heading different ways; but when, at the last, the breeze came with the darkling ripple that ran very blue on a pale sea, they all went in the same direction together. For this was the homeward-bound fleet from the far-off ends of the earth, and a Falmouth fruit-schooner, the smallest of them all, was heading the flight. One could have imagined her very fair, if not divinely tall, leaving a scent of lemons and oranges in her wake.

===Chinese===
从一般高桅大船的主桅顶望去，地平线画出一个方圆数英里的圆圈，另一艘船的水线都尽收眼底；而此刻写下这些字句的这双眼睛，当年曾在亚速尔群岛（Azores）附近数过上百艘帆船——高低不等——静静地停泊着，仿佛被一个魔环困住了。它们几乎没有两艘的航向完全一致，仿佛每艘都在盘算从罗盘的不同方位冲出这道魔咒。但无风的咒力是强大的魔法。第二天望去，它们仍然各自散落、互在视线之内，航向依旧各异；然而当微风终于吹来，在苍白的海面上荡起幽暗的蓝色涟漪时，它们便齐齐地朝同一个方向驶去。因为这是一支从天涯海角返航的船队，而法尔茅斯（Falmouth）的一条水果纵帆船，其中最小的一条，正引领着这场归航。你几乎可以想象她秀美而不失威仪，身后拖着柠檬与橙子的芬芳。

===Original===
The next day there were very few ships in sight from our mastheads—seven at most, perhaps, with a few more distant specks, hull down, beyond the magic ring of the horizon. The spell of the fair wind has a subtle power to scatter a white-winged company of ships looking all the same way, each with its white fillet of tumbling foam under the bow. It is the calm that brings ships mysteriously together; it is your wind that is the great separator.

===Chinese===
第二天从桅顶望去，视野里已寥寥无几——顶多七艘，还有几个更远的黑点，船身隐没在地平线魔环之外。顺风有着一种微妙的力量，能将一群白翼船舶——它们原先都朝同一个方向，船首各自压出一道翻滚的白沫——驱散得七零八落。是无风把船神秘地聚拢在一起，是风，才是伟大的离散者。

===Original===
The taller the ship, the further she can be seen; and her white tallness breathed upon by the wind first proclaims her size. The tall masts holding aloft the white canvas, spread out like a snare for catching the invisible power of the air, emerge gradually from the water, sail after sail, yard after yard, growing big, till, under the towering structure of her machinery, you perceive the insignificant, tiny speck of her hull.

===Chinese===
船越高，便能看得越远；而被风轻拂的白色高耸船身，最先昭示她的体量。高高的桅杆撑起白色的帆布——像一张张罗网，捕捉那无形的空气之力——自水面渐次显现，一帆接一帆，一桁接一桁，越长越大，直到你透过那高耸的帆装结构，看见下方微不足道的一小点——那便是船体。

===Original===
The tall masts are the pillars supporting the balanced planes that, motionless and silent, catch from the air the ship's motive-power, as it were a gift from Heaven vouchsafed to the audacity of man; and it is the ship's tall spars, stripped and shorn of their white glory, that incline themselves before the anger of the clouded heaven.

===Chinese===
高桅是支撑平衡翼面的柱子，那翼面纹丝不动、默然无声，从空气中攫取船舶的驱动力——仿佛上天赐予人之胆魄的一份馈赠；而正是船的高大圆材，在剥尽白色荣光之后，向阴云密布的天穹的怒意俯首。

===Original===
When they yield to a squall in a gaunt and naked submission, their tallness is brought best home even to the mind of a seaman. The man who has looked upon his ship going over too far is made aware of the preposterous tallness of a ship's spars. It seems impossible but that those gilt trucks which one had to tilt one's head back to see, now falling into the lower plane of vision, must perforce hit the very edge of the horizon. Such an experience gives you a better impression of the loftiness of your spars than any amount of running aloft could do. And yet in my time the royal yards of an average profitable ship were a good way up above her decks.

===Chinese===
当它们在飑风面前以嶙峋而赤裸的姿态屈服时，那种高耸便深深印入了水手的心智。亲眼目睹自己的船倾斜得过分的人，会深切意识到船的圆材是何等荒唐地高大。那些要仰起头才能看到的鎏金桅顶——如今却跌入视线的低位——似乎不可能不径直撞上地平线的边缘。这样一次经历，比你攀爬无数次桅梯所获得的对桅杆高耸的感受，都要深刻得多。而在我那个年头，一条中等盈利的船的皇桅帆桁（royal yards），离甲板也还是高得很。

===Original===
No doubt a fair amount of climbing up iron ladders can be achieved by an active man in a ship's engine-room, but I remember moments when even to my supple limbs and pride of nimbleness the sailing-ship's machinery seemed to reach up to the very stars.

===Chinese===
诚然，在机舱里一个灵活的人可以攀爬相当多的铁梯，但我记得有些时刻，即便以我柔软的四肢和敏捷的自负，帆船的那套"机械装置"仿佛直抵星辰。

===Original===
For machinery it is, doing its work in perfect silence and with a motionless grace, that seems to hide a capricious and not always governable power, taking nothing away from the material stores of the earth. Not for it the unerring precision of steel moved by white steam and living by red fire and fed with black coal. The other seems to draw its strength from the very soul of the world, its formidable ally, held to obedience by the frailest bonds, like a fierce ghost captured in a snare of something even finer than spun spun. For what is the array of the strongest ropes, the tallest spars and the stoutest canvas against the mighty breath of the infinite, but thistle stalks, cobwebs and gossamer?

===Chinese===
因为这正是那套机械装置，以完美的寂静和凝然不动的优雅工作着，却似乎暗藏着一股反复无常、并非总能驯服的力量，而且不耗取大地的任何物质储备。它不像那钢铁，被白色的蒸汽驱动，靠红色的火焰维生，以黑色的煤炭喂养，精准而从无差池。这一位——帆装——仿佛从世界的灵魂中汲取力量，它那令人生畏的盟友，只被最纤弱的纽带束缚着，宛如一头凶猛的幽灵，被一张比丝线更细的罗网捕获。因为面对那无穷巨力的呼吸，最坚韧的绳索、最高的圆材、最厚实的帆布——又算什么？不过是蓟草的茎、蛛网和游丝罢了。

### XI / 第十一篇

===Original===
Indeed, it is less than nothing, and I have seen, when the great soul of the world turned over with a heavy sigh, a perfectly new, extra-stout foresail vanish like a bit of some airy stuff much lighter than gossamer. Then was the time for the tall spars to stand fast in the great uproar. The machinery must do its work even if the soul of the world has gone mad.

===Chinese===
诚然，那不过是蚊蚋之力，而我曾亲见，当世界之魂沉重地叹息着翻了个身，一面崭新的、特厚的前帆便像一缕比蛛丝还轻的飘渺之物，倏然消失在天地之间。那才是高耸的桅桁在惊天喧嚣中岿然屹立的时刻。即便世界之魂已经癫狂，机器也必须完成它的使命。

===Original===
The modern steamship advances upon a still and overshadowed sea with a pulsating tremor of her frame, an occasional clang in her depths, as if she had an iron heart in her iron body; with a thudding rhythm in her progress and the regular beat of her propeller, heard afar in the night with an august and plodding sound as of the march of an inevitable future. But in a gale, the silent machinery of a sailing-ship would catch not only the power, but the wild and exulting voice of the world's soul. Whether she ran with her tall spars swinging, or breasted it with her tall spars lying over, there was always that wild song, deep like a chant, for a bass to the shrill pipe of the wind played on the sea-tops, with a punctuating crash, now and then, of a breaking wave. At times the weird effects of that invisible orchestra would get upon a man's nerves till he wished himself deaf.

===Chinese===
现代汽船行驶在静谧而阴翳的海面上，船体微微震颤，深处偶发铿然之声，仿佛铁铸的躯壳里跳动着一颗铁打的心脏；它行进的节奏沉闷而有规律，螺旋桨的拍击声在夜里远远传来，庄严而迟缓，如同一种不可阻挡的未来在行进。但在风暴中，帆船那无声的机械所捕获的，不仅仅是力量，更是世界之魂那狂野而欢腾的歌喉。无论她高桅摇荡着飞驰，还是高桅倾倒着迎风搏击，那狂野的歌声始终如一——深沉如诵经，为风在浪尖上奏出的尖利笛音充当低音，间或以碎浪的轰然巨响为节拍。有时，那支隐形管弦乐队的诡异效果会侵入人的神经，令人恨不得自己是个聋子。

===Original===
And this recollection of a personal wish, experienced upon several oceans, where the soul of the world has plenty of room to turn over with a mighty sigh, brings me to the remark that in order to take a proper care of a ship's spars it is just as well for a seaman to have nothing the matter with his ears. Such is the intimacy with which a seaman had to live with his ship of yesterday that his senses were like her senses, that the stress upon his body made him judge of the strain upon the ship's masts.

===Chinese===
这段在几大洋上——世界之魂有足够空间施展其雄浑叹息的地方——所生出的私人愿望的回忆，引出了我的一个看法：要想妥善照料一艘船的桅桁，水手最好别让耳朵出毛病。昨日的水手与他的船之间的亲密关系便是如此：他的感官如同船的感官，他身上所承受的压力使他能判断船桅所承受的张力。

===Original===
I had been some time at sea before I became aware of the fact that hearing plays a perceptible part in gauging the force of the wind. It was at night. The ship was one of those iron wool-clippers that the Clyde had floated out in swarms upon the world during the seventh decade of the last century. It was a fine period in shipbuilding, and also, I might say, a period of over-masting. The spars rigged up on the narrow hulls were indeed tall then, and the ship of which I think, with her coloured-glass skylight ends bearing the motto, "Let Glasgow Flourish," was certainly one of the most heavily-sparred specimens. She was built for hard driving, and unquestionably she got all the driving she could stand.

===Chinese===
我出海已有相当一段时日，才意识到听觉在估测风力时所起的显著作用。那是一个夜晚。那条船是克莱德河（the Clyde）在上世纪七十年代成群结队地漂向世界的铁质运羊毛快帆船（iron wool-clipper）之一。那是一个造船的黄金时代，同时也可以说是一个桅杆过剩的时代。架在狭窄船体上的桅桁确实高得惊人，而我此刻想起的那条船，其彩色玻璃天窗端部镌刻着格言"愿格拉斯哥繁荣"（Let Glasgow Flourish），无疑是桅杆最为繁密的标本之一。她是为苦干而生的，而她也确实吃尽了一切她能承受的苦干。

===Original===
Our captain was a man famous for the quick passages he had been used to make in the old *Tweed*, a ship famous the world over for her speed. The *Tweed* had been a wooden vessel, and he brought the tradition of quick passages with him into the iron clipper. I was the junior in her, a third mate, keeping watch with the chief officer; and it was just during one of the night watches in a strong, freshening breeze that I overheard two men in a sheltered nook of the main deck exchanging these informing remarks. Said one:

===Chinese===
我们的船长以在老特威德号（Tweed）上创造快捷航速而闻名——那是一条全世界都因其速度而闻名的船。特威德号是一条木质船，而他把快捷航速的传统带进了这条铁质快帆船。我是船上的末等官——三副，与大副一起值更；就在一个风力渐强的夜间值更中，我在主甲板一个避风的角落里无意中听到了两个水手交换的如下高论。一个说：

===Original===
"Should think 'twas time some of them light sails were coming off her."

===Chinese===
"我看是时候把那些轻帆收几面下来了。"

===Original===
And the other, an older man, uttered grumpily:

===Chinese===
另一个年纪大些的，没好气地说：

===Original===
"No fear! not while the chief mate's on deck. He's that deaf he can't tell how much wind there is."

===Chinese===
"想都别想！只要大副还在甲板上。他聋得根本不知道风有多大。"

===Original===
And, indeed, poor P——, quite young, and a smart seaman, was very hard of hearing. At the same time, he had the name of being the very devil of a fellow for carrying on sail on a ship. He was wonderfully clever at concealing his deafness, and, as to carrying on heavily, though he was a fearless man, I don't think that he ever meant to take undue risks. I can never forget his naive sort of astonishment when remonstrated with for what appeared a most daredevil performance. The only person, of course, that could remonstrate with telling effect was our captain, himself a man of daredevil tradition; and really, for me, who knew under whom I was serving, those were impressive scenes. Captain S—— had a great name for sailor-like qualities—the sort of name that compelled my youthful admiration. To this day I preserve his memory, for, indeed, it was he in a sense who completed my training. It was often a stormy process, but let that pass. I am sure he meant well, and I am certain that never, not even at the time, could I bear him malice for his extraordinary gift of incisive criticism. And to hear *him* make a fuss about too much sail on the ship seemed one of those incredible experiences that take place only in one's dreams.

===Chinese===
确实，可怜的P先生（Mr. P——）年纪虽轻，却是个出色的水手，只是耳朵很背。与此同时，他又以猛撑帆的"亡命徒"名声著称。他隐藏耳聋的本事令人叹服，而关于猛撑帆——虽然他确实是个无所畏惧的人——我倒不认为他有意冒不必要的风险。我永远忘不了他那种天真的惊愕：有人对他那看似玩命的行径提出抗议时，他竟一脸莫名其妙。当然，唯一能有效劝阻他的人便是我们的船长（Captain S——）——他自己也是一个有着亡命徒传统的人；而对我来说，知道自己在谁手下当差，那些场面实在令人刻骨铭心。S船长以水手本色著称——那是一种令我年轻时肃然起敬的名声。时至今日我仍铭记着他，因为在某种意义上，正是他完成了我的训练。那常常是一个暴风骤雨般的过程，但暂且不提。我相信他本意是好的，我也确信，即便在当时，我也从未因他那令人叹服的犀利批评天赋而对他怀恨在心。而听到*他*因为船上帆挂得太多而大动肝火，简直就像是只在梦中才会发生的不可思议的经历。

===Original===
It generally happened in this way: Night clouds racing overhead, wind howling, royals set, and the ship rushing on in the dark, an immense white sheet of foam level with the lee rail. Mr. P——, in charge of the deck, hooked on to the windward mizzen rigging in a state of perfect serenity; myself, the third mate, also hooked on somewhere to windward of the slanting poop, in a state of the utmost preparedness to jump at the very first hint of some sort of order, but otherwise in a perfectly acquiescent state of mind. Suddenly, out of the companion would appear a tall, dark figure, bareheaded, with a short white beard of a perpendicular cut, very visible in the dark—Captain S——, disturbed in his reading down below by the frightful bounding and lurching of the ship. Leaning very much against the precipitous incline of the deck, he would take a turn or two, perfectly silent, hang on by the compass for a while, take another couple of turns, and suddenly burst out:

===Chinese===
事情通常是这样的：夜云在头顶疾驰，风在号叫，皇桅帆（royal）已经挂好，船在黑暗中向前猛冲，一大片白花花的浪沫与下风舷齐平。P大副（Mr. P——）负责甲板，神态自若地钩在上风舷后桅索具上；我，三副，也钩在倾斜的艉楼甲板上风的某个位置，随时准备一听到某种命令的暗示便一跃而起，除此之外则心如止水。突然，升降口里闪出一个高大黝黑的身影，没戴帽子，留着一绺垂直修剪的白色短须，在黑暗中清晰可辨——正是S船长，被船可怕的颠簸和摇晃从下面的读书中惊扰了。他斜倚在陡峭倾斜的甲板上，默默无声地踱了一两圈，抓住罗盘柜待一会儿，又踱了两圈，然后突然爆发：

===Original===
"What are you trying to do with the ship?"

===Chinese===
"你到底想把这条船怎么样？"

===Original===
And Mr. P——, who was not good at catching what was shouted in the wind, would say interrogatively:

===Chinese===
P大副——他不擅长在风中听清喊话——会试探性地问：

===Original===
"Yes, sir?"

===Chinese===
"什么事，先生？"

===Original===
Then in the increasing gale of the sea there would be a little private ship's storm going on in which you could detect strong language, pronounced in a tone of passion and exculpatory protestations uttered with every possible inflection of injured innocence.

===Chinese===
于是，在越来越猛烈的海风中，一场属于这条船的私人风暴便上演了，你能捕捉到暴烈的言辞，以激昂的语调喷出，伴随着无辜受伤的千般腔调的辩白。

===Original===
"By Heavens, Mr. P-! I used to carry on sail in my time, but—"

===Chinese===
"天哪，P先生！我当年也是猛撑帆的主儿，可——"

===Original===
And the rest would be lost to me in a stormy gust of wind.

===Chinese===
剩下的话便淹没在一阵暴风骤雨中了。

===Original===
Then, in a lull, P——'s protesting innocence would become audible:

===Chinese===
然后，在一个间隙里，P——那委屈的申辩变得可闻：

===Original===
"She seems to stand it very well."

===Chinese===
"她似乎扛得很好。"

===Original===
And then another burst of an indignant voice:

===Chinese===
接着又是一声愤怒的爆发：

===Original===
"Any fool can carry sail on a ship—"

===Chinese===
"随便哪个傻瓜都能往船上挂帆——"

===Original===
And so on and so on, the ship meanwhile rushing on her way with a heavier list, a noisier splutter, a more threatening hiss of the white, almost blinding, sheet of foam to leeward. For the best of it was that Captain S—— seemed constitutionally incapable of giving his officers a definite order to shorten sail; and so that extraordinarily vague row would go on till at last it dawned upon them both, in some particularly alarming gust, that it was time to do something. There is nothing like the fearful inclination of your tall spars overloaded with canvas to bring a deaf man and an angry one to their senses.

===Chinese===
就这样你来我往，与此同时船以更大的倾斜、更喧闹的飞沫、下风舷那白得几乎刺目的泡沫之帘发出更具威胁性的嘶嘶声向前疾驰。妙就妙在S船长似乎天生就没有给手下下达明确减帆命令的本事；于是那场格外含混不清的争吵便一直持续下去，直到某个特别令人惊骇的阵风终于让他们二位都醒悟过来：该做点什么了。要说有什么能一下子让一个聋子和一个怒汉清醒过来，没有什么比超负荷的高桅在帆重下那可怕的倾斜更有效的了。

### XII / 第十二篇

===Original===
So sail did get shortened more or less in time even in that ship, and her tall spars never went overboard while I served in her. However, all the time I was with them, Captain S— and Mr. P— did not get on very well together. If P— carried on "like the very devil" because he was too deaf to know how much wind there was, Captain S—(who, as I have said, seemed constitutionally incapable of ordering one of his officers to shorten sail) resented the necessity forced upon him by Mr. P—'s desperate goings on. It was in Captain S—'s tradition rather to reprove his officers for not carrying on quite enough—in his phrase "for not taking every ounce of advantage of a fair wind." But there was also a psychological motive that made him extremely difficult to deal with on board that iron clipper.

===Chinese===
于是，在那条船上，收帆的时机大体上还是把控住了，高耸的帆桁在我服役期间也没落过水。然而，我与他们共事的那段时间里，S 船长（Captain S—）与 P 大副（Mr. P—）相处得并不融洽。P 大副因为耳聋太厉害，听不出风力有多大，便"像魔鬼似的"猛撑着帆；而 S 船长——正如我所说的，他似乎天生就不愿命令手下的军官收帆——对 P 大副那种不要命的操船方式强加给他的无奈十分反感。在 S 船长的传统里，他更习惯责备军官们没有把帆撑够——用他的话说，就是"没有把顺风的每一盎司好处都榨干"。但还有一个心理层面的原因，使他在那条铁质快帆船上极难相处。

===Original===
He had just come out of the marvellous *Tweed*, a ship, I have heard, heavy to look at but of phenomenal speed. In the middle sixties she had beaten by a day and a half the steam mail-boat from Hong Kong to Singapore. There was something peculiarly lucky, perhaps, in the placing of her masts—who knows? Officers of men-of-war used to come on board to take the exact dimensions of her sail-plan. Perhaps there had been a touch of genius or the finger of good fortune in the fashioning of her lines at bow and stern. It is impossible to say. She was built in the East Indies somewhere, of teakwood throughout, except the deck. She had a great sheer, high bows, and a clumsy stern.

===Chinese===
他刚从那条了不起的特威德号（Tweed）上下来。我听说那条船看上去笨重，速度却惊人。六十年代中期，她从香港到新加坡的航程比蒸汽邮轮还快了一天半。也许桅杆的布局上有什么特别的玄机——谁知道呢？军舰上的军官们常登船测量她帆装的精确尺寸。也许船首船尾的线条造型里，有那么一点天才的灵光或好运的造化。这谁也说不准。她是在东印度群岛某地建造的，除甲板外，整条船都是柚木的。她有着极大的舷弧，高耸的船首，和一个笨拙的船尾。

===Original===
The men who had seen her described her to me as "nothing much to look at." But in the great Indian famine of the seventies that ship, already old then, made some wonderful dashes across the Gulf of Bengal with cargoes of rice from Rangoon to Madras. She took the secret of her speed with her, and, unsightly as she was, her image surely has its glorious place in the mirror of the old sea.

===Chinese===
见过她的人对我说，她"没什么好看的"。但在七十年代那场印度大饥荒中，这条已经老迈的船，从仰光（Rangoon）运载大米到马德拉斯（Madras），在孟加拉湾（the Bay of Bengal）上完成了一系列令人惊叹的冲刺。她把速度的秘密随身带走了，尽管其貌不扬，但在旧日大海的镜子里，她的形象必定有其荣耀的一席之地。

===Original===
The point, however, is that Captain S—, who used to say frequently, "She never made a decent passage after I left her," seemed to think that the secret of her speed lay in her famous commander. No doubt the secret of many a ship's excellence does lie with the man on board, but it was hopeless for Captain S— to try to make his new iron clipper equal the feats which made the old *Tweed* a name of praise upon the lips of English-speaking seamen. There was something pathetic in it, as in the endeavour of an artist in his old age to equal the masterpieces of his youth—for the *Tweed*'s famous passages were Captain S—'s masterpieces. It was pathetic, and perhaps just the least bit dangerous.

===Chinese===
然而关键在于，S 船长常挂在嘴边的话是"自从我离开她之后，她再没跑出过一次像样的航程"——他似乎认为那条船的速度秘诀全在她那位著名的船长身上。毫无疑问，许多船的卓越确实系于船上的人，但 S 船长想让他的新铁质快帆船重现旧日特威德号在讲英语的水手们口中传为佳话的壮举，那是无望的。这里面有某种令人心酸的东西，就像一位老年的艺术家试图重现年轻时的杰作——因为特威德号那些著名的航程，正是 S 船长的杰作。这令人心酸，也许还带着一丝极其微末的危险。

===Original===
At any rate, I am glad that, what between Captain S—'s yearning for old triumphs and Mr. P—'s deafness, I have seen some memorable carrying on to make a passage. And I have carried on myself upon the tall spars of that Clyde shipbuilder's masterpiece as I have never carried on in a ship before or since.

===Chinese===
无论如何，我很庆幸，在 S 船长对昔日荣光的渴望与 P 大副的耳聋之间，我目睹了一些令人难忘的猛撑硬闯以赶航程的场面。而我自己，也在那条克莱德船厂匠人杰作的高耸帆桁上，从未有过后来或从前那般拼命地撑过帆。

===Original===
The second mate falling ill during the passage, I was promoted to officer of the watch, alone in charge of the deck. Thus the immense leverage of the ship's tall masts became a matter very near my own heart. I suppose it was something of a compliment for a young fellow to be trusted, apparently without any supervision, by such a commander as Captain S—; though as far as I can remember, neither the tone, nor the manner, nor yet the drift of Captain S—'s remarks addressed to myself did ever, by the most strained interpretation, imply a favourable opinion of my abilities. And he was, I must say, a most uncomfortable commander to get your orders from at night.

===Chinese===
航程中二副病了，我被提拔为值更军官，独自掌管甲板。于是，那条船高耸桅杆所蕴含的巨大杠杆力，便成了一桩与我切身相关的事。我想，像 S 船长这样的人物竟然信任一个年轻人，似乎完全不加任何监督，这多少算是一种褒奖吧。尽管据我记忆所及，无论 S 船长对我说话的语气、态度还是内容，哪怕用最牵强的解读方式，也从未暗示过他对我的能力有什么好评价。而且，我得说，他是一位在夜间接令时最令人不安的长官。

===Original===
If I had the watch from eight till midnight, he would leave the deck about nine with the words, "Don't take any sail off her." Then, on the point of disappearing down the companionway, he would add curtly: "Don't carry anything away." I am glad to say that I never did; one night, however, I was caught, not quite prepared, by a sudden shift of wind.

===Chinese===
如果我的值更时间是从八点到午夜，他大约九点就会离开甲板，丢下一句"别收她的帆"。然后，在即将消失在升降口之前，他会简短地补上一句："别把什么东西撑飞了。"我很高兴地说，我从未出过事；不过有一个夜晚，一阵突如其来的风向突变，让我措手不及。

===Original===
There was, of course, a good deal of noise—running about, the shouts of the sailors, the thrashing of the sails—enough, in fact, to wake the dead. But S— never came on deck. When I was relieved by the chief mate an hour afterwards, he sent for me. I went into his stateroom; he was lying on his couch wrapped up in a rug, with a pillow under his head.

===Chinese===
当然，那动静可不小——跑动声、水手们的喊叫声、帆的拍击声——事实上，那声响大得足以惊醒死人。但 S 船长始终没有上甲板。一小时后，大副来换我的班，船长派人叫我。我走进他的特等舱；他正裹着一条毯子躺在躺椅上，头下垫着一个枕头。

===Original===
"What was the matter with you up there just now?" he asked.
"Wind flew round on the lee quarter, sir," I said.
"Couldn't you see the shift coming?"
"Yes, sir, I thought it wasn't very far off."
"Why didn't you have your courses hauled up at once, then?" he asked in a tone that ought to have made my blood run cold.

===Chinese===
"刚才上面怎么回事？"他问道。
"风向转到了下风舷，先生，"我说。
"你没看出来要转吗？"
"看出来了，先生，我觉得还有一阵子才到。"
"那你为什么不立刻把主下帆收起来？"他问，那语气本该让我血液冰冷。

===Original===
But this was my chance, and I did not let it slip.
"Well, sir," I said in an apologetic tone, "she was going eleven knots very nicely, and I thought she would do for another half-hour or so."
He gazed at me darkly out of his head, lying very still on the white pillow, for a time.
"Ah, yes, another half-hour. That's the way ships get dismasted."
And that was all I got in the way of a wigging. I waited a little while and then went out, shutting carefully the door of the stateroom after me.

===Chinese===
但这正是我的机会，我不能放过它。
"是这样的，先生，"我用一种歉疚的口吻说，"她跑着十一节，跑得很好，我以为还能再撑个半小时左右。"
他阴沉沉地盯着我，一动不动地躺在白色的枕头上，沉默了好一会儿。
"啊，对，再撑半小时。船就是这样被弄断桅杆的。"
这就是我得到的全部训斥。我等了一会儿，然后走出去，小心地关上了特等舱的门。

===Original===
Well, I have loved, lived with, and left the sea without ever seeing a ship's tall fabric of sticks, cobwebs and gossamer go by the board. Sheer good luck, no doubt. But as to poor P—, I am sure that he would not have got off scot-free like this but for the god of gales, who called him away early from this earth, which is three parts ocean, and therefore a fit abode for sailors. A few years afterwards I met in an Indian port a man who had served in the ships of the same company. Names came up in our talk, names of our colleagues in the same employ,, and, naturally enough, I asked after P—. Had he got a command yet? And the other man answered carelessly:

===Chinese===
说来也怪，我爱过大海，与大海一起生活过，最后离开了大海，却从未亲眼目睹过一艘船那由桅杆、蛛网和轻纱构成的高耸结构轰然倒塌入海。这纯粹是运气好，毫无疑问。但至于可怜的 P 大副，我确信，若非那位飓风之神早早地将他从这个三分之二都是海洋因而适合水手栖居的地球上召走，他绝不会像这样全身而退。几年后，我在一个印度港口遇到了一个曾在同一家公司船上服役的人。谈话中提到了一些名字，都是我们曾经的同事，于是我很自然地问起了 P 大副。他当上船长了吗？那人漫不经心地回答道：

===Original===
"No; but he's provided for, anyhow. A heavy sea took him off the poop in the run between New Zealand and the Horn."
Thus P— passed away from amongst the tall spars of ships that he had tried to their utmost in many a spell of boisterous weather. He had shown me what carrying on meant, but he was not a man to learn discretion from. He could not help his deafness. One can only remember his cheery temper, his admiration for the jokes in *Punch*, his little oddities—like his strange passion for borrowing looking-glasses, for instance. Each of our cabins had its own looking-glass screwed to the bulkhead, and what he wanted with more of them we never could fathom. He asked for the loan in confidential tones. Why? Mystery. We made various surmises. No one will ever know now. At any rate, it was a harmless eccentricity, and may the god of gales, who took him away so abruptly between New Zealand and the Horn, let his soul rest in some Paradise of true seamen, where no amount of carrying on will ever dismast a ship!

===Chinese===
"没有；不过他好歹有了着落。从新西兰到合恩角（Cape Horn）的航程中，一排大浪把他从艉楼上卷走了。"
就这样，P 大副从那些他曾竭尽全力拼搏过的高耸帆桁间消逝了。他曾向我展示过猛撑硬闯的含义，但他并非一个能让人学到谨慎的人。他耳聋也是无奈。人们只能记住他那爽朗的脾气、他对《笨拙》杂志（Punch）笑话的欣赏、他那些小小的怪癖——比如他奇怪地热衷于借镜子。我们每个人的舱室舱壁上都用螺丝固定着一面镜子，而他为何需要更多面镜子，我们始终不得其解。他总是用一种神秘兮兮的口吻借镜子。为什么？这是个谜。我们做了各种猜测。如今再没人能知道了。无论如何，这不过是一种无害的怪癖。愿那位飓风之神——他在新西兰和合恩角之间如此突然地带走了 P 大副——让他的灵魂安息在某个真正水手的天堂里，在那里，再怎么猛撑硬闯也不会弄断桅杆！

## The Weight of the Burden / 重负之重

### XIII / 第十三篇

===Original===
There has been a time when a ship’s chief mate, pocketbook in hand and pencil behind his ear, kept one eye aloft upon his riggers and the other down the hatchway on the stevedores, and watched the disposition of his ship’s cargo, knowing that even before she started he was already doing his best to secure for her an easy and quick passage.

The hurry of the times, the loading and discharging organization of the docks, the use of hoisting machinery which works quickly and will not wait, the cry for prompt despatch, the very size of his ship, stand nowadays between the modern seaman and the thorough knowledge of his craft.

There are profitable ships and unprofitable ships. The profitable ship will carry a large load through all the hazards of the weather, and, when at rest, will stand up in dock and shift from berth to berth without ballast. There is a point of perfection in a ship as a worker when she is spoken of as being able to *sail* without ballast. I have never met that sort of paragon myself, but I have seen these paragons advertised amongst ships for sale. Such excess of virtue and good-nature on the part of a ship always provoked my mistrust. It is open to any man to say that his ship will sail without ballast; and he will say it, too, with every mark of profound conviction, especially if he is not going to sail in her himself. The risk of advertising her as able to sail without ballast is not great, since the statement does not imply a warranty of her arriving anywhere. Moreover, it is strictly true that most ships will sail without ballast for some little time before they turn turtle upon the crew.

===Chinese===
曾经有一个时代，船上的大副手执记事本，铅笔别在耳后，一只眼睛盯着桅杆上的索具工，另一只眼睛透过舱口注视着装卸工，观察着船上货物的配置，因为他知道，即便在启航之前，他已竭尽全力为船舶争取一次轻松而快捷的航程。

时代的匆忙、码头装卸的组织化、起重机械的运用（它运转迅速，不会等待）、对快速调度的呼唤、乃至船舶本身的巨大尺寸，如今都横亘在现代水手与对他这门手艺的透彻了解之间。

有利可图的船和无利可图的船。有利可图的船能满载货物穿越所有天气风险，停泊时能在码头稳稳站立，无需压舱物便能从一个泊位转移到另一个泊位。当一艘船被称为能够*空载航行*时，它作为工作船舶便达到了完美的境界。我自己从未遇到过这种完美典范，但我在待售船舶的广告中见过这类完美之选。这种船舶过度的美德与温厚总是激起我的怀疑。任何人都可以夸口说他的船能空载航行；而且他会说得斩钉截铁，尤其当他自己不打算乘这艘船出航时。宣称船舶能空载航行的风险并不大，因为这并不保证它能抵达任何地方。况且，严格来说，大多数船舶在把船员掀翻之前，都能空载航行一小段时间。

===Original===
A shipowner loves a profitable ship; the seaman is proud of her; a doubt of her good looks seldom exists in his mind; but if he can boast of her more useful qualities it is an added satisfaction for his self-love.

The loading of ships was once a matter of skill, judgment, and knowledge. Thick books have been written about it. *Stevens on Stowage* is a portly volume with the renown and weight (in its own world) of *Coke on Littleton*. Stevens is an agreeable writer, and, as is the case with men of talent, his gifts adorn his sterling soundness. He gives you the official teaching on the whole subject, is precise as to rules, mentions illustrative events, quotes law cases where verdicts turned upon a point of stowage. He is never pedantic, and, for all his close adherence to broad principles, he is ready to admit that no two ships can be treated exactly alike.

Stevedoring, which had been a skilled labour, is fast becoming a labour without the skill. The modern steamship with her many holds is not loaded within the sailor-like meaning of the word. She is filled up. Her cargo is not stowed in any sense; it is simply dumped into her through six hatchways, more or less, by twelve winches or so, with clatter and hurry and racket and heat, in a cloud of steam and a mess of coal-dust. As long as you keep her propeller under water and take care, say, not to fling down barrels of oil on top of bales of silk, or deposit an iron bridge-girder of five ton or so upon a bed of coffee-bags, you have done about all in the way of duty that the cry for prompt despatch will allow you to do.

===Chinese===
船东喜爱有利可图的船；水手为她自豪；他心中很少怀疑她的美貌；但若能夸耀她更实用的品质，那便是对自尊心的额外满足。

装载船舶曾经是一门需要技能、判断力和知识的行当。为此写过厚厚的专著。《斯蒂文斯论积载》（*Stevens on Stowage*）是一部厚重的大部头，在其专业领域内享有与《科克论利特尔顿》（*Coke on Littleton*）同等的声誉和分量。斯蒂文斯是个令人愉悦的作家，和许多才子一样，他的天赋为其扎实的功底增色。他向你传授这一课题的官方教义，对规则一丝不苟，列举说明性事件，引用那些判决取决于积载要点的法律案件。他从不学究气十足，尽管严格遵循广泛的原则，他也随时准备承认没有两艘船能被一视同仁地对待。

曾经是技术性劳动的装卸工作，正迅速变成无需技术的劳动。现代蒸汽船拥有众多货舱，其装载方式已不具有水手所理解的"装载"含义。它只是被填满。它的货物谈不上任何意义上的积载；不过是通过大约六个舱口、由大约十二台绞车倾倒进去，伴随着叮当声、匆忙、喧嚣和热浪，蒸汽弥漫，煤灰四散。只要你让螺旋桨没入水中，并且注意——比如说——不要把油桶扔到丝绸包上，也不要把五吨左右的铁桥大梁压在咖啡袋上，那么，对于那"快速调度"的呼唤所允许你履行的职责而言，你便已做到了一切。

## XIV / 第十四篇

===Original===
The sailing-ship, when I knew her in her days of perfection, was a sensible creature. When I say her days of perfection, I mean perfection of build, gear, seaworthy qualities and ease of handling, not the perfection of speed. That quality has departed with the change of building material. No iron ship of yesterday ever attained the marvels of speed which the seamanship of men famous in their time had obtained from their wooden, copper-sheeted predecessors. Everything had been done to make the iron ship perfect, but no wit of man had managed to devise an efficient coating composition to keep her bottom clean with the smooth cleanness of yellow metal sheeting. After a spell of a few weeks at sea, an iron ship begins to lag as if she had grown tired too soon. It is only her bottom that is getting foul. A very little affects the speed of an iron ship which is not driven on by a merciless propeller. Often it is impossible to tell what inconsiderate trifle puts her off her stride. A certain mysteriousness hangs around the quality of speed as it was displayed by the old sailing-ships commanded by a competent seaman. In those days the speed depended upon the seaman; therefore, apart from the laws, rules, and regulations for the good preservation of his cargo, he was careful of his loading——or what is technically called the trim of his ship. Some ships sailed fast on an even keel, others had to be trimmed quite one foot by the stern, and I have heard of a ship that gave her best speed on a wind when so loaded as to float a couple of inches by the head.

===Chinese===
我所认识的帆船，在她全盛时代，是一种通情达理的生灵。我说她的全盛时代，是指建造工艺、索具装备、适航性能与操驾便捷都臻于完美的时代，而非速度的完美。那种品质随造船材料的更迭而消逝了。昨日的铁壳船，从未达到那个时代以精湛船艺闻名的水手们从覆铜木帆船上榨取的惊人航速。一切手段都用上了，为的是让铁壳船趋于完美，然而人类的才智始终未能发明出一种有效的涂层配方，能让船底保持如铜皮包裹那般光洁。在海上漂泊几个星期后，铁壳船便开始迟滞，仿佛过早地疲倦了。不过是她的船底变脏了而已。对于一艘不靠无情螺旋桨驱动的铁壳船，微乎其微的污损便足以影响航速。究竟是哪桩不经意的微末小事打乱了她的节奏，往往无从断定。由称职的水手统率的老式帆船所展现出的那种速度品质，始终笼罩着一层神秘色彩。在那个年代，航速取决于水手；因此，除了那些旨在妥善保管货物的法规条例之外，水手还十分在意装船——即航海术语所说的"配载"。有些船平吃水便跑得快，另一些则必须尾倾整整一英尺，我还听说过一条船，当货物装到船首吃水深出几英寸时，反而在戗风中达到了最佳航速。

===Original===
I call to mind a winter landscape in Amsterdam——a flat foreground of waste land, with here and there stacks of timber, like the huts of a camp of some very miserable tribe; the long stretch of the Handelskade; cold, stone-faced quays, with the snow-sprinkled ground and the hard, frozen water of the canal, in which were set ships one behind another with their frosty mooring-ropes hanging slack and their decks idle and deserted, because, as the master stevedore (a gentle, pale person, with a few golden hairs on his chin and a reddened nose) informed me, their cargoes were frozen-in upcountry on barges and schuyts. In the distance, beyond the waste ground, and running parallel with the line of ships, a line of brown, warm-toned houses seemed bowed under snow-laden roofs. From afar at the end of Tsar Peter Straat, issued in the frosty air the tinkle of bells of the horse tramcars, appearing and disappearing in the opening between the buildings, like little toy carriages harnessed with toy horses and played with by people that appeared no bigger than children.

===Chinese===
我忆起阿姆斯特丹的一个冬日景象——一片平坦的荒地前景，四处散落着木材堆，宛如某个极贫困部落营地的棚屋；汉德尔斯卡德（Handelskade）长长的堤岸伸展出去；冰冷的石砌码头上，地面覆着薄雪，运河的水面坚硬如冻，一条条船首尾相接地停泊其间，覆霜的系泊缆松弛地垂悬，甲板空寂无人——正如那位工头（一个性情温和、面色苍白的人，下巴上几根金色胡须，鼻子冻得通红）告诉我的，货物在上游的驳船和平底船上冻住了。远处，越过荒地，与船队平行排列着一排棕色调的暖色房屋，在积雪压覆的屋顶下显得低垂。从沙皇彼得街（Tsar Peter Straat）尽头远处，马车铃铛的清响在寒冷空气中传来，马车在建筑间的开口处时隐时现，仿佛套着玩具马的小玩具车，被看上去不过孩童般大小的人们驾驭着。

===Original===
I was, as the French say, biting my fists with impatience for that cargo frozen upcountry; with rage at that canal set fast, at the wintry and deserted aspect of all those ships that seemed to decay in grim depression for want of the open water. I was chief mate, and very much alone. Directly I had joined I received from my owners instructions to send all the ship's apprentices away on leave together, because in such weather there was nothing for anybody to do, unless to keep up a fire in the cabin stove. That was attended to by a snuffy and mop-headed, inconceivably dirty, and weirdly toothless Dutch ship-keeper, who could hardly speak three words of English, but who must have had some considerable knowledge of the language, since he managed invariably to interpret in the contrary sense everything that was said to him.

===Chinese===
我正像法国人所说的那样，咬着拳头干着急，为那些冻在上游的货物而焦躁；对那条冻封的运河满腔怒火，对所有那些船萧索凄寒、了无生气的冬日景象满腔怒火——它们仿佛因失去了开阔水面而颓然衰朽，一片死寂。我是大副，而且孤身一人。我一上任，便接到船东的指令，把所有见习水手一律放假遣走，因为这种天气谁也无事可做，除非在船长室的炉子里维持一炉火。这炉火由一个邋遢的荷兰看船人照管——他满头乱发，鼻烟渍斑斑，脏得不可理喻，牙齿奇异地掉光了，几乎说不出三个英文词来，但想必对这门语言有相当的了解，因为他说什么都会被理解成相反的意思。

===Original===
Notwithstanding the little iron stove, the ink froze on the swing-table in the cabin, and I found it more convenient to go ashore stumbling over the arctic wasteland and shivering in glazed tramcars in order to write my evening letter to my owners in a gorgeous café in the centre of the town. It was an immense place, lofty and gilt, upholstered in red plush, full of electric lights and so thoroughly warmed that even the marble tables felt tepid to the touch. The waiter who brought me my cup of coffee bore, by comparison with my utter isolation, the dear aspect of an intimate friend. There, alone in a noisy crowd, I would write slowly a letter addressed to Glasgow, of which the gist would be: There is no cargo, and no prospect of any coming till late spring apparently. And all the time I sat there the necessity of getting back to the ship bore heavily on my already half-congealed spirits——the shivering in glazed tramcars, the stumbling over the snow-sprinkled waste ground, the visions of ships frozen in a row, appearing vaguely like corpses of black vessels in a white world, so silent, so lifeless, so soulless they seemed to be.

===Chinese===
尽管有那小小的铁皮炉子，舱室摇桌上的墨水还是冻住了。于是我发觉，与其在舱里挨冻，不如蹒跚走过北极般的荒原，瑟缩在有玻璃窗的电车里，去镇中心一家豪华咖啡馆给船东写晚间信件更方便些。那地方十分宏大，穹顶高耸，金碧辉煌，红色绒布铺陈，电灯通明，暖气烧得极足，连大理石桌面摸上去都是温热的。端来咖啡的侍者，比起我绝对的孤寂，简直宛如一位亲密友人的可亲面孔。在那嘈杂人群里独自坐着，我缓缓写下一封寄往格拉斯哥的信，大意是：没有货物，看来直到暮春也无望到货。而我坐在那里每一刻，返回船上的迫切感都沉沉压在我已经半冻僵的精神之上——有玻璃窗的电车里瑟瑟发抖，雪覆的荒地上跌跌撞撞，那一列列冻住的船只的幻影隐约浮现，如同白色世界里一具具黑色船舶的尸体，那般沉寂，那般了无生气，那般失魂落魄。

===Original===
With precaution I would go up the side of my own particular corpse, and would feel her as cold as ice itself and as slippery under my feet. My cold berth would swallow up like a chilly burial niche my bodily shivers and my mental excitement. It was a cruel winter. The very air seemed as hard and trenchant as steel; but it would have taken much more than this to extinguish my sacred fire for the exercise of my craft. No young man of twenty-four appointed chief mate for the first time in his life would have let that Dutch tenacious winter penetrate into his heart. I think that in those days I never forgot the fact of my elevation for five consecutive minutes. I fancy it kept me warm, even in my slumbers, better than the high pile of blankets, which positively crackled with frost as I threw them off in the morning. And I would get up early for no reason whatever except that I was in sole charge. The new captain had not been appointed yet.

===Chinese===
我会小心翼翼地攀上我那条专属尸骸的船舷，发觉她浑身冰冷如冰，脚下打滑。我那冰冷的铺位会像一处寒彻的墓龛，吞没我身体的颤抖与精神的亢奋。那是一个酷烈的冬天。空气本身都硬朗锋锐如钢铁；但要浇灭我那为操持船艺而燃烧的神圣之火，单凭这些还远远不够。一个二十四岁的年轻人，有生以来头一回被任命为大副，断不会让那荷兰式的绵绵寒意渗入他的心房。我想，在那些日子里，我从未连续五分钟忘记过自己晋升的事实。我猜想，它比那厚厚一摞毯子更能为我保暖，甚至在睡梦中也是如此——那些毯子早晨掀开时确确实实嘎吱作响，结满了霜花。而我总是没有任何理由便早早起身，唯一的缘由就是我独自当家。新船长尚未任命。

===Original===
Almost each morning a letter from my owners would arrive, directing me to go to the charterers and clamour for the ship's cargo; to threaten them with the heaviest penalties of demurrage; to demand that this assortment of varied merchandise, set fast in a landscape of ice and windmills somewhere upcountry, should be put on rail instantly, and fed up to the ship in regular quantities every day. After drinking some hot coffee, like an Arctic explorer setting off on a sledge journey towards the North Pole, I would go ashore and roll shivering in a tramcar into the very heart of the town, past clean-faced houses, past thousands of brass knockers upon a thousand painted doors glimmering behind rows of trees of the pavement species, leafless, gaunt, seemingly dead forever.

===Chinese===
几乎每天早上都会收到船东的来信，指示我去找租船方，为船货大声疾呼；以最严厉的滞期罚金威胁他们；要求那批冻在上游某处冰雪与风车之间的各色杂货立刻装上铁路，每天按量稳稳地送抵船上。喝下几杯热咖啡后，我便像一个准备乘雪橇前往北极的探险家，上岸钻进电车，瑟缩着一路摇晃到镇中心，经过一栋栋面容整洁的房屋，经过上千扇漆色门扉上数千个黄铜门环在行道树——那些光秃、枯瘦、仿佛永远死去了的行道树——间闪烁而过。

===Original===
That part of the expedition was easy enough, though the horses were painfully glistening with icicles, and the aspect of the tram-conductors' faces presented a repulsive blending of crimson and purple. But as to frightening or bullying, or even wheedling some sort of answer out of Mr. Hudig, that was another matter altogether. He was a big, swarthy Netherlander, with black moustaches and a bold glance. He always began by shoving me into a chair before I had time to open my mouth, gave me cordially a large cigar, and in excellent English would start to talk everlastingly about the phenomenal severity of the weather. It was impossible to threaten a man who, though he possessed the language perfectly, seemed incapable of understanding any phrase pronounced in a tone of remonstrance or discontent. As to quarrelling with him, it would have been stupid. The weather was too bitter for that. His office was so warm, his fire so bright, his sides shook so heartily with laughter, that I experienced always a great difficulty in making up my mind to reach for my hat.

===Chinese===
那一段远征还算轻松，尽管马匹身上挂满了亮晶晶的冰凌，令人心疼，电车售票员的脸色也呈现出一种令人厌恶的深红与绛紫的混合。但要吓唬、威逼，甚至哄骗胡迪格先生（Mr. Hudig）给出一个答复，那就是另一码事了。他是个高大黝黑的荷兰人，留着黑髭须，目光大胆。每次我还没来得及张嘴，他便一把将我摁进椅子里，热诚地递上一支大雪茄，然后用地道的英语滔滔不绝地谈论天气反常的严酷。你没法威胁一个英语说得分明、却偏偏对任何带抗议或不满语气的话充耳不闻的人。至于和他争吵，那未免太蠢了。天气已经够冷的了。他的办公室那样暖和，炉火那样明亮，他的身躯笑起来那样爽朗地抖动，我每次都很难下定决心伸手去拿帽子。

===Original===
At last the cargo did come. At first it came dribbling in by rail in trucks, till the thaw set in; and then fast, in a multitude of barges, with a great rush of unbound waters. The gentle master stevedore had his hands very full at last; and the chief mate became worried in his mind as to the proper distribution of the weight of his first cargo in a ship he did not personally know before.

===Chinese===
货物终于来了。起初只是零零星星由铁路货车运来，直到化冻开始；然后便是大潮般涌入的驳船队，伴随着奔涌的春潮浩浩荡荡地倾泻而至。那位温和的工头终于忙得不可开交；而大副则开始为如何在一条自己尚不熟悉的船上妥善分配这第一票货的重量而忧心忡忡。

===Original===
Ships do want humouring. They want humouring in handling; and if you mean to handle them well, they must have been humoured in the distribution of the weight which you ask them to carry through the good and evil fortune of a passage. Your ship is a tender creature, whose idiosyncrasies must be attended to if you mean her to come with credit to herself and you through the rough-and-tumble of her life.

===Chinese===
船确实需要哄着来。操驾上需要哄着；而若你想把她们操驾好，在分配你要求她们承载的重量——无论航途是吉是凶——时就必须哄着她们。你的船是一个娇嫩的生灵，若要让她在颠沛流离的一生中为你也为她自己赢得体面，就必须顾及她的种种脾性。

### XV / 第十五篇

===Original===
So seemed to think the new captain, who arrived the day after we had finished loading, on the very eve of the day of sailing. I first beheld him on the quay, a complete stranger to me, obviously not a Hollander, in a black bowler and a short drab overcoat, ridiculously out of tone with the winter aspect of the wastelands, bordered by the brown fronts of houses with their roofs dripping with melting snow.

This stranger was walking up and down absorbed in the marked contemplation of the ship's fore and aft trim; but when I saw him squat on his heels in the slush at the very edge of the quay to peer at the draught of water under her counter, I said to myself, "this is the captain." And presently I descried his luggage coming along—a real sailor's chest, carried by means of rope-beckets between two men, with a couple of leather portmanteaus and a roll of charts sheeted in canvas piled upon the lid. The sudden, spontaneous agility with which he bounded aboard right off the rail afforded me the first glimpse of his real character. Without further preliminaries than a friendly nod, he addressed me: "You have got her pretty well in her fore and aft trim. Now, what about your weights?"

===Chinese===
新船长似乎也是这么想的。我们装完货的次日——启航前夜——他到了。我第一眼看见他是在码头上，一个素不相识的人，显然不是荷兰人，头戴黑色圆顶硬礼帽，身穿一件短短的土黄色大衣，与这荒凉冬景极不相称：四周棕褐色的房屋墙面前，屋顶的残雪正滴滴答答地融化。

这陌生人踱来踱去，全神贯注地审视着船的前后吃水差；可当我看见他蹲在码头边缘的泥泞里，弯腰去看船尾水下的吃水线时，我心里断定："这就是船长。"不多时，我便望见他的行李运来了——一只真正的水手箱，两个人用绳套抬着，箱盖上堆着两皮箱和一卷用帆布裹着的海图。他翻身跳过栏杆上船，那身手的敏捷与自如，让我头一回窥见了他此人的真性情。除了一个友善的点头，没有任何寒暄，他便开口对我说："你把她的前后吃水调得不错。那么，你的载重分布怎样？"

===Original===
I told him I had managed to keep the weight sufficiently well up, as I thought, one-third of the whole being in the upper part "above the beams," as the technical expression has it. He whistled "Phew!" scrutinizing me from head to foot. A sort of smiling vexation was visible on his ruddy face.

"Well, we shall have a lively time of it this passage, I bet," he said.

He knew. It turned out he had been chief mate of her for the two preceding voyages; and I was already familiar with his handwriting in the old logbooks I had been perusing in my cabin with a natural curiosity, looking up the records of my new ship's luck, of her behaviour, of the good times she had had, and of the troubles she had escaped.

===Chinese===
我告诉他，我以为载重分布还算妥当，整船货物有三分之一装在"甲板以上"——行话是这么说的。他吹了声口哨——"嚯！"——上下打量着我。那张红润的脸上浮起一种似笑非笑的懊恼。

"好吧，我敢打赌，这趟航程够咱们受的，"他说。

他果然知情。原来此前两个航次，他都在这条船上做大副；而我在舱里翻阅那些旧航海日志时——出于一种自然的好奇，想查查新船的运势、她的脾性、她经历过的好时光和逃脱过的麻烦——早已认得他的笔迹了。

===Original===
He was right in his prophecy. On our passage from Amsterdam to Samarang with a general cargo, of which, alas! only one-third in weight was stowed "above the beams," we had a lively time of it. It was lively, but not joyful. There was not even a single moment of comfort in it, because no seaman can feel comfortable in body or mind when he has made his ship uneasy.

To travel along with a cranky ship for ninety days or so is no doubt a nerve-trying experience; but in this case what was wrong with our craft was this: that by my system of loading she had been made much too stable.

Neither before nor since have I felt a ship roll so abruptly, so violently, so heavily. Once she began, you felt that she would never stop, and this hopeless sensation, characterizing the motion of ships whose centre of gravity is brought down too low in loading, made everyone on board weary of keeping on his feet. I remember once overhearing one of the hands say: "By Heavens, Jack! I feel as if I didn't mind how soon I let myself go, and let the blamed hooker knock my brains out if she likes." The captain used to remark frequently: "Ah, yes; I dare say one-third weight above beams would have been quite enough for most ships. But then, you see, there's no two of them alike on the seas, and she's an uncommonly ticklish jade to load."

===Chinese===
他的预言丝毫不差。从阿姆斯特丹到三宝垄（Samarang），我们运载着一批杂货——唉！按重量算，只有三分之一装在"甲板以上"——这一路真是够热闹的。热闹是热闹，却毫无乐趣。连一刻舒坦都谈不上，因为水手一旦让船身不得安稳，身心便都不会自在。

跟一条横摇不止的船走上九十来天，无疑是件磨神经的事；但我们的船问题出在别处：照我的装货法，她吃水太稳了，稳过了头。

此前此后，我再没有体会过一条船摇得如此突兀、如此暴烈、如此沉重。一旦开摇起来，便仿佛永无止境。那种绝望的感觉——重心在装货时被压得太低的船所特有的摇荡方式——让船上所有人都懒得再站稳脚跟。我记得有一次无意中听见一个水手说："老天爷，杰克！我真巴不得干脆撒手不管了，让这该死的破船爱怎么撞怎么撞，把我的脑浆撞出来算了。"船长也常念叨："啊，是啊；我敢说对大多数船来说，甲板以上三分之一的配重已经绰绰有余了。可你瞧，这世上没有两条一模一样的船，而她偏偏是一匹极难伺候的犟马。"

===Original===
Down south, running before the gales of high latitudes, she made our life a burden to us. There were days when nothing would keep even on the swing-tables, when there was no position where you could fix yourself so as not to feel a constant strain upon all the muscles of your body. She rolled and rolled with an awful dislodging jerk and that dizzily fast sweep of her masts on every swing. It was a wonder that the men sent aloft were not flung off the yards, the yards not flung off the masts, the masts not flung overboard. The captain in his armchair, holding on grimly at the head of the table, with the soup-tureen rolling on one side of the cabin and the steward sprawling on the other, would observe, looking at me: "That's your one-third above the beams. The only thing that surprises me is that the sticks have stuck to her all this time."

Ultimately some of the minor spars did go—nothing important: spanker-booms and suchlike—because at times the frightful impetus of her rolling would part a fourfold tackle of new three-inch Manilla line as if it were weaker than packthread.

===Chinese===
往南走，在高纬度的大风中顺风行驶，她把我们的日子变成了煎熬。有些日子，摇桌上什么也搁不住，浑身上下找不到一个不酸痛的姿势。她摇啊摇，每一下都带着骇人的离心猛甩，桅杆在空中划出令人目眩的弧线。被派上桅杆的人没被从桁上甩下来，桁没被从桅上甩下来，桅没被从船上甩出去——简直是奇迹。船长坐在扶手椅里，死死抓住桌子尽头，汤盆在舱室一侧滚来滚去，侍者在另一侧趴着，他却看着我说："这就是你那甲板以上的三分之一。唯一让我吃惊的是，那些木杆到现在还连在船上。"

最终，一些小圆材还是断了——无关紧要的东西：后桅纵帆桁（spanker-boom）之类——因为那骇人的摇晃势头有时会把四股新马尼拉绳编成的索具崩断，就像扯断一根棉线那么容易。

===Original===
It was only poetic justice that the chief mate who had made a mistake—perhaps a half-excusable one—about the distribution of his ship's cargo should pay the penalty. A piece of one of the minor spars that did carry away flew against the chief mate's back, and sent him sliding on his face for quite a considerable distance along the main deck. Thereupon followed various and unpleasant consequences of a physical order—"queer symptoms," as the captain, who treated them, used to say; inexplicable periods of powerlessness, sudden accesses of mysterious pain; and the patient agreed fully with the regretful mutters of his very attentive captain wishing that it had been a straightforward broken leg. Even the Dutch doctor who took the case up in Samarang offered no scientific explanation. All he said was: "Ah, friend, you are young yet; it may be very serious for your whole life. You must leave your ship; you must quite silent be for three months—quite silent."

Of course, he meant the chief mate to keep quiet—to lay up, as a matter of fact. His manner was impressive enough, if his English was childishly imperfect when compared with the fluency of Mr. Hudig, the figure at the other end of that passage, and memorable enough in its way. In a great airy ward of a Far Eastern hospital, lying on my back, I had plenty of leisure to remember the dreadful cold and snow of Amsterdam, while looking at the fronds of the palm-trees tossing and rustling at the height of the window. I could remember the elated feeling and the soul-gripping cold of those tramway journeys taken into town to put what in diplomatic language is called pressure upon the good Hudig, with his warm fire, his armchair, his big cigar, and the never-failing suggestion in his good-natured voice: "I suppose in the end it is you they will appoint captain before the ship sails?" It may have been his extreme good-nature, the serious, unsmiling good-nature of a fat, swarthy man with coal-black moustache and steady eyes; but he might have been a bit of a diplomatist, too. His enticing suggestions I used to repel modestly by the assurance that it was extremely unlikely, as I had not enough experience. "You know very well how to go about business matters," he used to say, with a sort of affected moodiness clouding his serene round face. I wonder whether he ever laughed to himself after I had left the office. I dare say he never did, because I understand that diplomatists, in and out of the career, take themselves and their tricks with an exemplary seriousness.

===Chinese===
犯了错的大副——或许情有可原——理应为他对货物分配的失误付出代价，这不过是天道报应。一根断裂的小圆材碎片飞起来砸中大副的后背，让他在主甲板上脸朝下往前滑了好长一段。随后便是各种身体上的不适——船长，也就是给他看病的那个人，管这叫"怪毛病"；莫名其妙的无力期，突如其来的隐痛；病人完全同意他那位尽心尽责的船长的遗憾嘀咕——巴不得干脆痛快地断条腿算了。就连三宝垄接手治伤的荷兰医生也没给出什么科学解释，只是说："唉，朋友，你还年轻；这可能会困扰你一辈子。你必须离开船；必须静养三个月——完全静养。"

船长的本意其实是让大副安心静养、卧床休息。他的态度够严肃的，虽然比起通道另一头那位胡迪格（Hudig）先生的流利英语，他的英语简直像个孩子。但在远东一家医院宽敞通风的大病房里，我仰面躺着，有大把空闲回想阿姆斯特丹那可怕的严寒和积雪，同时望着窗外高处棕榈叶在风中摇曳沙沙作响。我想起了那种兴奋的感觉和刺骨的寒意——坐着电车进城去对那位好心的胡迪格施加外交辞令式的"压力"时，他总是坐在温暖的炉火旁，坐在扶手椅里，叼着大雪茄，用他那和善的嗓音暗示着一句永远不会落空的话："我猜到最后，船启航之前，他们任命的船长该是你吧？"也许是他那过分的和善——一个黝胖、黑胡子、眼神坚定的人那种严肃、不苟言笑的和善；但他或许也有几分外交手腕。他那些诱人的暗示，我总是谦逊地以经验不足为由婉拒，说当船长的可能性微乎其微。"你处理事务很在行嘛，"他常常这么说，圆脸上装出一副忧郁的神情。我有时想，我离开办公室后他会不会独自笑出声来。我敢说他从不如此，因为据我所知，无论职业外交官还是业余外交家，都对自己的把戏和本人抱着一种堪称典范的严肃态度。

===Original===
But he had nearly persuaded me that I was fit in every way to be trusted with a command. There came three months of mental worry, hard rolling, remorse, and physical pain to drive home the lesson of insufficient experience.

Yes, your ship wants to be humoured with knowledge. You must treat with an understanding consideration the mysteries of her feminine nature, and then she will stand by you faithfully in the unceasing struggle with forces wherein defeat is no shame. It is a serious relation, that in which a man stands to his ship. She has her rights as though she could breathe and speak; and, indeed, there are ships that, for the right man, will do anything but speak, as the saying goes.

A ship is not a slave. You must make her easy in a seaway, you must never forget that you owe her the fullest share of your thought, of your skill, of your self-love. If you remember that obligation, naturally and without effort, as if it were an instinctive feeling of your inner life, she will sail, stay, run for you as long as she is able, or, like a seabird going to rest upon the angry waves, she will lay out the heaviest gale that ever made you doubt living long enough to see another sunrise.

===Chinese===
可是他差点说服我，让我相信自己各方面都足以胜任指挥之职了。接下来是三个月的精神折磨、剧烈摇晃、悔恨和肉体痛苦——这正是经验不足所要付出的代价。

是啊，你的船需要你用知识去迁就。你必须体谅她那女性化的神秘天性，带着善解人意的体贴去对待她，如此她才会在与自然力量永无休止的搏斗中忠实陪伴你——在那里，败北并非耻辱。人与船之间的关系是严肃的。她拥有自己的权利，仿佛能呼吸、能说话；而事实上，俗话说得好，遇到对的人，有些船什么都能做，唯独不会开口。

船不是奴隶。你必须让她在风浪中安稳舒适，切记要把最充分的心思、技艺和自尊心分给她。若你将这份义务铭记在心，自然而然、毫不费力，如同你内心深处的本能感受，她便会为你航行、为你驻泊、为你驰骋，直到力竭为止；又如一只海鸟栖息在怒涛之上，她会替你挡住那场令你疑心再也见不到日出的狂风巨浪。

## Overdue and Missing / 逾期与失踪

### XVI / 第十六篇

===Original===
Often I turn with melancholy eagerness to the space reserved in the newspapers under the general heading of "Shipping Intelligence." I meet there the names of ships I have known. Every year some of these names disappear—the names of old friends. "*Tempi passati!*"

The different divisions of that kind of news are set down in their order, which varies but slightly in its arrangement of concise headlines. And first comes "Speakings"—reports of ships met and signalled at sea, name, port, where from, where bound for, so many days out, ending frequently with the words "All well." Then come "Wrecks and Casualties"—a longish array of paragraphs, unless the weather has been fair and clear, and friendly to ships all over the world.

On some days there appears the heading "Overdue"—an ominous threat of loss and sorrow trembling yet in the balance of fate. There is something sinister to a seaman in the very grouping of the letters which form this word, clear in its meaning, and seldom threatening in vain.

===Chinese===
我时常怀着忧郁的渴望，翻阅报纸上"航运消息"（Shipping Intelligence）栏目预留的版面。我在那里遇见我所熟悉的船名。每年，其中一些名字都会消失——那些老友的名字。"逝者如斯！"（*Tempi passati!*）

这类新闻的不同栏目按顺序排列，简洁的标题编排变化不大。首先是"相遇"（Speakings）——报告在海上相遇并发出信号的船舶：船名、港口、从何处来、驶往何处、出海多少天，常以"一切安好"结尾。然后是"海难与事故"（Wrecks and Casualties）——除非天气晴好，对全世界的船舶都友好，否则会有一长串简报。

有些日子会出现"逾期"（Overdue）的标题——一个不祥的威胁，意味着损失与悲伤仍在命运的天平上摇摆。对水手而言，构成这个词的字母组合本身就带着某种险恶，其含义清晰，且鲜少是虚张声势的威胁。

===Original===
Only a very few days more—appallingly few to the hearts which had set themselves bravely to hope against hope—three weeks, a month later, perhaps, the name of ships under the blight of the "Overdue" heading shall appear again in the column of "Shipping Intelligence," but under the final declaration of "Missing."

"The ship, or barque, or brig So-and-So, bound from such a port, with such and such cargo, for such another port, having left at such and such a date, last spoken at sea on such a day, and never having been heard of since, was posted today as missing." Such in its strictly official eloquence is the form of funeral orations on ships that, perhaps wearied with a long struggle, or in some unguarded moment that may come to the readiest of us, had let themselves be overwhelmed by a sudden blow from the enemy.

Who can say? Perhaps the men she carried had asked her to do too much, had stretched beyond breaking-point the enduring faithfulness which seems wrought and hammered into that assemblage of iron ribs and plating, of wood and steel and canvas and wire, which goes to the making of a ship—a complete creation endowed with character, individuality, qualities and defects, by men whose hands launch her upon the water, and that other men shall learn to know with an intimacy surpassing the intimacy of man with man, to love with a love nearly as great as that of man for woman, and often as blind in its infatuated disregard of defects.

===Chinese===
只需再过寥寥数日——对那些勇敢地抱持绝望中之希望的心灵而言，这寥寥数日令人惊恐地短暂——三周、一个月后，或许，那些笼罩在"逾期"标题阴影下的船舶名字，将再次出现在"航运消息"栏目中，但这次是在最终的宣告之下："失踪"（Missing）。

"某某号三桅帆船（barque）或双桅横帆船（brig），从某某港驶往某某港，载有某某货物，于某日启航，最后于某日被海上目击，此后杳无音讯，今日被公告为失踪。"这便是以严格官方的雄辩形式，为那些或许已厌倦了漫长搏斗、或在某个疏忽大意的瞬间（这种瞬间可能降临到我们中最警觉的人身上）被敌人突袭击垮的船舶所作的悼词。

谁能说清呢？也许她搭载的船员要求她做得太多，将那似乎被锻造并锤打进铁肋、铁板、木材、钢铁、帆布和缆索之组合体——这构成了一艘船——的坚韧忠诚拉伸到了断裂点之外。这艘船是一件完整的造物，被赋予了性格、个性、品质与缺陷；那些用双手将她放归水面的人塑造了她，而另一些人则将学会以超越人与人之间亲密程度的亲密去了解她，以近乎男人对女人之爱般的爱去爱她，并且往往像痴迷地忽视缺陷的恋人一样盲目。

===Original===
There are ships which bear a bad name, but I have yet to meet one whose crew for the time being failed to stand up angrily for her against every criticism. One ship which I call to mind now had the reputation of killing somebody every voyage she made. This was no calumny, and yet I remember well, somewhere far back in the late seventies, that the crew of that ship were, if anything, rather proud of her evil fame, as if they had been an utterly corrupt lot of desperadoes glorying in their association with an atrocious creature. We, belonging to other vessels moored all about the Circular Quay in Sydney, used to shake our heads at her with a great sense of the unblemished virtue of our own well-loved ships.

I shall not pronounce her name. She is "missing" now, after a sinister but, from the point of view of her owners, a useful career extending over many years, and, I should say, across every ocean of our globe. Having killed a man for every voyage, and perhaps rendered more misanthropic by the infirmities that come with years upon a ship, she had made up her mind to kill all hands at once before leaving the scene of her exploits. A fitting end, this, to a life of usefulness and crime—in a last outburst of an evil passion supremely satisfied on some wild night, perhaps, to the applauding clamour of wind and wave.

How did she do it? In the word "missing" there is a horrible depth of doubt and speculation. Did she go quickly from under the men's feet, or did she resist to the end, letting the sea batter her to pieces, start her butts, wrench her frame, load her with an increasing weight of salt water, and, dismasted, unmanageable, rolling heavily, her boats gone, her decks swept, had she wearied her men half to death with the unceasing labour at the pumps before she sank with them like a stone?

However, such a case must be rare. I imagine a raft of some sort could always be contrived; and, even if it saved no one, it would float on and be picked up, perhaps conveying some hint of the vanished name. Then that ship would not be, properly speaking, missing. She would be "lost with all hands," and in that distinction there is a subtle difference—less horror and a less appalling darkness.

===Chinese===
有些船背负着恶名，但我至今未曾遇到过哪艘船的船员在当时不曾愤怒地为她辩护、对抗一切批评。我现在想起一艘船，她每航行一次就要夺去一条人命。这绝非诽谤，然而我清楚地记得，在遥远的七十年代末的某个地方，那艘船的船员若有什么不同，那便是他们对她的恶名颇为自豪，仿佛他们是一群彻头彻尾的亡命之徒，因与一个残暴的生灵为伍而得意洋洋。我们这些停泊在悉尼环形码头（Circular Quay）各处其他船只上的人，总是对她摇头，心中充满对自家深受爱戴之船的无瑕美德的优越感。

我不会说出她的名字。她如今"失踪"了，在经历了一段邪恶但——从船东的角度看——却有用的生涯之后，这段生涯延续多年，而且我敢说，跨越了我们地球的每一片海洋。每航行一次便杀死一个人，或许因岁月施加于船舶的衰老而变得更加厌世，她下定决心在离开她施展身手的舞台之前，一次性杀死所有船员。这便是对一段既有益又有罪的生命的恰当终结——在某个狂野的夜晚，也许，在风浪的欢呼喧嚣中，以邪恶激情的最后一次爆发作为巅峰，得到彻底的满足。

她是如何做到的？在"失踪"这个词背后，隐藏着可怕的疑虑和猜测。她是迅速从船员脚下消失了吗？还是抵抗到了最后，任凭大海将她击成碎片，撑开她的接缝，扭曲她的框架，用不断加重的海水压垮她，最终在失去桅杆、无法操纵、沉重翻滚、小艇无踪、甲板被扫荡一空的情况下，在她像石头一样与船员们一同沉没之前，用泵的持续劳作将他们累得半死？

然而，这种情况必定罕见。我想总能设法造出某种筏子；即使它救不了任何人，它也会漂浮着被捡起，或许传递出那个消失名字的些许线索。那么这艘船严格来说便不是"失踪"。她是"连人带船全部遇难"（lost with all hands），而这种区别有着微妙的不同——恐怖少一些，骇人的黑暗也少一些。

### XVII / 第十七篇

===Original===
The unholy fascination of dread dwells in the thought of the last moments of a ship reported as “missing” in the columns of the *Shipping Gazette*. Nothing of her ever comes to light﻿—no grating, no lifebuoy, no piece of boat or branded oar﻿—to give a hint of the place and date of her sudden end. The *Shipping Gazette* does not even call her “lost with all hands.” She remains simply “missing”; she has disappeared enigmatically into a mystery of fate as big as the world, where your imagination of a brother-sailor, of a fellow-servant and lover of ships, may range unchecked.

===Chinese===
在《航运公报》（*Shipping Gazette*）的版面上，关于一艘被报告为"失踪"（missing）的船舶，其最后时刻的念头里，盘踞着一种不祥的魅惑。她的任何东西都未曾显露——没有格栅、没有救生圈、没有船板或刻有标记的船桨——无从暗示她戛然而止的地点与日期。《航运公报》甚至不说她"连人带船沉没"（lost with all hands）。她仅仅"失踪"；她已神秘地消失在一个与世界同大的命运谜团里，在那里，你对一位水手兄弟、一位同为船舶仆役与爱好者的想象，可以不受约束地驰骋。

===Original===
And yet sometimes one gets a hint of what the last scene may be like in the life of a ship and her crew, which resembles a drama in its struggle against a great force bearing it up, formless, ungraspable, chaotic and mysterious, as fate.

===Chinese===
然而有时，人们会隐约瞥见船舶与其船员生命中那最后一幕可能是何模样，它如同一场戏剧，与一股巨大的力量搏斗——那力量托举着他们，无形、不可捉摸、混沌而神秘，如同命运。

===Original===
It was on a grey afternoon in the lull of a three days’ gale that had left the Southern Ocean tumbling heavily upon our ship, under a sky hung with rags of clouds that seemed to have been cut and hacked by the keen edge of a sou’-west gale.

===Chinese===
那是一个灰蒙蒙的午后，持续三天的烈风（gale）暂歇，留下了南大洋（Southern Ocean）沉重翻滚的余波，天空悬着破絮般的云块，仿佛被凛冽的西南风（sou’-west）之刃砍劈过。

===Original===
Our craft, a Clyde-built barque of 1,000 tons, rolled so heavily that something aloft had carried away. No matter what the damage was, but it was serious enough to induce me to go aloft myself with a couple of hands and the carpenter to see the temporary repairs properly done.

===Chinese===
我们的船（craft），一条在克莱德河（Clyde）建造的一千吨三桅帆船（barque），摇摆得如此剧烈，以至于高处有东西折断了。不管损坏的是什么，都严重到足以促使我亲自带上两名水手和木匠爬上去，确保临时修理妥当。

===Original===
Sometimes we had to drop everything and cling with both hands to the swaying spars, holding our breath in fear of a terribly heavy roll. And, wallowing as if she meant to turn over with us, the barque, her decks full of water, her gear flying in bights, ran at some ten knots an hour. We had been driven far south﻿—much farther that way than we had meant to go; and suddenly, up there in the slings of the foreyard, in the midst of our work, I felt my shoulder gripped with such force in the carpenter’s powerful paw that I positively yelled with unexpected pain. The man’s eyes stared close in my face, and he shouted, “Look, sir! look! What’s this?” pointing ahead with his other hand.

===Chinese===
有时我们不得不丢下一切，双手紧抓住摇摆的桅桁（spars），屏住呼吸，唯恐船体遭遇一次骇人的剧烈横摇。而这艘三桅帆船，甲板满是积水，索具在环扣中飞扬，以每小时约10节（knots）的速度航行，那颠簸仿佛要连我们一同掀翻。我们被驱赶到了南方很远的地方——比我们意图前往的还要远得多；突然间，在前桅横桁（foreyard）的吊索高处，在我们工作之际，我感到木匠那有力的手掌紧紧抓住了我的肩膀，疼得我不由自主地大叫起来。那人的双眼直盯着我的脸，他喊道："看，先生！看！那是什么？"同时用另一只手指向前方。

===Original===
At first I saw nothing. The sea was one empty wilderness of black and white hills. Suddenly, half-concealed in the tumult of the foaming rollers I made out awash, something enormous, rising and falling﻿—something spread out like a burst of foam, but with a more bluish, more solid look.

===Chinese===
起初我什么也没看见。大海是一片空旷的荒野，遍布着黑白相间的浪峰。突然间，在半掩于泡沫翻滚的浪涛喧嚣之中，我辨认出一件巨大的东西，在涨落起伏——它铺展开来，像一簇迸发的浪花，却带着更青蓝、更坚实的色泽。

===Original===
It was a piece of an ice-floe melted down to a fragment, but still big enough to sink a ship, and floating lower than any raft, right in our way, as if ambushed among the waves with murderous intent. There was no time to get down on deck. I shouted from aloft till my head was ready to split. I was heard aft, and we managed to clear the sunken floe which had come all the way from the Southern icecap to have a try at our unsuspecting lives. Had it been an hour later, nothing could have saved the ship, for no eye could have made out in the dusk that pale piece of ice swept over by the white-crested waves.

===Chinese===
那是一块浮冰（ice-floe）融化后剩下的残片，但依然大得足以沉没一条船，它漂浮得比任何木筏都低，恰好挡在我们的航路上，仿佛在浪涛中设下埋伏，心怀杀意。没有时间下到甲板了。我在高处拼命喊叫，直喊得头痛欲裂。船尾的人听到了，我们设法避开了那块沉没的浮冰——它从南方冰盖一路漂来，意图谋害我们这毫不知情的生命。倘若晚了一个小时，什么也救不了这船，因为在昏暗中，没有任何眼睛能看清那块被白顶浪花席卷而过的苍白冰块。

===Original===
And as we stood near the taffrail side by side, my captain and I, looking at it, hardly discernible already, but still quite close-to on our quarter, he remarked in a meditative tone:

===Chinese===
当我们——我的船长和我——并肩站在艉栏杆（taffrail）旁，望着它时，它已几乎难以辨认，却仍在我们船尾斜后方很近处，他以沉思的语调说道：

===Original===
“But for the turn of that wheel just in time, there would have been another case of a ‘missing’ ship.”

===Chinese===
"要不是那一把舵转得及时，又会多一桩'失踪'船舶的案例了。"

===Original===
Nobody ever comes back from a “missing” ship to tell how hard was the death of the craft, and how sudden and overwhelming the last anguish of her men. Nobody can say with what thoughts, with what regrets, with what words on their lips they died. But there is something fine in the sudden passing away of these hearts from the extremity of struggle and stress and tremendous uproar﻿—from the vast, unrestful rage of the surface to the profound peace of the depths, sleeping untroubled since the beginning of ages.

===Chinese===
从"失踪"的船舶上，从没有人回来讲述那艘船的死有多么艰难，她船员们最后的痛苦有多么突然而令人窒息。没有人能说他们死去时怀着怎样的思绪、怎样的遗憾、唇边吐出怎样的字句。然而，当这些心脏从斗争、重压与巨大喧嚣的极端——从那表面广袤而不安的狂怒——突然消逝，归于那自太初便安眠无扰的深渊的深沉宁静时，这其中自有一种壮美。

### XVIII / 第十八篇

===Original===
But if the word “missing” brings all hope to an end and settles the loss of the underwriters, the word “overdue” confirms the fears already born in many homes ashore, and opens the door of speculation in the market of risks.

Maritime risks, be it understood. There is a class of optimists ready to reinsure an “overdue” ship at a heavy premium. But nothing can insure the hearts on shore against the bitterness of waiting for the worst.

For if a “missing” ship has never turned up within the memory of seamen of my generation, the name of an “overdue” ship, trembling as it were on the edge of the fatal heading, has been known to appear as “arrived.”

It must blaze up, indeed, with a great brilliance the dull printer’s ink expended on the assemblage of the few letters that form the ship’s name to the anxious eyes scanning the page in fear and trembling. It is like the message of reprieve from the sentence of sorrow suspended over many a home, even if some of the men in her have been the most homeless mortals that you may find among the wanderers of the sea.

===Chinese===
然而，若说“失踪”二字令一切希望化为乌有，为保险商们盖棺定论了损失，那么“逾期”二字则证实了许多岸上人家早已滋生的恐惧，并在风险市场打开了投机之门。

请理解，这指的是海事风险。有一类乐观主义者，甘愿以高额保费为“逾期”船只续保。但没有什么能保佑岸上的心灵，免受等待最坏消息时的那种苦楚。

因为，倘若在我的同辈水手的记忆里，“失踪”的船只从未有返航的先例，那么“逾期”船只的名字——它仿佛悬在致命标题的边缘颤抖——却曾被报道为“已抵达”。

那几个字母拼凑出的船名，在印刷工单调的油墨下，对于那些怀着恐惧与颤抖扫视页面的焦灼双眼而言，必定会迸发出炫目的光芒。这如同从悬在许多人家头上的悲苦判决中传来的缓刑消息，即便船上有些人，或许是海上流浪者中最无家可归的凡人。

===Original===
The reinsurer, the optimist of ill-luck and disaster, slaps his pocket with satisfaction. The underwriter, who had been trying to minimize the amount of impending loss, regrets his premature pessimism. The ship has been stauncher, the skies more merciful, the seas less angry, or perhaps the men on board of a finer temper than he has been willing to take for granted.

“The ship So-and-so, bound to such a port, and posted as ‘overdue,’ has been reported yesterday as having arrived safely at her destination.”

Thus run the official words of the reprieve addressed to the hearts ashore lying under a heavy sentence. And they come swiftly from the other side of the earth, over wires and cables, for your electric telegrap is a great alleviator of anxiety. Details, of course, shall follow. And they may unfold a tale of narrow escape, of steady ill-luck, of high winds and heavy weather, of ice, of interminable calm or endless head-gales; a tale of difficulties overcome, of adversity defied by a small knot of men upon the great loneliness of the sea; a tale of resource, of courage—of helplessness, perhaps.

===Chinese===
那再保险商，这位厄运与灾难的乐观主义者，满意地拍打着自己的口袋。那位试图将即将来临的损失降至最低的保险商，则懊悔自己过早的悲观。这船更为坚固，天空更显仁慈，大海不那么狂怒，又或许，船上的人们比他原先愿意承认的更为坚韧。

“某某号轮船，驶往某港，此前被列为‘逾期’，已于昨日安全抵达目的地。”

这便是向岸上那些背负沉重判决的心灵传达的官方缓刑辞令。它们从地球另一端飞速传来，通过电线与电缆——你们的电报，可是焦虑的绝佳缓释剂。细节当然随后便到。它们可能展开一段死里逃生、持续厄运、狂风巨浪、冰层、无休止的平静或无尽顶头风的故事；一段由一小群人在大海的广袤孤寂中战胜困难、迎击逆境的故事；一段关于智谋、勇气——或许还有无助的故事。

===Original===
Of all ships disabled at sea, a steamer who has lost her propeller is the most helpless. And if she drifts into an unpopulated part of the ocean she may soon become overdue. The menace of the “overdue” and the finality of “missing” come very quickly to steamers whose life, fed on coals and breathing the black breath of smoke into the air, goes on in disregard of wind and wave. Such a one, a big steamship, too, whose working life had been a record of faithful keeping time from land to land, in disregard of wind and sea, once lost her propeller down south, on her passage out to New Zealand.

It was the wintry, murky time of cold gales and heavy seas. With the snapping of her tail-shaft her life seemed suddenly to depart from her big body, and from a stubborn, arrogant existence she passed all at once into the passive state of a drifting log. A ship sick with her own weakness has not the pathos of a ship vanquished in a battle with the elements, wherein consists the inner drama of her life. No seaman can look without compassion upon a disabled ship, but to look at a sailing-vessel with her lofty spars gone is to look upon a defeated but indomitable warrior. There is defiance in the remaining stumps of her masts, raised up like maimed limbs against the menacing scowl of a stormy sky; there is high courage in the upward sweep of her lines towards the bow; and as soon as, on a hastily-rigged spar, a strip of canvas is shown to the wind to keep her head to sea, she faces the waves again with an unsubdued courage.

===Chinese===
在所有海上失事的船只中，失去螺旋桨的轮船是最无助的。倘若它漂流到一片人迹罕至的海域，很快便会成为“逾期”船只。“逾期”的威胁与“失踪”的终局，对于那些以煤为燃料、将黑烟喷入空中、无视风浪而前行的轮船而言，来得尤为迅疾。就有这么一艘，还是一艘大轮船，其服役生涯本是一部忠实履行班期、无视风与海的纪录，在南下前往新西兰（New Zealand）的途中，于南方水域失去了螺旋桨。

那是一个冬日阴沉、寒风凛冽、海浪汹涌的时节。随着尾轴的断裂，她的生命仿佛骤然从庞大的躯体中流逝，从一种固执而傲慢的存在，瞬间跌入了一段浮木般的被动状态。一艘因自身弱点而病倒的船，缺少那种在与自然力量搏斗中被击败的船所具有的悲怆——而后者才是其生命内在戏剧的精华。没有一个水手能不对失事的船报以同情，但观看一艘失去高耸桅桁的帆船，却如同面对一位被击败却绝不屈服的战士。那残存的桅墩，如同残肢般高举，迎向暴风雨天空的狰狞怒容，透着不屈；那向上收束至船首的线条，洋溢着无畏的勇气；而一旦在临时架起的桁上，张开一面临时帆迎风，让船首顶住海浪，她便再次以不屈的勇气，直面那滔天巨浪。

### XIX / 第十九篇

===Original===
The efficiency of a steamship consists not so much in her courage as in the power she carries within herself. It beats and throbs like a pulsating heart within her iron ribs, and when it stops, the steamer, whose life is not so much a contest as the disdainful ignoring of the sea, sickens and dies upon the waves. The sailing-ship, with her unthrobbing body, seemed to lead mysteriously a sort of unearthly existence, bordering upon the magic of the invisible forces, sustained by the inspiration of life-giving and death-dealing winds.

===Chinese===
汽船（steamship）的效能与其说在于勇气，不如说在于它自身所携带的动力。那动力在铁骨肋架内搏动，如同一颗跳动的心脏；当它停息的时候——汽船的生命与其说是对大海的抗争，不如说是对大海的鄙夷无视——它便在波浪之间衰弱而死。帆船则以它那不搏动的躯体，似乎在过着一种神秘的、超凡脱俗的生活，接近于无形之力的魔力，由赋予生命与招致死亡的风之灵感维系。

===Original===
So that big steamer, dying by a sudden stroke, drifted, an unwieldy corpse, away from the track of other ships. And she would have been posted really as "overdue," or maybe as "missing," had she not been sighted in a snowstorm, vaguely, like a strange rolling island, by a whaler going north from her Polar cruising ground. There was plenty of food on board, and I don't know whether the nerves of her passengers were at all affected by anything else than the sense of interminable boredom or the vague fear of that unusual situation. Does a passenger ever feel the life of the ship in which he is being carried like a sort of honoured bale of highly sensitive goods? For a man who has never been a passenger it is impossible to say. But I know that there is no harder trial for a seaman than to feel a dead ship under his feet.

===Chinese===
于是那艘大汽船，因一场突袭而死，像一具笨重的尸体般漂离了其他船只的航线。若非一艘从极地巡航地北上的捕鲸船（whaler）在暴风雪中隐约望见了它——如同一座奇特的、滚动着的岛屿——它本会被正式通报为"逾期"，或许"失踪"。船上的食物充裕得很，我不确定乘客的神经是否还受到其他任何东西的影响，除了无尽的倦怠感或对这异常处境的隐约恐惧。乘客是否曾感受到载着他的那艘船的生命，如同感受到一件尊贵的、高度敏感的货物？对一个从未当过乘客的人来说，这无从断言。但我知道，对水手而言，没有什么比感觉脚下是一艘死去的船更严酷的考验了。

===Original===
There is no mistaking that sensation, so dismal, so tormenting and so subtle, so full of unhappiness and unrest. I could imagine no worse eternal punishment for evil seamen who die unrepentant upon the earthly sea than that their souls should be condemned to man the ghosts of disabled ships, drifting forever across a ghostly and tempestuous ocean.

===Chinese===
那种感觉无从错认：如此阴郁，如此折磨人而又如此微妙，如此充盈着不幸与不安。我能想象的对那些死在尘世之海、至死不悟的邪恶水手们最严酷的永恒惩罚，莫过于他们的灵魂被判处永远为失去动力的船之幽灵操舵，漂流在幽冥而暴虐的海洋之上，永无止境。

===Original===
She must have looked ghostly enough, that broken-down steamer, rolling in that snowstorm— a dark apparition in a world of white snowflakes to the staring eyes of that whaler's crew. Evidently they didn't believe in ghosts, for on arrival into port her captain unromantically reported having sighted a disabled steamer in latitude somewhere about 50 degrees S. and a longitude still more uncertain. Other steamers came out to look for her, and ultimately towed her away from the cold edge of the world into a harbour with docks and workshops, where, with many blows of hammers, her pulsating heart of steel was set going again to go forth presently in the renewed pride of its strength, fed on fire and water, breathing black smoke into the air, pulsating, throbbing, shouldering its arrogant way against the great rollers in blind disdain of winds and sea.

===Chinese===
那艘破败的汽船一定看起来足够幽灵般的了——在那场暴风雪中颠簸，对那些捕鲸船员瞪视的双眼而言，是一个在白色雪花世界中的暗影。显然他们不信鬼魂，因为抵达港口后，她的船长毫无浪漫色彩地报告说在大约南纬50度、经度更加不确定的地方目击了一艘失去动力的汽船。其他汽船出来寻找她，最终将她从世界的寒冷边缘拖进了一个拥有船坞和车间的港口；在那里，随着无数次锤击，她那钢铁的搏动之心再度启动，不久便以重获的力量之骄傲再度启航，以火与水为食，向空中喷吐黑色的烟尘，搏动着、震颤着、以傲慢之势迎击巨浪，盲目而鄙夷地无视风与海。

===Original===
The track she had made when drifting while her heart stood still within her iron ribs looked like a tangled thread on the white paper of the chart. It was shown to me by a friend, her second officer. In that surprising tangle there were words in minute letters—"gales," "thick fog," "ice"—written by him here and there as memoranda of the weather. She had interminably turned upon her tracks, she had crossed and recrossed her haphazard path till it resembled nothing so much as a puzzling maze of pencilled lines without a meaning. But in that maze there lurked all the romance of the "overdue" and a menacing hint of "missing."

===Chinese===
她在漂泊时留下的轨迹——当她铁骨之内的那颗心停止跳动时——在海图的白纸上看起来像一团纠缠的线。是她的大副——我的一位朋友——拿给我看的。在那惊人的缠绕中，有一些以微小字母书写的词语——"大风"、"浓雾"、"冰"——是他各处随手写下的天气备忘。她无休止地在自己的轨迹上打转，她横越又横越她那随意飘荡的路径，直到它看起来简直就像是一个令人困惑的、铅笔线条构成的无意义迷宫。但在这个迷宫中，潜伏着"逾期"的全部浪漫，以及"失踪"的不祥暗示。

===Original===
"We had three weeks of it," said my friend, "just think of that!"
"How did you feel about it?" I asked.
He waved his hand as much as to say: It's all in the day's work. But then, abruptly, as if making up his mind:
"I'll tell you. Towards the last I used to shut myself up in my berth and cry."
"Cry?"
"Shed tears," he explained briefly, and rolled up the chart.

===Chinese===
"我们熬了整整三个星期，"我的朋友说，"你想想看！"
"你当时什么感觉？"我问。
他挥了挥手，仿佛在说：这不过是日常差事。但接着，他突然像是下定了决心：
"我告诉你吧。到了最后那阵子，我常常把自己关在铺位里哭。"
"哭？"
"掉眼泪，"他简短地解释道，然后卷起了海图。

===Original===
I can answer for it, he was a good man—as good as ever stepped upon a ship's deck—but he could not bear the feeling of a dead ship under his feet: the sickly, disheartening feeling which the men of some "overdue" ships that come into harbour at last under a jury-rig must have felt, combated, and overcome in the faithful discharge of their duty.

===Chinese===
我可以为他担保，他是个好人——好得如同踏上过甲板的人中最优秀的那一个——但他承受不了脚下是一艘死去的船的那种感觉：那种病态的、令人灰心的感觉，那些最终以临时修补的帆装驶入港口的"逾期"船上的水手们必定感受到、与之抗争并克服了，以忠实地履行他们的职责。

## The Grip of the Land / 陆地的钳制

### XX / 第二十篇

===Original===
It is difficult for a seaman to believe that his stranded ship does not feel as unhappy at the unnatural predicament of having no water under her keel as he is himself at feeling her stranded.

===Chinese===
对于一个水手来说，很难相信他的船搁浅后——处于龙骨下没有水的这种反常境地——不会和他本人一样痛苦。

===Original===
Stranding is, indeed, the reverse of sinking. The sea does not close upon the waterlogged hull with a sunny ripple, or maybe with the angry rush of a curling wave, erasing her name from the roll of living ships. No. It is as if an invisible hand had been stealthily uplifted from the bottom to catch hold of her keel as it glides through the water.

===Chinese===
搁浅与沉没恰恰相反。海水不会以粼粼波光或者卷起的怒浪裹住那灌满水的船体，将她的名字从活着的船的名册上抹去。不。仿佛有一只无形的手从海底悄然伸出，在龙骨滑过水面时一把抓住了它。

===Original===
More than any other event does "stranding" bring to the sailor a sense of utter and dismal failure. There are strandings and strandings, but I am safe to say that 90 percent of them are occasions in which a sailor, without dishonour, may well wish himself dead; and I have doubt that of those who had the experience of their ship taking the ground, 90 percent did actually for five seconds or so wish themselves dead.

===Chinese===
比起任何其他事故，"搁浅"给水手带来的那种彻底而凄惨的挫败感都要更甚。搁浅各有不同，但我敢说，其中百分之九十的场合，水手都可以——且并无辱没——希望自己已经死了；而我毫不怀疑，凡经历过自己船只坐底的人，有百分之九十确实在那五秒钟左右的时间里希望自己已死。

===Original===
"Taking the ground" is the professional expression for a ship that is stranded in gentle circumstances. But the feeling is more as if the ground had taken hold of her. It is for those on her deck a surprising sensation. It is as if your feet had been caught in an imponderable snare; you feel the balance of your body threatened, and the steady poise of your mind is destroyed at once. This sensation lasts only a second, for even while you stagger something seems to turn over in your head, bringing uppermost the mental exclamation, full of astonishment and dismay, "By Jove! she's on the ground!"

===Chinese===
"坐底"是温和情形下搁浅的专业说法。但给人的感觉更像是地面抓住了她。对于甲板上的人来说，这是一种令人惊骇的感觉。仿佛你的双脚陷入了一张无重的罗网；你感到身体的平衡受到威胁，心灵的沉稳瞬间瓦解。这种感觉只持续一秒，因为在你踉跄的同时，有什么东西在脑海里翻了个个儿，把那句充满震惊与沮丧的内心惊呼顶到了嘴边："天哪！她坐底了！"

===Original===
And that is very terrible. After all, the only mission of a seaman's calling is to keep ships' keels off the ground. Thus the moment of her stranding takes away from him every excuse for his continued existence.
