To keep ships afloat is his business; it is his trust; it is the effective formula of the bottom of all these vague impulses, dreams, and illusions that go to the making up of a boy's vocation. The grip of the land upon the keel of your ship, even if nothing worse comes of it than the wear and tear of tackle and the loss of time, remains in a seaman's memory an indelibly fixed taste of disaster.

===Chinese===
这非常可怕。说到底，水手这一行的唯一使命，就是让船的龙骨不着地。因此，搁浅的那一刻剥夺了他继续存在的一切理由。让船浮着是他的本分；是他的信托；是所有那些模糊的冲动、梦想与幻想——它们共同构成了一个男孩的志向——的底层有效方程式。陆地抓住你船的龙骨，哪怕后果不过是索具的磨损和时间的损失，在水手的记忆中也永远是一道无法抹去的灾祸之味。

===Original===
"Stranded" within the meaning of this paper stands for a more or less excusable mistake. A ship may be "driven ashore" by stress of weather. It is a catastrophe, a defeat. To be "run ashore" has the littleness, poignancy, and bitterness of human error.

===Chinese===
本文中的"搁浅"（stranded）指的是多少可以原谅的过失。一条船可以被恶劣天气"冲上岸"（driven ashore），那是灾难，是失败。而"驶上岸"（run ashore）则有着人为失误的卑微、尖锐与苦涩。

### XXI / 第二十一篇

===Original===
That is why your "strandings" are for the most part so unexpected. In fact, they are all unexpected, except those heralded by some short glimpse of the danger, full of agitation and excitement, like an awakening from a dream of incredible folly.

The land suddenly at night looms up right over your bows, or perhaps the cry of "Broken water ahead!" is raised, and some long mistake, some complicated edifice of self-delusion, overconfidence, and wrong reasoning is brought down in a fatal shock, and the heart-searing experience of your ship's keel scraping and scrunching over, say, a coral reef. It is a sound, for its size, far more terrific to your soul than that of a world coming violently to an end. But out of that chaos your belief in your own prudence and sagacity reasserts itself. You ask yourself, Where on earth did I get to? How on earth did I get there? with a conviction that it could not be your own act, that there has been at work some mysterious conspiracy of accident; that the charts are all wrong, and if the charts are not wrong, that land and sea have changed their places; that your misfortune shall forever remain inexplicable, since you have lived always with the sense of your trust, the last thing on closing your eyes, the first on opening them, as if your mind had kept firm hold of your responsibility during the hours of sleep.

You contemplate mentally your mischance, till little by little your mood changes, cold doubt steals into the very marrow of your bones, you see the inexplicable fact in another light. That is the time when you ask yourself, How on earth could I have been fool enough to get there? And you are ready to renounce all belief in your good sense, in your knowledge, in your fidelity, in what you thought till then was the best in you, giving you the daily bread of life and the moral support of other men's confidence.

===Chinese===
正因如此，你的"搁浅"在大多数时候才显得如此出人意料。事实上，除了少数几次因短促的危险预兆而惊醒——满是惊惶与兴奋，仿佛从一场荒唐透顶的梦中醒来——之外，搁浅全都是意料之外的事。

陆地在夜间突然耸立在你船首正前方，又或许有人喊了一声"前方有浅滩！"，于是一桩长久的过错，一座由自欺、自负和错误推论堆砌而成的复杂大厦，在致命的冲击中轰然倒塌，你经历了船底龙骨刮擦、碾过珊瑚礁石时那撕心裂肺的一刻。那个声响，就其体量而言，对你灵魂的震撼远比一个世界的暴力终结更为剧烈。但在这混乱之中，你对自己审慎与明智的信念又重新抬起头来。你问自己：我究竟怎么到了这一步？我到底是怎么走到这儿来的？——心里确信这不可能是你自己的作为，一定是某种神秘的事故阴谋在作祟；海图全错了，要是海图没错，那一定是陆地和海洋互换了位置；你的不幸将永远无法解释，因为你一直怀着对职责的敬畏入睡，那是你合上眼的最后一个念头，睁开眼的第一个念头，仿佛你在睡眠中也始终牢牢攥着自己的使命。

你在心中反复咀嚼这场不幸，直到心境渐渐改变，冰冷的疑虑渗入骨髓，你用另一种眼光来看这件无法解释的事。这便是你自问"我究竟蠢到什么地步才会走到这一步"的时刻。你准备好放弃对自身判断力、学识、忠诚——以及你曾以为是自己最好之物的一切——的全部信念，那曾为你提供日常生计与他人信任之精神支撑的一切。

===Original===
The ship is lost or not lost. Once stranded, you have to do your best by her. She may be saved by your efforts, by your resource and fortitude bearing up against the heavy weight of guilt and failure. And there are justifiable strandings in fogs, on uncharted seas, on dangerous shores, through treacherous tides. But, saved or not saved, there remains with her commander a distinct sense of loss, a flavour in the mouth of the real, abiding danger that lurks in all the forms of human existence. It is an acquisition, too, that feeling. A man may be the better for it, but he will not be the same. Damocles has seen the sword suspended by a hair over his head, and though a good man need not be made less valuable by such a knowledge, the feast shall not henceforth have the same flavour.

===Chinese===
船或者失了，或者没有失。一旦搁浅，你便须竭尽所能去挽救她。她或许能因你的努力、你的机智和你在罪疚与失败的重压下的坚忍而获救。雾中搁浅、在未标绘的海域搁浅、在危险的海岸搁浅、因诡谲的潮汐而搁浅——这些都是情有可原的。但无论获救与否，在指挥官的心中都留下了一种清晰的失落感，一种余味——潜伏在人类一切生存形式之中的、真实而恒久的危险的余味。这种感觉也是一种收获。一个人或许因此变得更好，但他不再是原来的那个人了。达摩克利斯（Damocles）已经看见了悬于发丝之上的利剑，虽然一个好人不会因这见识而贬值，但自此之后，盛宴的滋味便不再如前了。

===Original===
Years ago I was concerned as chief mate in a case of stranding which was not fatal to the ship. We went to work for ten hours on end, laying out anchors in readiness to heave off at high water. While I was still busy about the decks forward I heard the steward at my elbow saying: "The captain asks whether you mean to come in, sir, and have something to eat today."

I went into the cuddy. My captain sat at the head of the table like a statue. There was a strange motionlessness of everything in that pretty little cabin. The swing-table which for seventy odd days had been always on the move, if ever so little, hung quite still above the soup-tureen. Nothing could have altered the rich colour of my commander's complexion, laid on generously by wind and sea; but between the two tufts of fair hair above his ears, his skull, generally suffused with the hue of blood, shone dead white, like a dome of ivory. And he looked strangely untidy. I perceived he had not shaved himself that day; and yet the wildest motion of the ship in the most stormy latitudes we had passed through, never made him miss one single morning ever since we left the Channel. The fact must be that a commander cannot possibly shave himself when his ship is aground. I have commanded ships myself, but I don't know; I have never tried to shave in my life.

===Chinese===
多年前，我以大副身份经历了一次搁浅——船没有报废。我们连续干了十个小时，布设锚位，准备趁高潮时将船绞离浅滩。我还在前甲板忙碌时，侍者在我耳边说："船长问您今天打算不打算进来吃点东西，先生。"

我走进了船员餐室。我的船长坐在桌子尽头，像一尊雕像。那间漂亮的小小舱室里，一切都诡异地静止不动了。那张七十多天来始终轻微晃动的摇桌，此刻纹丝不动地悬在汤盆上方。风浪给我指挥官的面庞染上的那层健康的红润，什么也改变不了；但在他两鬓浅发之间，那通常血色充盈的头颅却白得死气沉沉，像一座象牙圆顶。他还显得出奇地不修边幅。我发现他那天没刮胡子；然而自从我们驶离海峡以来，即使在最狂暴的纬度上经历过最剧烈的颠簸，他也从未错过一天早晨的剃须。事实必定是：船搁了浅，指挥官就不可能刮胡子。我自己也指挥过船，但我不知道；我这辈子还从没试过刮胡子。

===Original===
He did not offer to help me or himself till I had coughed markedly several times. I talked to him professionally in a cheery tone, and ended with the confident assertion:

"We shall get her off before midnight, sir."

He smiled faintly without looking up, and muttered as if to himself:

"Yes, yes; the captain put the ship ashore and we got her off."

Then, raising his head, he attacked grumpily the steward, a lanky, anxious youth with a long, pale face and two big front teeth.

"What makes this soup so bitter? I am surprised the mate can swallow the beastly stuff. I'm sure the cook's ladled some salt water into it by mistake."

The charge was so outrageous that the steward for all answer only dropped his eyelids bashfully.

There was nothing the matter with the soup. I had a second helping. My heart was warm with hours of hard work at the head of a willing crew. I was elated with having handled heavy anchors, cables, boats without the slightest hitch; pleased with having laid out scientifically bower, stream, and kedge exactly where I believed they would do most good. On that occasion the bitter taste of a stranding was not for my mouth. That experience came later, and it was only then that I understood the loneliness of the man in charge.

It's the captain who puts the ship ashore; it's *we* who get her off.

===Chinese===
他没有起身帮我或自己，直到我刻意咳嗽了好几声。我用欢快的语气跟他谈了谈专业的事，最后自信地断言：

"午夜之前我们就能把她弄下水，先生。"

他淡淡一笑，没有抬头，自言自语般嘟囔着：

"是啊，是啊；船长把船弄搁浅了，我们再把她弄下来。"

然后他抬起头，没好气地冲侍者发难。那侍者是个瘦长、焦虑的年轻人，长脸煞白，两颗门牙很大。

"这汤怎么这么苦？大副居然喝得下这玩意儿，我真是佩服。准是厨子错舀了海水进去。"

这指控荒唐至极，侍者唯一的回应只是羞怯地垂下了眼帘。

汤并没有什么问题。我又盛了一碗。我的心里暖洋洋的，因为带着一群乐意效劳的水手干了好几个小时的重活。操纵沉重的锚、锚缆和小艇而没有出丝毫差错，这让我兴奋不已；科学地布设了船首锚、流锚和小锚，恰好放在我认为最管用的位置，这让我心满意足。那一回，搁浅的苦涩滋味没有落到我的嘴里。那体会是后来才来的，直到那时我才明白执掌之人的孤独。

是船长把船搁上了岸；是*我们*把她弄下来的。

## The Character of the Foe / 敌手的品性

### XXII / 第二十二篇

===Original===
It seems to me that no man born and truthful to himself could declare that he ever saw the sea looking young as the earth looks young in spring. But some of us, regarding the ocean with understanding and affection, have seen it looking old, as if the immemorial ages had been stirred up from the undisturbed bottom of ooze. For it is a gale of wind that makes the sea look old.

From a distance of years, looking at the remembered aspects of the storms lived through, it is that impression which disengages itself clearly from the great body of impressions left by many years of intimate contact.

If you would know the age of the earth, look upon the sea in a storm. The greyness of the whole immense surface, the wind furrows upon the faces of the waves, the great masses of foam, tossed about and waving, like matted white locks, give to the sea in a gale an appearance of hoary age, lustreless, dull, without gleams, as though it had been created before light itself.

===Chinese===
在我看来，一个生而忠实于自我的人，恐怕说不出他曾见过大海如春天的大地那般青春焕发。但我们中有些人，怀着理解与深情凝望大海，却见过它苍老的模样，仿佛那亘古的岁月已从未受惊扰的淤泥深处被搅动起来。因为，是狂风让大海显出老态。

从多年以后回望，审视那些历经风暴的记忆留下的种种印象，唯有这种苍老之感，从多年亲密接触留下的庞杂印象中清晰地剥离出来。

若想知晓大地的年岁，就去看风暴中的大海。那整个无边无际的灰色水面，那风在浪尖上犁出的沟壑，那翻腾摇曳的巨大泡沫团，如同缠结的白发，赋予了风暴中的大海一种苍老的模样，灰暗、沉闷、毫无光泽，仿佛它诞生于光本身之前。

===Original===
Looking back after much love and much trouble, the instinct of primitive man, who seeks to personify the forces of Nature for his affection and for his fear, is awakened again in the breast of one civilized beyond that stage even in his infancy. One seems to have known gales as enemies, and even as enemies one embraces them in that affectionate regret which clings to the past.

Gales have their personalities, and, after all, perhaps it is not strange; for, when all is said and done, they are adversaries whose wiles you must defeat, whose violence you must resist, and yet with whom you must live in the intimacies of nights and days.

Here speaks the man of masts and sails, to whom the sea is not a navigable element, but an intimate companion. The length of passages, the growing sense of solitude, the close dependence upon the very forces that, friendly today, without changing their nature, by the mere putting forth of their might, becomes dangerous tomorrow, make for that sense of fellowship which modern seamen, good men as they are, cannot hope to know. And, besides, your modern ship which is a steamship makes her passages on other principles than yielding to the weather and humouring the sea. She receives smashing blows, but she advances; it is a slogging fight, and not a scientific campaign. The machinery, the steel, the fire, the steam, have stepped in between the man and the sea. A modern fleet of ships does not so much make use of the sea as exploit a highway. The modern ship is not the sport of the waves. Let us say that each of her voyages is a triumphant progress; and yet it is a question whether it is not a more subtle and more human triumph to be the sport of the waves and yet survive, achieving your end.

===Chinese===
在历经许多爱与许多磨难之后回首往事，那种原始人的本能——为了寄托爱与恐惧而将自然力拟人化的本能——在一个人的心中再度苏醒，尽管他在幼年时便已超越了那一阶段。人们似乎曾将狂风视为敌人，而即便作为敌人，也带着那份萦绕着过去的眷恋之情去拥抱它们。

狂风自有其个性，而这，归根结底，或许并不奇怪；因为，说到底，它们是你必须智胜其诡计、抵抗其暴虐，却又不得不与之共度日夜、亲密相处的对手。

此刻发言的，是一位与桅杆和帆为伴的人，在他眼中，大海并非可通航的介质，而是亲密的伴侣。漫长的航程，日益滋长的孤寂感，以及对那今日友善、明日仅凭施展其伟力便可骤然变得危险的自然力的深切依赖，共同营造出一种同袍情谊——这是现代水手，无论他们多么优秀，都无法企及的。况且，你们的现代轮船，其航行原则已非顺应天气、取悦大海。它承受着猛烈的撞击，却依然前行；这是一场肉搏战，而非一场科学战役。机器、钢铁、火焰与蒸汽，已介入人与大海之间。一支现代船队与其说是利用大海，不如说是开发一条通途。现代轮船不是浪花的玩物。不妨说，它的每一次航行都是一次凯旋的进军；然而，被浪花玩弄却依然存活下来，最终达成目的——这是否是一种更微妙、更具人性的胜利，倒是个值得思索的问题。

===Original===
In his own time a man is always very modern. Whether the seamen of three hundred years hence will have the faculty of sympathy it is impossible to say. An incorrigible mankind hardens its heart in the progress of its own perfectability. How will they feel on seeing the illustrations to the sea novels of our day, or of our yesterday? It is impossible to guess. But the seaman of the last generation, brought into sympathy with the caravels of ancient time by his sailing-ship, their lineal descendant, cannot look upon those lumbering forms navigating the naive seas of ancient woodcuts without a feeling of surprise, of affectionate derision, envy, and admiration. For those things, whose unmanageableness, even when represented on paper, makes one gasp with a sort of amused horror, were manned by men who are his direct professional ancestors.

No; the seamen of three hundred years hence will probably be neither touched nor moved to derision, affection, or admiration. They will glance at the photogravures of our nearly defunct sailing-ships with a cold, inquisitive and indifferent eye. Our ships of yesterday will stand to their ships as no lineal ancestors, but as mere predecessors whose course will have been run and the race extinct. Whatever craft he handles with skill, the seaman of the future shall be, not our descendant, but only our successor.

===Chinese===
一个人身处其时代时，总是觉得自己非常现代。至于三百年后的水手是否还具备共情的能力，谁也说不准。人类那不可救药的本性，在追求自身完美的进程中，渐渐硬化了心肠。他们看到我们这个时代，或我们昨日时代的航海小说插图时，会作何感想？这无从猜测。但上一代水手，因其帆船——那些古老卡拉维尔帆船（caravels）的直系后裔——而对古代产生了共情，他们看到那些笨拙的船形在古木刻版画中描绘的稚拙海洋里航行，不可能不感到惊讶、带着爱怜的嘲弄、羡慕与钦佩。因为那些家伙，即便只是在纸面上呈现其难以驾驭的模样，也足以让人带着一种有趣的惊骇倒吸一口凉气，而驾驭它们的，正是他职业上直接的先辈。

不；三百年后的水手，很可能既不会动容，也不会生出嘲弄、爱怜或钦佩之情。他们会用冷漠、好奇而无动于衷的目光，扫视我们那些几乎消亡的帆船的照片。我们的昨日之船，对他们的船而言，并非直系祖先，而仅仅是先行者，其航程已尽，血脉已绝。无论未来水手娴熟地操弄何种船只，他都不是我们的后代，而仅仅是我们的继任者。

### XXIII / 第二十三篇

===Original===
And so much depends upon the craft which, made by man, is one with man, that the sea shall wear for him another aspect. I remember once seeing the commander—officially the master, by courtesy the captain—of a fine iron ship of the old wool fleet shaking his head at a very pretty brigantine. She was bound the other way. She was a taut, trim, neat little craft, extremely well kept; and on that serene evening when we passed her close she looked the embodiment of coquettish comfort on the sea. It was somewhere near the Cape—*The* Cape being, of course, the Cape of Good Hope, the Cape of Storms of its Portuguese discoverer. And whether it is that the word "storm" should not be pronounced upon the sea where the storms dwell thickly, or because men are shy of confessing their good hopes, it has become the nameless cape—the Cape *tout court*. The other great cape of the world, strangely enough, is seldom if ever called a cape. We say, "a voyage round the Horn"; "we rounded the Horn"; "we got a frightful battering off the Horn"; but rarely "Cape Horn," and, indeed, with some reason, for Cape Horn is as much an island as a cape. The third stormy cape of the world, which is the Leeuwin, receives generally its full name, as if to console its second-rate dignity. These are the capes that look upon the gales.

===Chinese===
人的命运何其依赖于那艘由人制造、与人合一的船舶，以至于大海将为他呈现另一副面貌。我曾见过一位指挥官——官方称船长（master），礼貌上称船长（captain）——他是老旧羊毛船队中一艘精良铁船的指挥官，对着一艘非常漂亮的双桅纵帆船（brigantine）摇头。她正驶往相反的方向。她是一艘整洁、利落、精致的小船，保养得极好；在那个宁静的傍晚，当我们近距离驶过她时，她看起来像是海上娇媚舒适的化身。那是在好望角（Cape）附近——"好望角"（*The* Cape），当然指好望角（Cape of Good Hope），其葡萄牙发现者所称的风暴角（Cape of Storms）。无论这是因为"风暴"一词不应在风暴频繁出没的大海上被提及，还是因为人们羞于承认自己的美好希望，它已成为无名之角——*the Cape tout court*（好望角，简称）。世界上另一个伟大的海角，奇怪的是，很少甚至从不被称为海角。我们说"绕合恩角的航行"；"我们绕过了合恩角"；"我们在合恩角外遭到可怕的猛击"；但很少说"合恩角"（Cape Horn），确实有些道理，因为合恩角既像海角又像岛屿。世界上第三个风暴海角是卢因角（Leeuwin），通常被完整称呼，仿佛要安慰它那二等的尊严。这些便是注视着风暴的海角。

===Original===
The little brigantine, then, had doubled the Cape. Perhaps she was coming from Port Elizabeth, from East London—who knows? It was many years ago, but I remember well the captain of the wool-clipper nodding at her with the words, "Fancy having to go about the sea in a thing like that!"

He was a man brought up in big deep-water ships, and the size of the craft under his feet was a part of his conception of the sea. His own ship was certainly big as ships went then. He may have thought of the size of his cabin, or—unconsciously, perhaps—have conjured up a vision of a vessel so small tossing amongst the great seas. I didn't inquire, and to a young second mate the captain of the little pretty brigantine, sitting astride a camp stool with his chin resting on his hands that were crossed upon the rail, might have appeared a minor king amongst men. We passed her within earshot, without a hail, reading each other's names with the naked eye.

Some years later, the second mate, the recipient of that almost involuntary mutter, could have told his captain that a man brought up in big ships may yet take a peculiar delight in what we should both then have called a small craft. Probably the captain of the big ship would not have understood very well. His answer would have been a gruff, "Give me size," as I heard another man reply to a remark praising the handiness of a small vessel. It was not a love of the grandiose or the prestige attached to the command of great ton位, for he continued, with an air of disgust and contempt, "Why, you get flung out of your bunk as likely as not in any sort of heavy weather."

===Chinese===
那么，那艘小小的双桅纵帆船（brigantine）已经绕过了海角。也许她从伊丽莎白港（Port Elizabeth）、从东伦敦（East London）来——谁知道呢？那是很多年前的事了，但我清楚地记得那位运羊毛快帆船（wool-clipper）的船长对着她点头，说道："想想看，得乘那样的东西在海上航行！"

他是一个在大型深海船舶上长大的人，脚下船舶的大小是他对大海构想的一部分。他自己的船按当时的船的标准来说确实很大。他也许想到了自己船舱的大小，或者——也许是无意识地——想象出一艘如此小的船在巨浪中颠簸的景象。我没有追问，而对于一个年轻的大副来说，那位坐在折叠凳上、下巴搁在交叉放在栏杆的双手上的漂亮小船船长，在人群中可能看起来像个小小的国王。我们在能听见呼喊的距离内驶过她，没有打招呼，用肉眼读着彼此的船名。

多年后，那个无意中听到那句嘟囔的大副，本可以告诉他的船长：一个在大型船舶上长大的人，仍然可能对我们当时都称之为小船的东西怀有特殊的喜爱。那位大船船长可能不会太理解。他的回答会是粗声粗气的"给我大船"，就像我听到另一个人在回应赞扬小船灵活时说的那样。这不是对宏伟的热爱，也不是对指挥大吨位船舶的声望的迷恋，因为他继续带着厌恶和轻蔑的神情说："哎，任何恶劣天气里，你都可能从铺位上被甩出来。"

===Original===
I don't know. I remember a few nights in my lifetime, and in a big ship, too (as big as they made them then), when one did not get flung out of one's bed simply because one never even attempted to get in; one had been made too weary, too hopeless, to try. The expedient of turning your bedding out on to a damp floor and lying on it there was no earthly good, since you could not keep your place or get a second's rest in that or any other position. But of the delight of seeing a small craft run bravely amongst the great seas there can be no question to him whose soul does not dwell ashore. Thus I well remember a three days' run got out of a little barque of 400 tons somewhere between the islands of St. Paul and Amsterdam and Cape Otway on the Australian coast. It was a hard, long gale, grey clouds and green sea, heavy weather undoubtedly, but still what a sailor would call manageable. Under two lower topsails and a reefed foresail the barque seemed to race with a long, steady sea that did not becalm her in the troughs. The solemn thundering combers caught her up from astern, passed her with a fierce boiling up of foam level with the bulwarks, swept on ahead with a swish and a roar: and the little vessel, dipping her jibboom into the tumbling froth, would go on running in a smooth, glassy hollow, a deep valley between two ridges of the sea, hiding the horizon ahead and astern. There was such fascination in her pluck, nimbleness, the continual exhibition of unfailing seaworthiness, in the semblance of courage and endurance, that I could not give up the delight of watching her run through the three unforgettable days of that gale which my mate also delighted to extol as "a famous shove."

===Chinese===
我不知道。我记得我生命中有几个夜晚，而且是在大船上（当时最大的那种），那时你不会从床上被甩出来，仅仅是因为你根本就没试图躺进去；你已经太疲惫、太绝望，不想尝试了。把铺盖卷扔到潮湿的地板上、躺在上面的权宜之计毫无用处，因为无论在那个位置还是其他任何位置，你都无法保持不动或获得片刻休息。但对于灵魂不驻留在岸上的人来说，看着小船在巨浪中勇敢地航行，其喜悦是毋庸置疑的。因此，我清楚地记得，在圣保罗岛（St. Paul）和阿姆斯特丹岛（Amsterdam）之间、澳大利亚海岸的奥特韦角（Cape Otway）附近的某处，一艘400吨的小三桅帆船（barque）连续航行了三天。那是一场漫长而猛烈的风暴，灰色的云，绿色的海，无疑是恶劣天气，但用水手的话说仍算可控。在两顶下顶帆（lower topsails）和一面收帆的前帆（reefed foresail）下，这艘三桅帆船似乎在与一道绵长而平稳的海浪竞速，海浪没有在浪谷中使她停滞。庄严轰鸣的涌浪从船尾追上她，从她身旁掠过，泡沫翻滚得与船舷齐高，呼啸着向前冲去：而小船，将艏斜桁（jibboom）探入翻滚的浪沫，继续在一道光滑、玻璃般的凹谷中航行——那是两道海脊之间的深谷，遮蔽了前后方的海平线。她的勇敢、敏捷、不断展现的适航性、勇气与坚韧的表象，这一切如此令人着迷，以至于我无法放弃观看她航行的喜悦，那场持续了三天、令人难忘的风暴，我的大副也乐于称赞为"一场了不起的猛推"。

===Original===
And this is one of those gales whose memory in after-years returns, welcome in dignified austerity, as you would remember with pleasure the noble features of a stranger with whom you crossed swords once in knightly encounter and are never to see again. In this way gales have their physiognomy. You remember them by your own feelings, and no two gales stamp themselves in the same way upon your emotions. Some cling to you in woebegone misery; others come back fiercely and weirdly, like ghouls bent upon sucking your strength away; others, again, have a catastrophic splendour; some are unvenerated recollections, as of spiteful wildcats clawing at your agonized vitals; others are severe, like a visitation; and one or two rise up draped and mysterious, with an aspect of ominous menace. In each of them there is a characteristic point at which the whole feeling seems contained in one single moment. Thus there is a certain four o'clock in the morning in the confused roar of a black and white world when coming on deck to take charge of my watch I received the instantaneous impression that the ship could not live for another hour in such a raging sea.

===Chinese===
这便是那些风暴之一，多年后记忆会回来，带着庄严的冷峻，令人愉快，就像你会愉快地回忆起一位陌生人的高贵面容——你曾在骑士般的交锋中与他交过剑，却再也见不到他了。就这样，风暴也有自己的面容。你凭自己的感受记住它们，没有两场风暴以同样的方式在你的情感上留下印记。有些以凄惨的痛苦纠缠着你；另一些则凶猛地、怪异地回来，像食尸鬼般吸干你的力量；还有一些带有灾难性的壮丽；有些是不受尊敬的回忆，像恶毒的野猫抓挠你痛苦的内脏；另一些则严厉如天灾；有一两场则披着神秘的帷幔升起，带着不祥的威胁之相。在每一场风暴中，都有一个特征性的时刻，整个感受似乎凝聚在一个瞬间。因此，在某个清晨四点，在黑白世界混乱的咆哮中，当我走上甲板接替值更时，我瞬间产生了这样一个印象：这艘船在如此狂暴的海面上活不过一个小时了。

===Original===
I wonder what became of the men who silently (you couldn't hear yourself speak) must have shared that conviction with me. To be left to write about it is not, perhaps, the most enviable fate; but the point is that this impression resumes in its intensity the whole recollection of days and days of desperately dangerous weather. We were then, for reasons which it is not worth while to specify, in the close neighbourhood of Kerguelen Land; and now, when I open an atlas and look at the tiny dots on the map of the Southern Ocean, I see as if engraved upon the paper the enraged physiognomy of that gale.

Another, strangely, recalls a silent man. And yet it was not din that was lacking; in fact, it was terrific. That one was a gale that came upon the ship swiftly, like a pampero, which last is a very sudden wind indeed. Before we knew very well what was coming all the sails we had set had burst; the furled ones were blowing loose, ropes flying, sea hissing—it hissed tremendously—wind howling, and the ship lying on her side, so that half of the crew were swimming and the other half clawing desperately at whatever came to hand, according to the side of the deck each man had been caught on by the catastrophe, either to leeward or to windward. The shouting I need not mention—it was the merest drop in an ocean of noise—and yet the character of the gale seems contained in the recollection of one small, not particularly impressive, sallow man without a cap and with a very still face. Captain Jones—let us call him Jones—had been caught unawares. Two orders he had given at the first sign of an utterly unforeseen onset; after that the magnitude of his mistake seemed to have overwhelmed him. We were doing what was needed and feasible. The ship behaved well. Of course, it was some time before we could pause in our fierce and laborious exertions; but all through the work, the excitement, the uproar, and some dismay, we were aware of this silent little man at the break of the poop, perfectly motionless, soundless, and often hidden from us by the drift of sprays.

When we officers clambered at last upon the poop, he seemed to come out of that numbed composure, and shouted to us down wind: "Try the pumps." Afterwards he disappeared. As to the ship, I need not say that, although she was presently swallowed up in one of the blackest nights I can remember, she did not disappear. In truth, I don't fancy that there had ever been much danger of that, but certainly the experience was noisy and particularly distracting—and yet it is the memory of a very quiet silence that survives.

===Chinese===
我好奇那些与我默默分享这一信念的人（你听不见自己的声音）后来怎样了。被留下来书写这件事，或许并非最令人羡慕的命运；但关键在于，这个印象以其强度涵盖了数日数日极度危险天气的全部回忆。那时，出于不值得细说的理由，我们在凯尔盖朗群岛（Kerguelen Land）附近；如今，当我打开地图集，看着南大洋地图上的小点时，我仿佛看到那场风暴愤怒的面容铭刻在纸上。

另一场风暴，奇怪地，让我想起一个沉默的人。然而并非缺乏喧嚣；事实上，那喧嚣是惊人的。那是一场像潘佩罗风（pampero）一样迅速袭击船舶的风暴，后者确实是一种非常突然的风。在我们弄清发生了什么之前，我们升起的所有帆都爆裂了；收起的帆被吹散，缆绳飞舞，大海嘶嘶作响——那嘶声震耳欲聋——风在呼啸，船侧倾着，以至于一半船员在游泳，另一半则拼命抓住手边能抓住的东西，取决于每个人被灾难抛在哪一侧甲板上，要么在下风侧，要么在上风侧。喊叫声我无需提及——在喧嚣的海洋中那只是最微不足道的一滴——然而这场风暴的特性似乎包含在一个不起眼、不太令人印象深刻、面色苍白、没戴帽子、面容异常平静的小个子男人的回忆中。琼斯船长（Captain Jones）——我们称他为琼斯——被打了个措手不及。在完全未预料的袭击初露端倪时，他下达了两道命令；之后，他错误的严重性似乎压倒了他。我们正在做必要且可行的事。船舶表现良好。当然，我们激烈的劳作之后过了一段时间才能停下来；但在整个工作、兴奋、喧嚣和些许沮丧中，我们都意识到这个沉默的小个子男人站在艉楼（poop）的断裂处，完全静止，悄无声息，经常被飘来的浪花遮蔽。

当我们这些官员最终爬上艉楼时，他似乎从那种麻木的镇定中醒来，迎风向我们喊道："试试泵。"之后他便消失了。至于船，我无需说，尽管她很快被我记忆中最黑暗的夜色之一吞噬，她并没有消失。事实上，我并不认为当时真有多大危险，但那段经历确实喧闹得特别令人心烦——然而留存下来的是对一种非常安静的寂静的记忆。

## XXIV / 第二十四篇

===Original===
For, after all, a gale of wind, the thing of mighty sound, is inarticulate. It is a man who, in a chance phrase, interprets the elemental passion of his enemy. Thus there is another gale in my memory, a thing of endless, deep, humming roar, moonlight, and a spoken sentence.

===Chinese===
说到底，大风——那声势浩大的事物——是无言的。是一个人，用一句偶发的话语，诠释了他与之搏斗的原始狂暴。因此，我记忆里还有另一场大风，一个无尽的、深沉的、嗡鸣般咆哮的事物，月光，和一句脱口而出的话。

===Original===
It was off that other cape which is always deprived of its title as the Cape of Good Hope is robbed of its name. It was off the Horn. For a true expression of dishevelled wildness there is nothing like a gale in the bright moonlight of a high latitude.

===Chinese===
那是另一处海角——一如好望角（Cape of Good Hope）被掠去了名号，它也被剥夺了头衔。那是合恩角（Cape Horn）。若要领略那种蓬头散发般的荒蛮本色，没有比高纬度明亮月光下的大风更贴切的了。

===Original===
The ship, brought-to and bowing to enormous flashing seas, glistened wet from deck to trucks; her one set sail stood out a coal-black shape upon the gloomy blueness of the air. I was a youngster then, and suffering from weariness, cold, and imperfect oilskins which let water in at every seam. I craved human companionship, and, coming off the poop, took my place by the side of the boatswain (a man whom I did not like) in a comparatively dry spot where at worst we had water only up to our knees. Above our heads the explosive booming gusts of wind passed continuously, justifying the sailor's saying "It blows great guns." And just from that need of human companionship, being very close to the man, I said, or rather shouted:

===Chinese===
船顶风停住（brought-to），在滔天巨浪前俯首，从甲板到桅顶湿漉漉地泛着光；她仅存的一面帆在阴郁的蓝灰色天空中凸显为一道煤黑色的剪影。那时我还是个毛头小伙，疲惫不堪，寒冷刺骨，油布雨衣处处开缝往里灌水。我渴望人的陪伴，便从艉楼下来，在水手长（一个我不喜欢的人）身旁找了个相对干燥的位置——最坏的情况下，水也只没到膝盖。我们头顶上，爆炸般的狂风持续隆隆掠过，印证了水手的那句老话："风猛如炮。"恰恰因为那份对人的陪伴的渴望，又因为离他很近，我开口了——或者说，喊道：

===Original===
"Blows very hard, boatswain."

===Chinese===
"风真大啊，水手长。"

===Original===
His answer was:

===Chinese===
他的回答是：

===Original===
"Ay, and if it blows only a little harder things will begin to go. I don't mind as long as everything holds, but when things begin to go it's bad."

===Chinese===
"嗯，再大一点儿，东西就该开始出事了。只要撑得住我倒不在乎，可一旦出了事就糟了。"

===Original===
The note of dread in the shouting voice, the practical truth of these words, heard years ago from a man I did not like, have stamped its peculiar character on that gale.

===Chinese===
那喊声里的恐惧，那话语中务实的真相——多年前从一个我不喜欢的人口中听到——为那场大风烙上了它独特的印记。

===Original===
A look in the eyes of a shipmate, a low murmur in the most sheltered spot where the watch on duty are huddled together, a meaning moan from one to the other with a glance at the windward sky, a sigh of weariness, a gesture of disgust passing into the keeping of the great wind, become part and parcel of the gale. The olive hue of hurricane clouds presents an aspect peculiarly appalling. The inky ragged wrack, flying before a nor'west wind, makes you dizzy with its headlong speed that depicts the rush of the invisible air. A hard sou'wester startles you with its close horizon and its low grey sky, as if the world were a dungeon wherein there is no rest for body or soul. And there are black squalls, white squalls, thunder squalls, and unexpected gusts that come without a single sign in the sky; and of each kind no one of them resembles another.

===Chinese===
同船水手眼神中的一瞥、值更水手挤在最避风处的低声呢喃、两人之间意味深长的一声呻吟伴随着对迎风天穹的抬眼一望、一声疲惫的叹息、一个厌恶的手势——风将这一切纳入它的怀抱，成为风暴的有机组成部分。飓风云团那橄榄色的色调，呈现出一种格外骇人的面貌。墨色破碎的残云在西北风前狂飞，那义无反顾的速度令人目眩，恰似无形之空气的奔涌。一场强劲的西南风以低矮的天际和灰沉沉的低压令你惊骇，仿佛整个世界是一间地牢，身心皆不得安息。还有黑飑、白飑、雷飑，以及毫无征兆便突袭而来的阵风；每一种都与另一种截然不同。

===Original===
There is infinite variety in the gales of wind at sea, and except for the peculiar, terrible, and mysterious moaning that may be heard sometimes passing through the roar of a hurricane——except for that unforgettable sound, as if the soul of the universe had been goaded into a mournful groan——it is, after all, the human voice that stamps the mark of human consciousness upon the character of a gale.

===Chinese===
海上的大风有无穷的变相，而除了那偶尔穿过飓风咆哮传来的、怪异的、可怖的、神秘的呻吟——除了那令人难忘的声音，仿佛宇宙的灵魂被刺痛而发出悲切的呻吟——归根结底，是人的声音，将人类意识的印记烙在了大风的性格上。

## Rulers of East and West / 东西方的统治者

### XXV / 第二十五篇

===Original===
There is no part of the world of coasts, continents, oceans, seas, straits, capes, and islands which is not under the sway of a reigning wind, the sovereign of its typical weather. The wind rules the aspects of the sky and the action of the sea. But no wind rules unchallenged his realm of land and water. As with the kingdoms of the earth, there are regions more turbulent than others.

===Chinese===
在海岸、大陆、大洋、海洋、海峡、海岬和岛屿构成的世界里，没有哪一个角落不处于一股当令之风的统辖之下——那风便是其典型气候的君主。风主宰着天色的面貌和大海的脾性。然而，没有哪一种风能在其陆地与水域的领地上毫无争议地称王。正如世间的王国一样，有些地方比别处更为动荡不安。

===Original===
In the middle belt of the earth the Trade Winds reign supreme, undisputed, like monarchs of long-settled kingdoms, whose traditional power, checking all undue ambitions, is not so much an exercise of personal might as the working of long-established institutions. The intertropical kingdoms of the Trade Winds are favourable to the ordinary life of a merchantman. The trumpet-call of strife is seldom borne on their wings to the watchful ears of men on the decks of ships. The regions ruled by the northeast and southeast Trade Winds are serene. In a southern-going ship, bound out for a long voyage, the passage through their dominions is characterized by a relaxation of strain and vigilance on the part of the seamen. Those citizens of the ocean feel sheltered under the aegis of an uncontested law, of an undisputed dynasty. There, indeed, if anywhere on earth, the weather may be trusted.

===Chinese===
在地球的中间地带，信风（Trade Winds）至高无上、无可争辩地统治着，宛如那些根基深厚的王国的君主，其传统权力遏制着一切僭越的野心——与其说是个人威权的施展，不如说是悠久制度的运作。信风的热带王国对商船的日常航行颇为友善。冲突的号角很少随它们的翼翅传到甲板上警觉的水手耳中。东北信风和东南信风所管辖的区域一片安详。对于一艘向南远航的船来说，穿越它们领地的那段航程，水手们的精神紧张与警惕都会有所松弛。那些海洋的公民们觉得自己庇护在一部无可争议的法律、一个无可质疑的王朝的荫蔽之下。在那个地方——如果地球上真有这样一个地方的话——天气确实可以信赖。

===Original===
Yet not too implicitly. Even in the constitutional realm of Trade Winds, north and south of the Equator, ships are overtaken by strange disturbances. Still, the easterly winds, and, generally speaking, the easterly weather all the world over, is characterized by regularity and persistence.

===Chinese===
然而也不可过于轻信。即使在赤道南北信风的宪政领地里，船只也会遭遇奇异的扰动。不过，东风——以及全世界范围内东风天气的总基调——以规律性和持久性著称。

===Original===
As a ruler, the East Wind has a remarkable stability; as an invader of the high latitudes lying under the tumultuous sway of his great brother, the Wind of the West, he is extremely difficult to dislodge, by the reason of his cold craftiness and profound duplicity.

===Chinese===
作为统治者，东风拥有非凡的稳定性；而作为侵入高纬度地区的入侵者——那里处于其伟大兄弟西风（Wind of the West）喧嚣的统治之下——它极难被驱逐，凭借的正是其冷酷的狡黠和深沉的诡诈。

===Original===
The narrow seas around these isles, where British admirals keep watch and ward upon the marches of the Atlantic Ocean, are subject to the turbulent sway of the West Wind. Call it northwest or southwest, it is all one—a different phase of the same character, a changed expression on the same face. In the orientation of the winds that rule the seas, the north and south directions are of no importance. There are no North and South Winds of any account upon this earth. The North and South Winds are but small princes in the dynasties that make peace and war upon the sea. They never assert themselves upon a vast stage. They depend upon local causes—the configuration of coasts, the shapes of straits, the accidents of bold promontories round which they play their little part. In the polity of winds, as amongst the tribes of the earth, the real struggle lies between East and West.

===Chinese===
围绕着这些岛屿的狭窄海域——英国海军上将们在大西洋（the Atlantic）的边疆上值守巡逻之地——处于西风狂暴统治的辖下。无论称它西北风还是西南风，都是一回事——不过是同一性格的不同面相，同一张脸上的表情变化。在主宰海洋的风的定向体系中，南北方向无足轻重。在这颗星球上，没有任何值得一提的北风或南风。北风和南风不过是海上王朝中制造和平与战争的小诸侯。它们从不在广阔的舞台上自作主张。它们依赖于局部原因——海岸的走向、海峡的形状、海岬的偶然轮廓——在那些地方扮演着它们的小角色。在风的政体中，正如在尘世的部族之间，真正的较量存在于东方与西方之间。

### XXVI / 第二十六篇

===Original===
The West Wind reigns over the seas surrounding the coasts of these kingdoms; and from the gateways of the channels, from promontories as if from watchtowers, from estuaries of rivers as if from postern gates, from passageways, inlets, straits, firths, the garrison of the Isle and the crews of the ships going and returning look to the westward to judge by the varied splendours of his sunset mantle the mood of that arbitrary ruler. The end of the day is the time to gaze at the kingly face of the Westerly Weather, who is the arbiter of ships’ destinies. Benignant and splendid, or splendid and sinister, the western sky reflects the hidden purposes of the royal mind. Clothed in a mantle of dazzling gold or draped in rags of black clouds like a beggar, the might of the Westerly Wind sits enthroned upon the western horizon with the whole North Atlantic as a footstool for his feet and the first twinkling stars making a diadem for his brow. Then the seamen, attentive courtiers of the weather, think of regulating the conduct of their ships by the mood of the master. The West Wind is too great a king to be a dissembler: he is no calculator plotting deep schemes in a sombre heart; he is too strong for small artifices; there is passion in all his moods, even in the soft mood of his serene days, in the grace of his blue sky whose immense and unfathomable tenderness reflected in the mirror of the sea embraces, possesses, lulls to sleep the ships with white sails. He is all things to all oceans; he is like a poet seated upon a throne—magnificent, simple, barbarous, pensive, generous, impulsive, changeable, unfathomable—but when you understand him, always the same. Some of his sunsets are like pageants devised for the delight of the multitude, when all the gems of the royal treasure-house are displayed above the sea. Others are like the opening of his royal confidence, tinged with thoughts of sadness and compassion in a melancholy splendour meditating upon the short-lived peace of the waters. And I have seen him put the pent-up anger of his heart into the aspect of the inaccessible sun, and cause it to glare fiercely like the eye of an implacable autocrat out of a pale and frightened sky.

===Chinese===
西风统治着环绕这些王国海岸的海洋；从海峡的门户，从仿佛瞭望塔般的岬角，从仿佛侧门般的河口，从航道、小湾、海峡、河口湾，岛上的守军与来往船只的船员们，都向西眺望，试图从西风那变幻的落日华服中，判断这位专横统治者的心境。一日之末，正是凝望西风那王者面容的时刻——他是船舶命运的仲裁者。温和而辉煌，或辉煌而险恶，西方的天空映照出那位王者心中不可告人的意图。身披耀眼金袍，或如乞丐般披着褴褛的黑云，西风的伟力端坐于西方地平线的宝座之上，整个北大西洋化作他脚下的脚凳，初现的闪烁星辰在他额前缀成冠冕。此时，水手们——天气那恭谨的朝臣——便想着依照主子的心情来调适自己船只的航向。西风是如此伟大的王者，不屑于伪装：他不是那种在阴暗心底盘算深谋的阴谋家；他过于强大，无需雕虫小技；他的一切情绪都饱含激情，即便在他宁静日子的柔和心境中，在他蓝天的优雅里——那浩瀚而深不可测的温柔，倒映在大海的镜中，拥抱、俘获、催眠着白帆的船只。他对所有海洋都一视同仁；他宛如一位端坐王座的诗人——雄浑、质朴、野蛮、沉思、慷慨、冲动、善变、深不可测——但当你理解了他，他始终如一。他的一些落日，宛若为取悦大众而设计的盛典，将王家宝库中的全部珍宝陈列于海面之上。另一些则像王内心的倾诉，带着哀思与怜悯的忧郁华彩，沉思着水面那短暂的和平。我曾见过他将胸中郁结的怒火，注入那遥不可及的太阳的面容，使它从苍白而惊恐的天空中，如一位无情专制者的眼睛般凶狠地怒视。

===Original===
He is the warlord who sends his battalions of Atlantic rollers to the assault of our seaboard. The compelling voice of the West Wind musters up to his service all the might of the ocean. At the bidding of the West Wind there arises a great commotion in the sky above these Islands, and a great rush of waters falls upon our shores. The sky of the Westerly Weather is full of flying clouds, of great big white clouds coming thicker and thicker till they seem to stand welded into a solid canopy, upon whose grey face the lower wrack of the gale, thin, black and angry-looking, flies past with vertiginous speed. Denser and denser grows this dome of vapours, descending lower and lower upon the sea, narrowing the horizon around the ship. And the characteristic aspect of Westerly Weather, the thick, grey, smoky, and sinister tone sets in, circumscribing the view of the men, drenching their bodies, oppressing their souls, taking their breath away with booming gusts, deafening, blinding, driving, rushing them onwards in a swaying ship towards our coasts lost in mists and rain.

The caprice of the winds, like the wilfulness of men, is fraught with the disastrous consequences of self-indulgence. Long anger, the sense of his uncontrolled power, spoils the frank and generous nature of the West Wind. It is as if his heart were corrupted by a malevolent and brooding rancour. He devastates his own kingdom in the wantonness of his force. Southwest is the quarter of the heavens where he presents his darkened brow. He breathes his rage in terrific squalls, and overwhelms his realm with an inexhaustible welter of clouds. He strews the seeds of anxiety upon the decks of scudding ships, makes the foam-stripped ocean look old, and sprinkles with grey hairs the heads of shipmasters in the homeward-bound ships running for the Channel. The Westerly Wind asserting his sway from the southwest quarter is often like a monarch gone mad, driving forth with wild imprecations the most faithful of his courtiers to shipwreck, disaster, and death.

===Chinese===
他是那位派遣大西洋涌浪军团袭击我们海岸的军阀。西风那威严的号令，召唤起海洋的全部伟力为其效劳。应西风之令，这些岛屿上空的天空便掀起巨大的骚动，汹涌的潮水扑向我们的海岸。西风天气的天空满是飞驰的云朵，巨大的白云越来越密，直至仿佛焊结成一个坚实的穹顶，而风暴下层那稀薄、黝黑、狰狞的云絮，在其灰色的表面上，以令人目眩的速度飞掠而过。这个蒸汽的穹顶变得越来越浓密，越压越低地逼近海面，不断收窄船只周围的地平线。西风天气的典型面貌——那浓重、灰暗、烟熏般、险恶的色调——开始降临，遮蔽了人们的视野，淋湿他们的身体，压抑他们的灵魂，用轰鸣的狂风夺走他们的呼吸，令人耳聋、目盲，裹挟着他们在摇晃的船中疾驰，冲向我们那迷失在雾雨中的海岸。

风的任性，如同人的固执，往往带来放纵自身的灾难性后果。长久的愤怒，对其不受约束之力的自觉，败坏了西风坦率而慷慨的本性。仿佛他的心已被恶毒而郁结的怨恨所腐蚀。他在力量的恣意妄为中，蹂躏着自己的王国。西南方向是他展现那阴沉眉宇的天空方位。他在可怕的飑风中呼出怒火，用无穷无尽翻腾的云层淹没他的疆域。他将焦虑的种子播撒在疾驰的船只甲板上，让被浪花剥去装饰的海洋显出苍老，让驶向海峡的返航船只的船长们头上平添几缕灰发。从西南方位施展其权威的西风，常似一位发狂的君主，用狂乱的诅咒，将他最忠诚的朝臣驱向海难、灾祸与死亡。

===Original===
The Southwesterly Weather is the thick weather *par excellence*. It is not the thickness of the fog; it is rather a contraction of the horizon, a mysterious veiling of the shores with clouds that seem to make a low-vaulted dungeon around the running ship. It is not blindness; it is a shortening of the sight. The West Wind does not say to the seaman, “You shall be blind”; it restricts merely the range of his vision and raises the dread of land within his breast. It makes of him a man robbed of half his force, of half his efficiency. Many times in my life, standing in long sea-boots and streaming oilskins at the elbow of my commander on the poop of a homeward-bound ship making for the Channel, and gazing ahead into the grey and tormented waste, I have heard a weary sigh shape itself into a studiously casual comment:

“Can’t see very far in this weather.”

And have made answer in the same low, perfunctory tone:

“No, sir.”

It would be merely the instinctive voicing of an ever-present thought associated closely with the consciousness of the land somewhere ahead and of the great speed of the ship. Fair wind, fair wind! Who would dare to grumble at a fair wind? It was a favour of the Western King, who rules masterfully the North Atlantic from the latitude of the Azores to the latitude of Cape Farewell. A famous shove this to end a good passage with; and yet, somehow, one could not muster upon one’s lips the smile of a courtier’s gratitude. This favour was dispensed to you from under an overbearing scowl, which is the true expression of the great autocrat when he has made up his mind to give a battering to some ships and to hunt certain others home in one breath of cruelty and benevolence, equally distracting.

“No, sir. Can’t see very far.”

Thus would the mate’s voice repeat the thought of the master, both gazing ahead, while under their feet the ship rushes at some twelve knots in the direction of the lee shore; and only a couple of miles in front of her swinging and dripping jibboom, carried naked with an upward slant like a spear, a grey horizon closes the view with a multitude of waves surging upwards violently as if to strike at the stooping clouds.

===Chinese===
西南天气是“坏天气”的典范。它并非雾的浓重；而更像是地平线的收缩，云层神秘地遮蔽了海岸，仿佛在疾驰的船只四周筑起一个低矮的穹顶地牢。它不是失明；而是视线的缩短。西风并未对水手说：“你将失明”；它仅仅是限制了他的视野范围，却在他心中唤起了对陆地的恐惧。它使人丧失了一半的力量，一半的效能。我一生中许多次，穿着长筒海靴和滴水的油布雨衣，站在一艘驶向海峡的返航船只的艉楼甲板上，在指挥官身旁，凝望着前方那灰暗而翻腾的荒原，曾听到一声疲惫的叹息，化作一句刻意装作随意的评论：

“这天气看不远。”

而我也总是以同样低沉、敷衍的语调回答：

“是的，先生。”

这不过是将一种时刻萦绕的思绪——与前方某处陆地的意识以及船只的高速航行紧密相连——本能地表达出来。顺风，顺风！谁敢抱怨顺风呢？这是西风王的恩典，他从亚速尔群岛（Azores）到法韦尔角（Cape Farewell）的纬度间，威严地统治着北大西洋。这是一记著名的推动，足以圆满结束一次良好的航程；然而，不知为何，人们却无法在唇边挤出朝臣般的感激微笑。这份恩典，是在一副傲慢的怒容下赐予你的——那才是这位伟大专制者的真实面目，当他决定给予某些船只一顿痛击，或用同一种残忍与仁慈的气息，将另一些船只驱赶回家时，那种令人迷惑的神情。

“是的，先生。看不远。”

于是，大副的声音会重复船长的心思，两人都凝望着前方，而他们脚下的船正以约十二节的速度冲向背风岸；就在她那摇荡滴水的艏斜桁——光秃秃地斜指向上方，如一柄长矛——前方仅两英里处，灰色的地平线便封住了视野，无数海浪汹涌上涌，仿佛要击打那低垂的云层。

===Original===
Awful and threatening scowls darken the face of the West Wind in his clouded, southwest mood; and from the King’s throne-hall in the western board stronger gusts reach you, like the fierce shouts of raving fury to which only the gloomy grandeur of the scene imparts a saving dignity. A shower pelts the deck and the sails of the ship as if flung with a scream by an angry hand; and when the night closes in, the night of a southwesterly gale, it seems more hopeless than the shade of Hades. The southwesterly mood of the great West Wind is a lightless mood, without sun, moon, or stars, with no gleam of light but the phosphorescent flashes of the great sheets of foam that, boiling up on each side of the ship, fling bluish gleams upon her dark and narrow hull, rolling as she runs, chased by enormous seas, distracted in the tumult. There are some bad nights in the kingdom of the West Wind for homeward-bound ships making for the Channel; and the days of wrath dawn upon them colourless and vague like the timid turning up of invisible lights upon the scene of a tyrannical and passionate outbreak, awful in the monotony of its method and the increasing strength of its violence. It is the same wind, the same clouds, the same wildly racing seas, the same thick horizon around the ship. Only the wind is stronger, the clouds seem denser and more overwhelming, the waves appear to have grown bigger and more threatening during the night. The hours, whose minutes are marked by the crash of the breaking seas, slip by with the screaming, pelting squalls overtaking the ship as she runs on and on with darkened canvas, with streaming spars and dripping ropes. The downpours thicken. Preceding each shower a mysterious gloom, like the passage of a shadow above the firmament of grey clouds, filters down upon the ship. Now and then the rain pours upon your head in streams as if from spouts. It seems as if your ship were going to be drowned before she sank, as if all atmosphere had turned to water. You gasp, you splutter, you are blinded and deafened, you are submerged, obliterated, dissolved, annihilated, streaming all over as if your limbs, too, had turned to water. And every nerve on the alert you watch for the clearing-up mood of the Western King, that shall come with a shift of wind as likely as not to whip all the three masts out of your ship in the twinkling of an eye.

===Chinese===
在西南方位的阴郁心境中，西风的面容被可怖而威胁性的怒容所笼罩；从西方那王者的殿堂，更猛烈的阵风向你袭来，如同疯狂的怒吼，唯有这场景的阴沉壮丽，才赋予其一丝体面的尊严。一阵骤雨如愤怒的手抛掷般，尖叫着抽打船只的甲板和船帆；当黑夜降临，这西南风暴之夜，似乎比冥府的阴影更令人绝望。伟大的西风那西南心境，是一个无光的心境，没有日、月、星辰，没有任何光亮，唯有船只两侧沸腾而起的巨大浪沫，将幽蓝的微光投射在她那黑暗而狭窄的船体上——她滚动着前行，被巨浪追逐，在混乱中惊惶失措。在西风王国里，对驶向海峡的返航船只而言，有一些可怕的夜晚；而愤怒的白昼在它们面前展开，苍白而模糊，如同在一个暴虐而激烈的爆发场景中，怯生生地亮起了无形的灯光——其单调的手段和不断增强的暴力，都令人敬畏。是同样的风，同样的云，同样疯狂竞逐的海浪，船只周围同样浓重的地平线。只是风更猛了，云似乎更浓、更具压迫感，海浪在夜间似乎变得更大、更具威胁。那些小时，其分钟由崩浪的撞击声标记，在尖叫的飑风追击下溜走——船只在风帆紧缩、桅桁淌水、绳索滴水的状态下，继续疾驰。暴雨愈发密集。每阵雨前，一团神秘的幽暗，如同影子掠过灰色云层的天穹，笼罩在船只上空。雨水不时如管道般从头顶倾泻而下。仿佛你的船在沉没之前就要被淹死，仿佛整个大气都化为了水。你喘息，你呛咳，你目盲耳聋，你被吞没、抹除、溶解、消灭，浑身湿透，仿佛你的四肢也化为了水。你每一根神经都处于警觉状态，等待着西风王那转晴的心境——它或许会随风向的转变而来，但更可能在一眨眼间，将你船上的三根桅杆尽数扫走。

### XXVII / 第二十七篇

===Original===
Heralded by the increasing fierceness of the squalls, sometimes by a faint flash of lightning like the signal of a lighted torch waved far away behind the clouds, the shift of wind comes at last, the crucial moment of the change from the brooding and veiled violence of the southwest gale to the sparkling, flashing, cutting, clear-eyed anger of the King's northwesterly mood. You behold another phase of his passion, a fury bejewelled with stars, mayhap bearing the crescent of the moon on its brow, shaking the last vestiges of its torn cloud-mantle in inky-black squalls, with hail and sleet descending like showers of crystals and pearls, bounding off the spars, drumming on the sails, pattering on the oilskin coats, whitening the decks of homeward-bound ships.

===Chinese===
风向的转变终于来了——先是飑越来越猛烈，有时伴随一道隐约的闪光，像远处云层后面一支燃着的火炬在挥舞的信号。这一刻是关键的转折，从西南风暴那沉郁而遮蔽的暴虐，转向西北风凌厉、明澈、切肤的怒意。你目睹了这位君主激情的另一面：一腔镶满星辰的狂怒，也许眉间还悬着一弯月牙，在漆黑如墨的飑中抖落破烂云氅的最后残片；冰雹与雨雪如水晶和珍珠般倾泻而下，弹跳在帆桁上，鼓击着船帆，噼啪敲打着油布雨衣，将归航之船的甲板染成一片白色。

===Original===
Faint, ruddy flashes of lightning flicker in the starlight upon her mastheads. A chilly blast hums in the taut rigging, causing the ship to tremble to her very keel, and the soaked men on her decks to shiver in their wet clothes to the very marrow of their bones. Before one squall has flown over to sink in the eastern board, the edge of another peeps up already above the western horizon, racing up swift, shapeless, like a black bag full of frozen water ready to burst over your devoted head.

===Chinese===
微弱的、泛红的闪电在星光中明灭不定，映照在她的桅顶。一股寒风在绷紧的索具间嗡嗡作响，令船身从龙骨开始颤抖，甲板上浑身湿透的水手们裹着湿漉漉的衣服，冷到骨髓里去。一个飑还没飞过东边的天际沉落下去，另一个飑的边缘已从西边的海平线上探出头来，疾速攀升，没有形状，像一只装满冰水的黑袋子，即将在你头顶爆裂开来。

===Original===
The temper of the ruler of the ocean has changed. Each gust of the clouded mood that seemed warmed by the heat of a heart flaming with anger has its counterpart in the chilly blasts that seem blown from a breast turned to ice with a sudden revulsion of feeling. Instead of blinding your eyes and crushing your soul with a terrible apparatus of cloud and mists and seas and rain, the King of the West turns his power to contemptuous pelting of your back with icicles, to making your weary eyes water as if in grief, and your worn-out carcass quake pitifully. But each mood of the great autocrat has its own greatness, and each is hard to bear. Only the northwest phase of that mighty display is not demoralizing to the same extent, because between the hail and sleet squalls of a northwesterly gale one can see a long way ahead.

===Chinese===
海洋统治者的性情变了。那阴沉云层中每一阵似乎被愤怒之心的烈焰加热过的疾风，都对应着另一阵仿佛从一颗因骤然反悔而冻成冰的胸膛里吹出的寒冽之风。西风之王不再用云、雾、海与雨的恐怖装置来蒙蔽你的眼睛、碾碎你的灵魂，而是转而以冰雹轻蔑地敲打你的脊背，让你疲惫的双眼仿佛因悲伤而淌泪，让你筋疲力尽的可怜躯壳瑟瑟发抖。但这位大独裁者的每一种心绪都有其各自的壮阔，每一种都难以承受。只是在西北风的怒意中，那种骇人的景象不至于让人完全崩溃，因为在西北风的冰雹和雨雪飑之间，人还能望见前方很远。

===Original===
To see! to see!—this is the craving of the sailor, as of the rest of blind humanity. To have his path made clear for him is the aspiration of every human being in our beclouded and tempestuous existence. I have heard a reserved, silent man, with no nerves to speak of, after three days of hard running in thick southwesterly weather, burst out passionately: "I wish to God we could get sight of something!"

===Chinese===
看见！看见！——这是水手的渴望，也是所有盲目的人类共同的渴望。让前方的道路变得清晰可见，是每一个身处我们这阴云密布、风暴肆虐的存在中的人的夙愿。我曾听过一个沉默寡言、几乎没有神经可言的人，在浓重的西南天气中连续苦跑了三天之后，突然爆发出一声激烈的呐喊："老天保佑，哪怕让我们看见什么东西也好！"

===Original===
We had just gone down below for a moment to commune in a battened-down cabin, with a large white chart lying limp and damp upon a cold and clammy table under the light of a smoky lamp. Sprawling over that seaman's silent and trusted adviser, with one elbow upon the coast of Africa and the other planted in the neighbourhood of Cape Hatteras (it was a general track-chart of the North Atlantic), my skipper lifted his rugged, hairy face, and glared at me in a half-exasperated, half-appealing way. We had seen no sun, moon, or stars for something like seven days. By the effect of the West Wind's wrath the celestial bodies had gone into hiding for a week or more, and the last three days had seen the force of a southwest gale grow from fresh, through strong, to heavy, as the entries in my logbook could testify.

===Chinese===
我们刚刚下到舱里，在一间密封的舱室中待了片刻，一张大幅白色海图松垮而潮湿地铺在一张冰冷粘腻的桌子上，上方一盏冒烟的灯照着。我的船长——他粗糙多毛的脸庞半是恼怒半是恳求地瞪着我——整个人趴在那张水手们默然信赖的顾问之上，一只肘撑在非洲海岸附近，另一只搁在哈特拉斯角（Cape Hatteras）一带（那是一张北大西洋总航迹图）。我们大约有七天没有见过太阳、月亮或星辰了。在西风之怒的影响下，天体隐匿了整整一周有余，而最近三天的航程日志可以证明，西南风的威力从清劲一路飙升到猛烈再到狂暴。

===Original===
Then we separated, he to go on deck again, in obedience to that mysterious call that seems to sound forever in a shipmaster's ears, I to stagger into my cabin with some vague notion of putting down the words "Very heavy weather" in a logbook not quite written up-to-date. But I gave it up, and crawled into my bunk instead, boots and hat on, all standing (it did not matter; everything was soaking wet, a heavy sea having burst the poop skylights the night before), to remain in a nightmarish state between waking and sleeping for a couple of hours of so-called rest.

===Chinese===
然后我们分开了。他又回到甲板上去，听从那似乎永远在船长耳中回响的神秘召唤；而我则摇摇晃晃走进自己的舱室，模糊地想在尚未写完的航海日志上记下"天气极恶劣"几个字。但我放弃了，转而爬进了铺位，靴子、帽子什么都没脱——也没关系，一切都湿透了，前一天夜里一排巨浪冲破了艉楼的天窗。我就这样在似醒似睡的噩梦状态中躺了大约两个小时，权当所谓的休息。

===Original===
The southwesterly mood of the West Wind is an enemy of sleep, and even of a recumbent position, in the responsible officers of a ship. After two hours of futile, lightheaded, inconsequent thinking upon all things under heaven in that dark, dank, wet and devastated cabin, I arose suddenly and staggered up on deck. The autocrat of the North Atlantic was still oppressing his kingdom and its outlying dependencies, even as far as the Bay of Biscay, in the dismal secrecy of thick, very thick, weather.

===Chinese===
西风的西南心绪，对船上负有责任的军官们来说，是睡眠乃至躺卧的敌人。在那间黑暗、潮湿、被海水蹂躏过的舱室里，我徒劳地、晕乎乎地、漫无边际地胡思乱想了两个小时天地间的一切，然后突然起身，踉踉跄跄走上甲板。北大西洋的独裁者依然在压迫着他的王国及其偏远的属地，一直延伸到比斯开湾（Bay of Biscay），笼罩在浓密、极浓密的阴霾那令人沮丧的隐秘之中。

===Original===
The force of the wind, though we were running before it at the rate of some ten knots an hour, was so great that it drove me with a steady push to the front of the poop, where my commander was holding on.
"What do you think of it?" he addressed me in an interrogative yell.

===Chinese===
风力之猛——尽管我们正以大约每小时十节的速度顺风疾驶——把我一路稳稳地推到了艉楼前端，我的指挥官正紧紧扶着那里。
"你觉得怎么样？"他以一种质问般的喊声向我发话。

===Original===
What I really thought was that we both had had just about enough of it. The manner in which the great West Wind chooses at times to administer his possessions does not commend itself to a person of peaceful and law-abiding disposition, inclined to draw distinctions between right and wrong in the face of natural forces, whose standard, naturally, is that of might alone. But, of course, I said nothing.

===Chinese===
我真正想说的是，我们俩都差不多受够了。那位伟大的西风有时选择用来治理其领地的方式，对于一个性情平和、安分守己、倾向于在自然力量面前区分是非的人来说，实在不怎么令人欣赏——而自然力量的标准，说到底，不过是力量本身罢了。不过，我当然什么都没说。

===Original===
For a man caught, as it were, between his skipper and the great West Wind silence is the safest sort of diplomacy. Moreover, I knew my skipper. He did not want to know what I thought. Shipmasters hanging on a breath before the thrones of the winds ruling the seas have their psychology, whose workings are as important to the ship and those on board of her as the changing moods of the weather.

===Chinese===
对于一个夹在船长与伟大西风之间的人来说，沉默是最安全的外交。况且，我了解我的船长。他并不想知道我的想法。那些在主宰海洋的风的王座前屏息以待的船长们，自有他们的心理运作——这种运作对船只和船上的人的影响，与天气的变幻莫测不相上下。

===Original===
The man, as a matter of fact, under no circumstances, ever cared a brass farthing for what I or anybody else in his ship thought. He had had just about enough of it, I guessed, and what he was at really was a process of fishing for a suggestion. It was the pride of his life that he had never wasted a chance, no matter how boisterous, threatening, and dangerous, of a fair wind. Like men racing blindfold for a gap in a hedge, we were finishing a splendidly quick passage from the Antipodes, with a tremendous rush for the Channel in as thick a weather as any I can remember, but his psychology did not permit him to bring the ship to with a fair wind blowing—at least not on his own initiative. And yet he felt that very soon indeed something would have to be done. He wanted the suggestion to come from me, so that later on, when the trouble was over, he could argue this point with his own uncompromising spirit, laying the blame upon my shoulders.

===Chinese===
事实上，这个人无论在何种情形下，都从未在乎过我或船上其他人哪怕一丁点儿的想法。我猜他也差不多受够了，而他实际上做的，是一个套取建议的过程。他一生引以为傲的是，无论风多么凶猛、多么具有威胁性、多么危险，他从不浪费任何利用顺风的机会。像蒙着眼睛冲向篱笆缺口的人一样，我们正在完成一段从对跖地（the Antipodes）出发的极其快捷的航程，在我记忆中最浓重的天气里一路疾冲向海峡（the Channel），但他的心理不允许他在顺风正劲时停下船来——至少不能由他主动提出。然而，他确实感觉到很快不得不做点什么了。他想让建议从我口中说出，这样日后等麻烦过去了，他可以跟自己那绝不妥协的精神争辩这一点，把责任推到我肩上。

===Original===
I must render him the justice that this sort of pride was his only weakness.
But he got no suggestion from me. I understood his psychology. Besides, I had my own stock of weaknesses at the time (it is a different one now), and amongst them was the conceit of being remarkably well up in the psychology of the Westerly Weather. I believed—not to mince matters—that I had a genius for reading the mind of the great ruler of high latitudes. I fancied I could discern already the coming of a change in his royal mood. And all I said was:
"The weather's bound to clear up with the shift of wind."
"Anybody knows that much!" he snapped at me, at the highest pitch of his voice.

===Chinese===
我必须为他说句公道话：这种骄傲是他唯一的弱点。
但他从我这里得不到建议。我理解他的心理。况且，我那时也有自己的一堆弱点（如今换成了另一堆），其中之一便是自以为对西风天气的心理了如指掌。我不怕说得直白——我相信自己有解读那位高纬度大统治者心思的天赋。我自以为已经能察觉到他皇家心绪即将发生的变化。而我说的不过是：
"风一转，天气必定会放晴。"
"这谁都知道！"他以最高的嗓门对我厉声说道。

===Original===
"I mean before dark!" I cried.
This was all the opening he ever got from me. The eagerness with which he seized upon it gave me the measure of the anxiety he had been labouring under.

===Chinese===
"我是说天黑之前！"我喊道。
这就是他从我这里得到的全部暗示。他急切地抓住这个暗示的劲头，让我掂量出了他一直在承受的焦虑有多深。

===Original===
"Very well," he shouted, with an affectation of impatience, as if giving way to long entreaties. "All right. If we don't get a shift by then we'll take that foresail off her and put her head under her wing for the night."

===Chinese===
"好吧，"他喊道，装出一副不耐烦的样子，仿佛是在长久的恳求之后终于让步。"行。到时候要是风还没转，我们就收掉前帆，让她把头埋在翅膀底下过夜。"

===Original===
I was struck by the picturesque character of the phrase as applied to a ship brought-to in order to ride out a gale with wave after wave passing under her breast. I could see her resting in the tumult of the elements like a seabird sleeping in wild weather upon the raging waters with its head tucked under its wing. In imaginative precision, in true feeling, this is one of the most expressive sentences I have ever heard on human lips.

===Chinese===
这番话用在一艘顶风停船以度过风暴——巨浪一波又一波从她胸膛下涌过——的船上，其生动的画面感令我为之一震。我能看见她在元素的喧嚣中栖息，像一只在狂风暴雨中栖于怒涛之上的海鸟，把头埋在翅膀下。在想象的精确和真实的情感上，这是我听过的最具表现力的句子之一。

===Original===
But as to taking the foresail off that ship before we put her head under her wing, I had my grave doubts. They were justified. That long enduring piece of canvas was confiscated by the arbitrary decree of the West Wind, to whom belong the lives of men and the contrivances of their hands within the limits of his kingdom. With the sound of a faint explosion it vanished into the thick weather bodily, leaving behind of its stout substance not so much as one solitary strip big enough to be picked into a handful of lint for, say, a wounded elephant. Torn out of its bolt-ropes, it faded like a whiff of smoke in the smoky drift of clouds shattered and torn by the shift of wind.

===Chinese===
但说到在我们让船把头埋进翅膀之前先收掉前帆，我心中满是疑虑。而这些疑虑是有道理的。那块饱经风霜的帆布被西风的一道独断专令没收了——在他的王国范围内，人的生命和人手的造物都归他所有。伴随着一声微弱的爆裂声，它整个地消失在浓重的天气里，它那结实的躯体连一条能捡起来搓成一小把敷料——比方说给一头受伤的大象用的——的碎布条都没留下。它被从帆边绳上撕下来，在被风向突变撕碎的云层的烟雾般的飘流中，像一缕烟般消散了。

===Original===
For the shift of wind had come. The unveiled, low sun glared angrily from a chaotic sky upon a confused and tremendous sea dashing itself upon a coast. We recognized the headland, and looked at each other in the silence of dumb wonder. Without knowing it in the least, we had run up alongside the Isle of Wight, and that tower, tinged a faint evening red in the salt wind-haze, was the lighthouse on St. Catherine's Point.

===Chinese===
因为风向已经转了。云翳散开的低垂太阳，从混乱的天空中愤怒地照耀着一片混乱的、滔天巨浪拍击海岸的汪洋。我们认出了那个海岬，在无言的惊愕中面面相觑。我们丝毫不知道自己已经不知不觉地驶到了怀特岛（Isle of Wight）旁边——那座在咸涩风雾中泛着淡淡暮红色的塔楼，正是圣凯瑟琳角（St. Catherine's Point）的灯塔。

===Original===
My skipper recovered first from his astonishment. His bulging eyes sank back gradually into their orbits. His psychology, taking it all round, was really very creditable for an average sailor. He had been spared the humiliation of laying his ship to with a fair wind; and at once that man, of an open and truthful nature, spoke up in perfect good faith, rubbing together his brown, hairy hands—the hands of a master-craftsman upon the sea:
"Humph! that's just about where I reckoned we had got to."
The transparency and ingenuousness, in a way, of that delusion, the airy tone, the hint of already growing pride, were perfectly delicious. But, in truth, this was one of the greatest surprises ever sprung by the clearing up mood of the West Wind upon one of the most accomplished of his courtiers.

===Chinese===
我的船长最先从惊愕中回过神来。他那双鼓起的眼睛慢慢缩回了眼眶。总体而言，他的心理表现对于一个普通水手来说确实相当不错。他免于在顺风中停船的屈辱；于是这个性格开朗、诚实的人立刻以十足的诚意开口说道，一边搓着他那双棕色的、毛茸茸的手——那是一位海上匠人的手：
"哼！我估摸着咱们大概就在这一带。"
那种自欺的透明与天真、那轻飘飘的语调、那隐隐已经冒出来的骄傲，实在令人忍俊不禁。但说实话，这是西风的放晴心绪有史以来向他最出色的朝臣发动的最大突袭之一。

### XXVIII / 第二十八篇

===Original===
The winds of North and South are, as I have said, but small princes amongst the powers of the sea. They have no territory of their own; they are not reigning winds anywhere. Yet it is from their houses that the reigning dynasties which have shared between them the waters of the earth are sprung. All the weather of the world is based upon the contest of the Polar and Equatorial strains of that tyrannous race. The West Wind is the greatest king. The East rules between the Tropics. They have shared each ocean between them. Each has his genius of supreme rule. The King of the West never intrudes upon the recognized dominion of his kingly brother. He is a barbarian, of a northern type. Violent without craftiness, and furious without malice, one may imagine him seated masterfully with a double-edged sword on his knees upon the painted and gilt clouds of the sunset, bowing his shock head of golden locks, a flaming beard over his breast, imposing, colossal, mighty-limbed, with a thundering voice, distended cheeks and fierce blue eyes, urging the speed of his gales. The other, the East king, the king of bloodred sunrises, I represent to myself as a spare Southerner with clear-cut features, black-browed and dark-eyed, grey-robed, upright in sunshine, resting a smooth-shaven cheek in the palm of his hand, impenetrable, secret, full of wiles, fine-drawn, keen—meditating aggressions.

===Chinese===
北方与南方的风，如我所言，不过是海洋诸力中的小公爵罢了。它们没有自己的领地，在任何地方都不是主宰之风。然而，正是从它们的谱系中，诞生了瓜分了地球水域的那些统治王朝。全世界的天气都建立在这暴虐家族中极地血统与赤道血统之间的较量之上。西风（West Wind）是最伟大的王。东风（East Wind）统治着热带之间的海域。它们各自瓜分了每一片海洋。每一位都拥有至上统治的天才。西风之王从不侵入他那王族兄弟的公认领地。他是一个北方类型的蛮族人。暴烈而无狡诈，狂怒而无恶意，你可以想象他威严地坐在落日那色彩斑斓、镀金的云霞之上，膝上横放着一把双刃剑，低垂着一头金发乱蓬蓬的脑袋，胸前火焰般的长须，威仪赫赫，身形巨硕，四肢雄健，声如雷霆，双颊涨鼓，蓝眼凶猛，催促着他的风暴加速前行。另一位，东风之王，血红日出之王，我把他想象成一个清瘦的南方人，五官棱角分明，浓眉黑眼，灰袍加身，在阳光下笔直而立，一只削瘦的手托着光滑的下颏，深不可测，神秘莫测，满腹诡计，精明，犀利——正在筹谋侵略。

===Original===
The West Wind keeps faith with his brother, the King of the Easterly Weather. "What we have divided we have divided," he seems to say in his gruff voice, this ruler without guile, who hurls as if in sport enormous masses of cloud across the sky, and flings the great waves of the Atlantic clear across from the shores of the New World upon the hoary headlands of Old Europe, which harbours more kings and rulers upon its seamed and furrowed body than all the oceans of the world together. "What we have divided we have divided; and if no rest and peace in this world have fallen to my share, leave me alone. Let me play at quoits with cyclonic gales, flinging the discs of spinning cloud and whirling air from one end of my dismal kingdom to the other: over the Great Banks or along the edges of pack-ice—this one with true aim right into the bight of the Bay of Biscay, that other upon the fjords of Norway, across the North Sea where the fishermen of many nations look watchfully into my angry eye. This is the time of kingly sport."

===Chinese===
西风信守着与他的兄弟——东风之王——的盟约。"我们瓜分了的就瓜分了，"他仿佛用粗犷的嗓音这样说，这位没有诡计的统治者，嬉戏般地将大团大团的云抛过天空，将大西洋的巨浪从新大陆的海岸直接甩向古欧洲那苍老的海角——欧洲那沟壑纵横的身躯上容纳的国王和统治者，比全世界所有海洋加在一起还要多。"我们瓜分了的就瓜分了；如果这世间的安逸与和平没有我的份，那就别管我。让我用气旋风暴玩套圈游戏，把旋转的云盘和旋涡气流从我这阴郁王国的一端甩到另一端：越过纽芬兰大浅滩（Great Banks），或者沿着浮冰边缘——这一个准准地射入比斯开湾（Bay of Biscay）的弧形水域，那一个落在挪威的峡湾上空，横跨北海——那里的多国渔民正警觉地注视着我愤怒的眼睛。这才是王者的游戏。"

===Original===
And the royal master of high latitudes sighs mightily, with the sinking sun upon his breast and the double-edged sword upon his knees, as if wearied by the innumerable centuries of a strenuous rule and saddened by the unchangeable aspect of the ocean under his feet—by the endless vista of future ages where the work of sowing the wind and reaping the whirlwind shall go on and on till his realm of living waters becomes a frozen and motionless ocean. But the other, crafty and unmoved, nursing his shaven chin between the thumb and forefinger of his slim and treacherous hand, thinks deep within his heart full of guile: "Aha! our brother of the West has fallen into the mood of kingly melancholy. He is tired of playing with circular gales, and blowing great guns, and unrolling thick streamers of fog in wanton sport at the cost of his own poor, miserable subjects. Their fate is most pitiful. Let us make a foray upon the dominions of that noisy barbarian, a great raid from Finisterre to Hatteras, catching his fishermen unawares, baffling the fleets that trust to his power, and shooting sly arrows into the livers of men who court his good graces. He is, indeed, a worthless fellow." And forthwith, while the West Wind meditates upon the vanity of his irresistible might, the thing is done, and the Easterly weather sets in upon the North Atlantic.

===Chinese===
高纬度的王者之主便长叹一声，落日映在他的胸膛，双刃剑横在膝头，仿佛被无以计数的艰辛统治岁月所累，又为脚下那亘古不变的海洋面容而哀伤——为那无尽的未来远景而哀伤，在那远景中，播撒风暴与收获旋风的劳作将永无止境地持续下去，直到他那生机勃勃的水域王国变成一片冰封死寂的海洋。而另一位——狡诈而无动于衷，用纤细而诡诈的手的拇指与食指托着光洁的下巴，在他那满腹诡计的心灵深处思忖："啊哈！我们的西风兄弟陷入了王者的忧郁。他厌倦了玩气旋风暴的游戏，厌倦了吹大炮，厌倦了以肆意的戏耍为代价，在他那些可怜的臣民头顶上展开厚重的雾帘。他们的命运何其悲惨。让我们去那个喧闹蛮族的领地掠夺一番吧——从菲尼斯特雷角（Finisterre）到哈特拉斯角（Hatteras）的大突袭，打他个措手不及，挫败那些信赖他力量的舰队，将阴险的箭射入那些巴结他恩宠的人的肝胆。他确实是个不值一提的家伙。"说干就干，当西风还在沉思他那不可抗拒之力的虚妄时，事情已经办妥，东风天气登上了北大西洋的舞台。

===Original===
The prevailing weather of the North Atlantic is typical of the way in which the West Wind rules his realm on which the sun never sets. North Atlantic is the heart of a great empire. It is the part of the West Wind's dominions most thickly populated with generations of fine ships and hardy men. Heroic deeds and adventurous exploits have been performed there, within the very stronghold of his sway. The best sailors in the world have been born and bred under the shadow of his sceptre, learning to manage their ships with skill and audacity before the steps of his stormy throne. Reckless adventurers, toiling fishermen, admirals as wise and brave as the world has ever known, have waited upon the signs of his westerly sky. Fleets of victorious ships have hung upon his breath. He has tossed in his hand squadrons of war-scarred three-deckers, and shredded out in mere sport the bunting of flags hallowed in the traditions of honour and glory. He is a good friend and a dangerous enemy, without mercy to unseaworthy ships and fainthearted seamen. In his kingly way he has taken but little account of lives sacrificed to his impulsive policy; he is a king with a double-edged sword bared in his right hand. The East Wind, an interloper in the dominions of Westerly weather, is an impassive-faced tyrant with a sharp poniard held behind his back for a treacherous stab.

===Chinese===
北大西洋的盛行天气，典型地体现了西风统治其日不落王国的方式。北大西洋是一个伟大帝国的心脏。它是西风领地中最密集地聚居着世代精良船只与坚韧水手的区域。英雄事迹与冒险壮举就在他的权力腹地之内上演。世界上最优秀的水手都在他的权杖阴影下出生、成长，在他那风暴王座的阶前学会了以精湛与胆魄驾驭船只。鲁莽的冒险者、劳碌的渔夫、世上最英明勇敢的海军上将，都在仰望他西天的征兆。凯旋舰队的命运系于他的呼吸。他将伤痕累累的战列舰（three-decker）中队在掌中颠弄，以纯粹的嬉戏撕碎了那些在光荣传统中被奉为神圣的旗帜彩带。他是一个好朋友，也是一个危险的敌人，对不适航海的船只和怯懦的水手毫无怜悯。以他王者的方式，他对他那冲动政策所牺牲的生命从不在意；他是一个右手握着出鞘双刃剑的王。东风——西风领地的闯入者——是一个面无表情的暴君，背后藏着一把锋利的匕首，专为背刺而来。

===Original===
In his forays into the North Atlantic the East Wind behaves like a subtle and cruel adventurer without a notion of honour or fair play. Veiling his clear-cut, lean face in a thin layer of a hard, high cloud, I have seen him, like a wizened robber sheik of the sea, hold up large caravans of ships to the number of three hundred or more at the very gates of the English Channel. And the worst of it was that there was no ransom that we could pay to satisfy his avidity; for whatever evil is wrought by the raiding East Wind, it is done only to spite his kingly brother of the West. We gazed helplessly at the systematic, cold, grey-eyed obstinacy of the Easterly Weather, while short rations became the order of the day, and the pinch of hunger under the breastbone grew familiar to every sailor in that held-up fleet. Every day added to our numbers. In knots and groups and straggling parties we flung to and forth before the closed gate. And meantime the outward-bound ships passed, running through our humiliated ranks under all the canvas they could show. It is my idea that the Easterly Wind helps the ships away from home in the wicked hope that they shall all come to an untimely end and be heard of no more. For six weeks did the robber sheik hold the trade route of the earth, while our liege lord, the West Wind, slept profoundly like a tired Titan, or else remained lost in a mood of idle sadness known only to frank natures. All was still to the westward; we looked in vain towards his stronghold: the King slumbered on so deeply that he let his foraging brother steal the very mantle of gold-lined purple clouds from his bowed shoulders. What had become of the dazzling hoard of royal jewels exhibited at every close of day? Gone, disappeared, extinguished, carried off without leaving a single gold band or the flash of a single sunbeam in the evening sky! Day after day through a cold streak of heavens as bare and poor as the inside of a rifled safe a rayless and despoiled sun would slink shamefacedly, without pomp or show, to hide in haste under the waters. And still the King slept on, or mourned the vanity of his might and his power, while the thin-lipped intruder put the impress of his cold and implacable spirit upon the sky and sea. With every daybreak the rising sun had to wade through a crimson stream, luminous and sinister, like the spilt blood of celestial bodies murdered during the night.

===Chinese===
东风在北大西洋的突袭中，活像一个毫无荣誉或公平竞技概念的阴险残暴的冒险家。用一层薄薄的硬质高云遮住他那棱角分明的瘦削面孔，我见过他——如同一个干瘪的海上强盗酋长——在英吉利海峡的大门口拦下了多达三百余艘船的庞大商队。最糟糕的是，我们根本付不出能令他满足的赎金；因为东风掠夺所造成的一切恶行，仅仅是为了与他的西风兄弟作对。我们无助地注视着东风天气那系统性的、冷酷的、灰眼睛般的固执，定量配给成了每日的常态，胸骨下方的饥饿挤压感成了这支被截留舰队中每个水手的家常便饭。每天都增添新的同伴。我们三五成群、零零散散地在这封闭的大门前徘徊。与此同时，出航的船只却从我们这蒙羞的队列中穿过，满帆航行。在我看来，东风帮助船只离家远航，怀着险恶的希望：它们都将遭遇不测，从此杳无音信。整整六周，这个强盗酋长把持着地球的贸易航线，而我们的君主——西风——像一个疲惫的泰坦（Titan）般沉沉大睡，或者陷入了一种只有天性坦率之人才懂的慵懒忧伤。西边一片沉寂；我们徒然地望向他的堡垒：国王沉睡得如此深沉，以至于让他的掠夺兄弟将那镶金紫云的斗篷从他低垂的肩头偷走。那些每日黄昏时分陈列的令人目眩的皇家珠宝般的云霞去了哪里？消散了，消失了，熄灭了，被席卷一空，天空中没有留下一条金边、一道阳光！日复一日，一轮被掠夺的、失去光泽的太阳，灰溜溜地、毫无排场地匆忙钻入水下，穿过一道如被洗劫过的保险柜内壁般空洞寒碜的天空。而国王依然沉睡，或者在哀叹他那力量与权能的虚妄，与此同时，薄唇的入侵者将他冷酷无情的精神烙印压在了天空与海面之上。每一个黎明，初升的太阳都不得不涉过一道猩红的洪流，明亮而不祥，仿佛夜间的天体被谋杀后流淌出的鲜血。

===Original===
In this particular instance the mean interloper held the road for some six weeks on end, establishing his particular administrative methods over the best part of the North Atlantic. It looked as if the Easterly Weather had come to stay forever, or, at least, till we had all starved to death in the held-up fleet—starved within sight, as it were, of plenty, within touch, almost, of the bountiful heart of the Empire. There we were, dotting with our white dry sails the hard blueness of the deep sea. There we were, a growing company of ships, each with her burden of grain, of timber, of wool, of hides, and even of oranges, for we had one or two belated fruit schooners in company. There we were, in that memorable spring of a certain year in the late seventies, dodging to and fro, baffled on every tack, and with our stores running down to sweepings of bread-lockers and scrapings of sugar-casks. It was just like the East Wind's nature to inflict starvation upon the bodies of unoffending sailors, while he corrupted their simple souls by an exasperation leading to outbursts of profanity as lurid as his bloodred sunrises. They were followed by grey days under the cover of high, motionless clouds that looked as if carved in a slab of ash-coloured marble. And each mean starved sunset left us calling with imprecations upon the West Wind even in its most veiled misty mood to wake up and give us our liberty, if only to rush on and dash the heads of our ships against the very walls of our unapproachable home.

===Chinese===
在这个特定的事例中，这个卑鄙的闯入者把持了航路大约六周之久，将其独特的行政手段施加于北大西洋的大半区域。东风天气仿佛要永远留下来了，或者说至少要留到我们这支被截留的舰队全体饿死——饿在丰饶的近旁，几乎触手可及帝国那慷慨心脏的近旁。我们在那里，白色的干帆点缀着深海那坚硬的蔚蓝。我们在那里，一支日益壮大的船队，每条船都满载着粮食、木材、羊毛、皮革，甚至还有橘子——因为我们中间还有一两条迟到的水果纵帆船（schooner）。我们在那里，在那个七十年代末某个值得纪念的春天，来回躲闪，每一个戗风转向都徒劳无功，而我们的存粮已经减少到面包柜底的碎屑和糖桶壁上的刮痕。这正是东风的本性：将饥饿施加于无辜水手的肉体，同时用令人暴怒的挫折腐蚀他们淳朴的灵魂，激发出与他那血红日出一样刺目的咒骂。灰色的日子接踵而至，笼罩在高高悬停、纹丝不动的云层之下——那些云看起来像是用灰白色大理石雕凿而成的。每一个吝啬而饥饿的黄昏都留下我们，用诅咒呼唤西风——即便在他最朦胧多雾的情绪中——醒来，还我们自由，哪怕只是为了让我们冲向前去，将船头撞进我们那遥不可及的家园的城墙。

### XXIX / 第二十九篇

===Original===
In the atmosphere of the Easterly Weather, as pellucid as a piece of crystal and refracting like a prism, we could see the appalling numbers of our helpless company, even to those who in more normal conditions would have remained invisible, sails down under the horizon. It is the malicious pleasure of the East Wind to augment the power of your eyesight, in order, perhaps, that you should see better the perfect humiliation, the hopeless character of your captivity. Easterly Weather is generally clear, and that is all that can be said for it—almost supernaturally clear when it likes; but whatever its mood, there is something uncanny in its nature. Its duplicity is such that it will deceive a scientific instrument. No barometer will give warning of an Easterly gale, were it ever so wet. It would be an unjust and ungrateful thing to say that a barometer is a stupid contrivance. It is simply that the wiles of the East Wind are too much for its fundamental honesty.

===Chinese===
在东风天气的大气中——澄澈如水晶，折射如棱镜——我们能看见我们那群无助同伴的惊人数量，甚至那些在更正常条件下本该隐没在地平线以下的、帆已降下的船。东风的恶意乐趣在于放大你视力的极限，或许正因如此，你才能更清楚地看到自己那彻底的屈辱、被囚的绝望本质。东风天气通常晴朗，这便是对它唯一的褒词——它乐意时近乎超自然地清澈；但无论何种心情，它的本质中总有些诡异。它的狡诈足以欺骗科学仪器。无论多潮湿，气压表都发不出东风烈风的预警。说气压表是愚蠢的装置，这既不公平也不厚道。只是东风的诡计实在超出了它诚实本性的应对能力。

===Original===
After years and years of experience the most trusty instrument of the sort that ever went to sea screwed on to a ship's cabin bulkhead will, almost invariably, be induced to rise by the diabolic ingenuity of the Easterly Weather, just at the moment when the Easterly Weather, discarding its methods of hard, dry, impassive cruelty, contemplates drowning what is left of your spirit in torrents of a peculiarly cold and horrid rain. The sleet-and-hail squalls following the lightning at the end of a Westerly gale are cold and benumbing and stinging and cruel enough. But the dry, Easterly Weather, when it turns to wet, seems to rain poisoned showers upon your head. It is a sort of steady, persistent, overwhelming, endlessly driving downpour, which makes your heart sick, and opens it to dismal forebodings.

===Chinese===
经过年复一年的经验，那种装在船舱壁板上、出过海的最可靠的仪器，几乎无一例外会被东风天气的恶魔般的心机诱骗而指针上升——偏偏就在东风天气抛弃了它那冷酷、干燥、面无表情的残忍方式，转而盘算着用一场异常冰冷可怖的暴雨淹没你残余的精神之时。西风烈风末尾伴随闪电而来的雨雪飑够冷、够麻、够刺痛、够残忍了。但干燥的东风天气一旦转潮，似乎就在你头顶降下毒雨。那是一种恒定的、执拗的、排山倒海般的、无休无止的倾泻，令人心头发怵，引出种种不祥的预感。

===Original===
And the stormy mood of the Easterly Weather looms black upon the sky with a peculiar and amazing blackness. The West Wind hangs heavy grey curtains of mist and spray before your gaze, but the Eastern interloper of the narrow seas, when he has mustered his courage and cruelty to the point of a gale, puts your eyes out, puts them out completely, makes you feel blind for life upon a lee-shore. It is the wind, also, that brings snow.

===Chinese===
而东风天气暴烈的脾气以一种独特而惊人的漆黑在天际显现。西风在你眼前垂下沉重的灰色水雾与浪花之幕，但这位狭海的东方入侵者——当他鼓足勇气与残忍酝酿成一场烈风时——会弄瞎你的眼睛，彻底弄瞎，让你觉得自己在背风海岸上永远失明。也是这风，带来了雪。

===Original===
Out of his black and merciless heart he flings a white blinding sheet upon the ships of the sea. He has more manners of villainy, and no more conscience than an Italian prince of the seventeenth century. His weapon is a dagger carried under a black cloak when he goes out on his unlawful enterprises. The mere hint of his approach fills with dread every craft that swims the sea, from fishing-smacks to four-masted ships that recognize the sway of the West Wind. Even in his most accommodating mood he inspires a dread of treachery. I have heard upwards of ten score of windlasses spring like one into clanking life in the dead of night, filling the Downs with a panic-struck sound of anchors being torn hurriedly out of the ground at the first breath of his approach.

===Chinese===
他从那颗冷酷无情的心中掷出一片刺目的白色帷幕，罩在海上的船舶上。他的恶行手段比十七世纪的意大利君主还多，良心却一样也没有。他出动从事非法勾当时，武器是藏在黑斗篷下的匕首。他行踪的蛛丝马迹便足以让海上航行的一切船只——从渔船到承认西风霸权的四桅大帆船——心生恐惧。即便在他最随和的时刻，他也会激起一种遭到背叛的恐惧。我曾听见超过两百架起锚机在深夜齐声弹起、铿然作响，整个当斯锚地（the Downs）充斥着锚被仓惶从地面扯出的惊恐之声——那是他接近时吹出的第一口气。

===Original===
Fortunately, his heart often fails him: he does not always blow home upon our exposed coast; he has not the fearless temper of his Westerly brother.

===Chinese===
所幸他经常怯阵：他并不总是在我们毫无遮蔽的海岸上全力施威；他没有他那位西风兄弟的无畏秉性。

===Original===
The natures of those two winds that share the dominions of the great oceans are fundamentally different. It is strange that the winds which men are prone to style capricious remain true to their character in all the various regions of the earth. To us here, for instance, the East Wind comes across a great continent, sweeping over the greatest body of solid land upon this earth. For the Australian east coast the East Wind is the wind of the ocean, coming across the greatest body of water upon the globe; and yet here and there its characteristics remain the same with a strange consistency in everything that is vile and base. The members of the West Wind's dynasty are modified in a way by the regions they rule, as a Hohenzollern, without ceasing to be himself, becomes a Romanian by virtue of his throne, or a Saxe-Coburg learns to put the dress of Bulgarian phrases upon his particular thoughts, whatever they are.

===Chinese===
分治大洋的两种风的本性根本不同。说来奇怪，那些人们动辄称之为反复无常的风，在地球各个区域却始终忠于自身的品性。以我们这里为例，东风跨越一片辽阔的大陆，掠过地球上最大的一块坚实陆地。对澳大利亚东海岸而言，东风却是来自大洋的风，跨越着全球最大的一片水域；然而无论在哪里，它的特性始终如一，在一切卑劣低俗之事上保持着惊人的一致性。西风王朝的成员则在一定程度上被其所统治的地区所改变，正如一位霍亨索伦（Hohenzollern）不丧失自身却因王位而成为罗马尼亚人，或一位萨克森-科堡（Saxe-Coburg）学会用保加利亚式的辞藻包装他那些无论什么内容的独特思想。

===Original===
The autocratic sway of the West Wind, whether forty north or forty south of the Equator, is characterized by an open, generous, frank, barbarous recklessness. For he is a great autocrat, and to be a great autocrat you must be a great barbarian. I have been too much moulded to his sway to nurse now any idea of rebellion in my heart. Moreover, what is a rebellion within the four walls of a room against the tempestuous rule of the West Wind? I remain faithful to the memory of the mighty King with a double-edged sword in one hand, and in the other holding out rewards of great daily runs and famously quick passages to those of his courtiers who knew how to wait watchfully for every sign of his secret mood. As we deep-water men always reckoned, he made one year in three fairly lively for anybody having business upon the Atlantic or down there along the "forties" of the Southern Ocean.

===Chinese===
西风的专制统治——无论在赤道以北四十度还是以南四十度——都以一种坦荡、慷慨、率直、蛮横的不计后果为特征。因为他是伟大的专制君主，而要做伟大的专制君主，就必须是伟大的蛮族人。我被他的统治塑造得太深，如今心中已不敢滋生任何反叛之念。况且，在四壁之内的房间里对西风的暴烈统治发起叛乱又算什么？我仍忠实地铭记着那位强大的君王——他一手持双刃剑，另一手向那些善于敏锐捕捉他隐秘心情之征兆的廷臣们抛出丰厚的日航程与著名快航的奖赏。正如我们这些深海水手一贯的估算，他每三年让大西洋或南方大洋"四十纬度带"上有事可做的人过上一年相当热闹的日子。

===Original===
You had to take the bitter with the sweet; and it cannot be denied he played carelessly with our lives and fortunes. But, then, he was always a great king, fit to rule over the great waters where, strictly speaking, a man would have no business whatever but for his audacity.

===Chinese===
你必须苦乐兼收；不可否认他随意摆弄着我们的生命与财产。但他到底始终是一位伟大的君王，配得上统治那片浩渺的水域——严格说来，若非人的胆大妄为，人类本不该在那里有任何营生。

===Original===
The audacious should not complain. A mere trader ought not to grumble at the tolls levied by a mighty king. His mightiness was sometimes very overwhelming; but even when you had to defy him openly, as on the banks of the Agulhas homeward bound from the East Indies, or on the outward passage round the Horn, he struck at you fairly his stinging blows (full in the face, too), and it was your business not to get too much staggered. And, after all, if you showed anything of a countenance, the good-natured barbarian would let you fight your way past the very steps of his throne. It was only now and then that the sword descended and a head fell; but if you fell you were sure of impressive obsequies and of a roomy, generous grave.

===Chinese===
胆大妄为者不应抱怨。一个普通的商人不应抱怨一位伟大君王征收的过路税。他的威势有时确实令人窒息；但即便你不得不公开违抗他——如同从东印度群岛返航归国时在阿古拉斯（Agulhas）浅滩上，或出海绕行合恩角（Cape Horn）时——他也会公正地向你打出刺痛的一击（而且是正中面部），你的职责就是别被打得太晕。说到底，如果你表现出几分胆气，这位宽厚的蛮族人会让你一路打到他王座的台阶前。只有偶尔才剑落头颅；但即便你倒下了，也必有体面的葬礼和一个宽敞慷慨的坟墓。

===Original===
Such is the king to whom Viking chieftains bowed their heads, and whom the modern and palatial steamship defies with impunity seven times a week. And yet it is but defiance, not victory. The magnificent barbarian sits enthroned in a mantle of gold-lined clouds looking from on high on great ships gliding like mechanical toys upon his sea and on men who, armed with fire and iron, no longer need to watch anxiously for the slightest sign of his royal mood. He is disregarded; but he has kept all his strength, all his splendour, and a great part of his power. Time itself, that shakes all the thrones, is on the side of that king. The sword in his hand remains as sharp as ever upon both its edges; and he may well go on playing his royal game of quoits with hurricanes, tossing them over from the continent of republics to the continent of kingdoms, in the assurance that both the new republics and the old kingdoms, the heat of fire and the strength of iron, with the untold generations of audacious men, shall crumble to dust at the steps of his throne, and pass away, and be forgotten before his own rule comes to an end.

===Chinese===
这便是维京首领们俯首称臣的那位君王，而现代豪华蒸汽轮船每周七次安然无恙地违抗着他。然而这不过是违抗，而非胜利。这位辉煌的蛮王端坐于金线镶边的云毯之上，从高处俯瞰大船如机械玩具般在他的海面上滑行，俯瞰那些手持火与铁、不再需要焦急等待他皇家心情之丝毫征兆的人。他被漠视了；但他保全了全部的力量、全部的辉煌和大半的权势。时间本身——那个动摇一切王座的力量——也站在这位国王一边。他手中的剑两刃依旧锋利如初；他大可继续与飓风对弈他那王室套圈游戏，将它们从共和之国抛向君主之邦，深信无论是新兴的共和国还是古老的王国、火的炽热还是铁的力量，以及世世代代胆大妄为的人，都将在他王座的台阶前化为尘土，在他统治终结之前消逝并被遗忘。

## The Faithful River / 忠实的河流

## XXX / 第三十篇

===Original===
The estuaries of rivers appeal strongly to an adventurous imagination. This appeal is not always a charm, for there are estuaries of a particularly dispiriting ugliness: lowlands, mudflats, or perhaps barren sandhills without beauty of form or amenity of aspect, covered with a shabby and scanty vegetation conveying the impression of poverty and uselessness. Sometimes such an ugliness is merely a repulsive mask. A river whose estuary resembles a breach in a sand rampart may flow through a most fertile country. But all the estuaries of great rivers have their fascination, the attractiveness of an open portal. Water is friendly to man.
