The ocean, a part of Nature furthest removed in the unchangeableness and majesty of its might from the spirit of mankind, has ever been a friend to the enterprising nations of the earth. And of all the elements this is the one to which men have always been prone to trust themselves, as if its immensity held a reward as vast as itself.

===Chinese===
河流的入海口对冒险的想象力有着强烈的感召。这种感召并非总是魅力，因为有些入海口丑陋得令人沮丧：低地、泥滩，抑或贫瘠的沙丘，既无形状之美，也无景致之悦，覆着一派破败稀疏的植被，满是贫瘠与荒废的意味。有时这种丑陋不过是一副令人生厌的面具。一条入海口状若沙堤缺口的河流，或许流经最肥沃的土地。但所有大河的入海口都自有其迷人之处——敞开门户的吸引力。水对人是友善的。海洋——自然之中在不可变更与威严两方面距人类精神最远的一部分——向来是地球上那些进取民族的朋友。在诸元素之中，人最甘愿将自己托付的便是这一个，仿佛它的浩渺蕴藏着同等浩渺的回报。

===Original===
From the offing the open estuary promises every possible fruition to adventurous hopes. That road open to enterprise and courage invites the explorer of coasts to new efforts towards the fulfilment of great expectations. The commander of the first Roman galley must have looked with an intense absorption upon the estuary of the Thames as he turned the beaked prow of his ship to the westward under the brow of the North Foreland. The estuary of the Thames is not beautiful; it has no noble features, no romantic grandness of aspect, no smiling geniality; but it is wide open, spacious, inviting, hospitable at the first glance, with a strange air of mysteriousness which lingers about it to this very day. The navigation of his craft must have engrossed all the Roman's attention in the calm of a summer's day (he would choose his weather), when the single row of long sweeps (the galley would be a light one, not a trireme) could fall in easy cadence upon a sheet of water like plate-glass, reflecting faithfully the classic form of his vessel and the contour of the lonely shores close on his left hand. I assume he followed the land and passed through what is at present known as Margate Roads, groping his careful way along the hidden sandbanks, whose every tail and spit has its beacon or buoy nowadays. He must have been anxious, though no doubt he had collected beforehand on the shores of the Gauls a store of information from the talk of traders, adventurers, fishermen, slave-dealers, pirates—all sorts of unofficial men connected with the sea in a more or less reputable way. He would have heard of channels and sandbanks, of natural features of the land useful for sea-marks, of villages and tribes and modes of barter and precautions to take: with the instructive tales about native chiefs dyed more or less blue, whose character for greediness, ferocity, or amiability must have been expounded to him with that capacity for vivid language which seems joined naturally to the shadiness of moral character and recklessness of disposition. With that sort of spiced food provided for his anxious thought, watchful for strange men, strange beasts, strange turns of the tide, he would make the best of his way up, a military seaman with a short sword on thigh and a bronze helmet on his head, the pioneer post-captain of an imperial fleet. Was the tribe inhabiting the Isle of Thanet of a ferocious disposition, I wonder, and ready to fall with stone-studded clubs and wooden lances hardened in the fire, upon the backs of unwary mariners?

===Chinese===
从外海望去，开阔的入海口向冒险的种种希许下了一切可能的果报。那条向进取与勇气敞开的道路，邀请着海岸的探险者为实现伟大的期望而不懈努力。第一艘罗马战舰的统帅一定曾以极度专注的目光凝视泰晤士河口（the Thames），当他将鸟喙般的船首转向西方，从北福兰角（North Foreland）的崖壁下驶过时。泰晤士河口并不美；它没有高贵的轮廓，没有浪漫壮丽的气象，没有和煦的亲切；但它开阔、宏大、殷勤、一瞥之间便觉好客，一种奇异的神秘感至今仍萦绕其间。在夏日的宁静里（他自会择天而行），驾驶战舰一定耗尽了那位罗马人的全部注意力——一排长桨（那该是一条轻型战舰而非三列桨战舰）有节拍地起落于如平板玻璃般平滑的水面上，忠实映照出他那古典造型的船身与左舷近在咫尺的荒寂海岸轮廓。我推测他贴着陆地航行，穿过了如今叫做马盖特锚地（Margate Roads）的水域，沿着隐没的沙洲谨慎摸索——这些沙洲的每一道沙嘴和沙脊如今都设有灯标或浮标。他一定焦虑不安，尽管毫无疑问他事先已在高卢海岸上搜集了大量信息，从商贩、冒险者、渔夫、奴隶贩子、海盗——各色以或体面或暧昧的方式与海洋关联的非官方人物——的交谈中。他一定听说过航道与沙洲、可供海标利用的陆地自然特征、村寨与部落、交易方式与防范要领，以及那些关于土著酋长的教益故事——他们或多或少染着靛蓝，其贪婪、凶残或友善的品性想必被以那种天生善用的生动语言向他渲染，而这种语言能力似乎天然与道德品格的暧昧和性情的放荡结缘。有这等辛辣的精神食粮供他焦虑的思绪咀嚼，警觉着陌生的人、陌生的兽、陌生的潮汐转折，他一路尽力向上游驶去——一个佩短剑于股、戴铜盔于首的军人水手，一支帝国舰队的先驱船长。我好奇，居住在萨尼特岛（Isle of Thanet）上的部落是否性情暴烈，随时准备以嵌石的棍棒和在火中淬硬的木矛，扑向掉以轻心的航海者们的后背？

===Original===
Amongst the great commercial streams of these islands, the Thames is the only one, I think, open to romantic feeling, from the fact that the sight of human labour and the sounds of human industry do not come down its shores to the very sea, destroying the suggestion of mysterious vastness caused by the configuration of the shore. The broad inlet of the shallow North Sea passes gradually into the contracted shape of the river; but for a long time the feeling of the open water remains with the ship steering to the westward through one of the lighted and buoyed passageways of the Thames, such as Queen's Channel, Prince's Channel, Four-Fathom Channel; or else coming down the Swin from the north. The rush of the yellow flood-tide hurries her up as if into the unknown between the two fading lines of the coast. There are no features to this land, no conspicuous, far-famed landmarks for the eye; there is nothing so far down to tell you of the greatest agglomeration of mankind on earth dwelling no more than five and twenty miles away, where the sun sets in a blaze of colour flaming on a gold background, and the dark, low shores trend towards each other. And in the great silence the deep, faint booming of the big guns being tested at Shoeburyness hangs about the Nore—a historical spot in the keeping of one of England's appointed guardians.

===Chinese===
在这些岛屿的通商大河之中，泰晤士河是唯一一条向浪漫情愫敞开的河流，原因在于：人类劳作的景象与人类工业的声响并未沿着它的两岸直抵大海，从而摧毁了海岸轮廓所营造的那种神秘而辽阔的暗示。浅海北海的宽阔入口逐渐收窄为河道的形状；但有很长一段时间，开阔水面的感觉始终伴随着向西穿过泰晤士河某条设灯标浮标的航道——如女王航道（Queen's Channel）、亲王航道（Prince's Channel）、四英寻航道（Four-Fathom Channel）——的船只，或者从北面沿斯温水道（the Swin）南下的船只。黄色涨潮的急流裹挟着她，仿佛驶向海岸两道渐渐淡去的线条之间的未知。这片土地没有显著的轮廓，没有引人注目的、远近闻名的地标可供望见；在这样远的下游，没有任何东西能告诉你，地表上最庞大的人类聚落就在不到二十五英里之外——那里落日在金色背景上燃起一片火焰般的绚丽霞光，而低矮黝暗的海岸彼此趋近。在巨大的寂静中，修伯内斯（Shoeburyness）试射重炮的低沉轰鸣萦绕在诺尔（the Nore）上空——那是一处由英格兰的守护者之一所看守的历史要地。

### XXXI / 第三十一篇

===Original===
The Nore sand remains covered at low-water, and never seen by human eye; but the Nore is a name to conjure with visions of historical events, of battles, of fleets, of mutinies, of watch and ward kept upon the great throbbing heart of the State. This ideal point of the estuary, this centre of memories, is marked upon the steely grey expanse of the waters by a lightship painted red that, from a couple of miles off, looks like a cheap and bizarre little toy. I remember how, on coming up the river for the first time, I was surprised at the smallness of that vivid object—a tiny warm speck of crimson lost in an immensity of grey tones. I was startled, as if of necessity the principal beacon in the waterway of the greatest town on earth should have presented imposing proportions. And, behold! the brown spritsail of a barge hid it entirely from my view.

Coming in from the eastward, the bright colouring of the lightship marking the part of the river committed to the charge of an Admiral (the Commander-in-Chief at the Nore) accentuates the dreariness and the great breadth of the Thames Estuary. But soon the course of the ship opens the entrance of the Medway, with its men-of-war moored in line, and the long wooden jetty of Port Victoria, with its few low buildings like the beginning of a hasty settlement upon a wild and unexplored shore. The famous Thames barges sit in brown clusters upon the water with an effect of birds floating upon a pond. On the imposing expanse of the great estuary the traffic of the port where so much of the world's work and the world's thinking is being done becomes insignificant, scattered, streaming away in thin lines of ships stringing themselves out into the eastern quarter through the various navigable channels of which the Nore lightship marks the divergence. The coasting traffic inclines to the north; the deep-water ships steer east with a southern inclination, on through the Downs, to the most remote ends of the world. In the widening of the shores sinking low in the grey, smoky distances the greatness of the sea receives the mercantile fleet of good ships that London sends out upon the turn of every tide. They follow each other, going very close by the Essex shore. Such as the beads of a rosary told by businesslike shipowners for the greater profit of the world they slip one by one into the open: while in the offing the inward-bound ships come up singly and in bunches from under the sea horizon closing the mouth of the river between Orfordness and North Foreland. They all converge upon the Nore, the warm speck of red upon the tones of drab and grey, with the distant shores running together towards the west, low and flat, like the sides of an enormous canal. The sea-reach of the Thames is straight, and, once Sheerness is left behind, its banks seem very uninhabited, except for the cluster of houses which is Southend, or here and there a lonely wooden jetty where petroleum ships discharge their dangerous cargoes, and the oil-storage tanks, low and round with slightly-domed roofs, peep over the edge of the foreshore, as it were a village of Central African huts imitated in iron. Bordered by the black and shining mudflats, the level marsh extends for miles. Away in the far background the land rises, closing the view with a continuous wooded slope, forming in the distance an interminable rampart overgrown with bushes.

===Chinese===
诺尔（the Nore）沙洲在低潮时沉于水下，永不见天日；但"诺尔"这个名字却能唤起历史事件、战役、舰队、兵变的幻象，以及对国家那伟大而跳动的心脏的守望与警戒。这个河口的理想之点，这片记忆的中心，在钢灰色的水面上标示出一艘漆成红色的灯船——离它一两英里望去，它不过是一件廉价而怪诞的小玩具。我记得第一次溯河而上时，看到那个鲜明的小东西竟然如此之小，不禁吃了一惊——它是灰色调的广袤中一个微小的温暖红点。我吓了一跳，仿佛作为世界上最伟大城市的水道中的首要航标，它理应呈现出雄伟的规模才对。然而！一条驳船棕色的斜桁帆一挡，它便完全从我视野中消失了。

从东面驶来，那艘灯船鲜艳的色彩标示出河段的管辖归属——由一位海军上将（诺尔总司令）负责——这反而突显了泰晤士河口（Thames Estuary）的荒凉与辽阔。不久，船的航线便敞开了梅德韦河（Medway）的入口，河中战舰一字排开锚泊着，维多利亚港长长的木质栈桥上矗立着几座低矮的建筑，像是在一片荒凉而未知的海岸上仓促建起的定居点雏形。著名的泰晤士驳船三五成群地浮在水面，棕褐色一片，宛如池塘上的水鸟。在大河口那气势恢宏的水面上，这座承载了世界如此多的劳作与思索的港口的交通变得微不足道、散乱无章，船舶排成细线，沿着诺尔灯船标示的各个通航航道向东方延伸。沿岸贸易航线偏向北方；远洋深水船则偏南向东行驶，穿过当斯锚地（the Downs），驶向世界最遥远的角落。在灰色烟霭中缓缓下沉、向两侧退去的海岸间，大海的恢弘接纳了伦敦每个潮汐都送出去的商船队。它们一条跟着一条，紧贴埃塞克斯海岸行驶。如同虔诚的船主们为世界更大的利润而拨动的念珠，它们逐一滑入开阔水域；而在外海，返航的船只单只或成群地从奥福德角（Orfordness）和北福兰角（North Foreland）之间那封住河口的海平面下浮现。它们全都汇聚于诺尔——那灰褐色调上的一点温暖红色——远处的海岸在西方合拢，低平如一条巨型运河的两壁。泰晤士河的海段笔直，一旦离开希尔内斯（Sheerness），两岸便显得异常荒凉，唯有绍森德（Southend）那簇房屋，或这里那里一座孤零零的木质栈桥——石油船在那里卸下危险的货物——以及那些低矮圆润、穹顶微隆的储油罐从岸线边缘探出头来，仿佛用铁仿制的中非茅屋村落。黑亮的泥滩镶边，平坦的沼泽地延伸数里。远处地势升高，以一道连绵的林木斜坡收束视野，化作远方一道似乎永无尽头的灌木丛生的壁垒。

===Original===
Then, on the slight turn of the Lower Hope Reach, clusters of factory chimneys come distinctly into view, tall and slender above the squat ranges of cement works in Grays and Greenhithe. Smoking quietly at the top against the great blaze of a magnificent sunset, they give an industrial character to the scene, speak of work, manufactures, and trade, as palm-groves on the coral strands of distant islands speak of the luxuriant grace, beauty and vigour of tropical nature. The houses of Gravesend crowd upon the shore with an effect of confusion as if they had tumbled down haphazard from the top of the hill at the back. The flatness of the Kentish shore ends there. A fleet of steam-tugs lies at anchor in front of the various piers. A conspicuous church spire, the first seen distinctly coming from the sea, has a thoughtful grace, the serenity of a fine form above the chaotic disorder of men's houses. But on the other side, on the flat Essex side, a shapeless and desolate red edifice, a vast pile of bricks with many windows and a slate roof more inaccessible than an Alpine slope, towers over the bend in monstrous ugliness, the tallest, heaviest building for miles around, a thing like an hotel, like a mansion of flats (all to let), exiled into these fields out of a street in West West Kensington. Just round the corner, as it were, on a pier defined with stone blocks and wooden piles, a white mast, slender like a stalk of straw and crossed by a yard like a knitting-needle, flying the signals of flag and balloon, watches over a set of heavy dock-gates. Mastheads and funnel-tops of ships peep above the ranges of corrugated iron roofs. This is the entrance to Tilbury Dock, the most recent of all London docks, the nearest to the sea.

===Chinese===
接着，在下霍普河段（Lower Hope Reach）微微转弯处，一簇簇工厂烟囱清晰可见，高高瘦瘦地矗立在格雷斯（Grays）和格林海斯（Greenhithe）那些低矮的水泥厂之上。它们在壮丽的落日余晖中安静地冒着烟，赋予这片景致以工业气息，诉说着劳作、制造与贸易——正如远处珊瑚海岸上的棕榈林诉说着热带自然的丰饶、优雅、美丽与活力。格雷夫森德（Gravesend）的房屋拥挤在岸上，一副混乱不堪的样子，仿佛是从背后山顶上随手滚落下来的。肯特海岸的平坦在那里终结。各码头前停泊着一支蒸汽拖轮船队。一座醒目的教堂尖塔——从海上驶来时第一座清晰辨认的建筑——有着沉思般的优雅，一种精美造型的宁静，凌驾于人类房屋的杂乱无章之上。但在对岸——平坦的埃塞克斯一侧——一座不成形、荒凉的红色庞然大物，一座由无数砖块堆砌而成的巨大建筑，窗户众多，石板屋顶比阿尔卑斯山坡更难以攀登，丑陋得令人生畏地耸立在河弯处，方圆数英里内最高最重的建筑，像一座旅馆，像一栋公寓大厦（全部出租），仿佛从西肯辛顿（West Kensington）的一条街道上被放逐到了这片田野。就在这拐角处——仿佛在一座由石块和木桩界定的栈桥上——一根白色的桅杆，细如稻草，一根横桁如织针，悬着旗号和气球信号，守望着一组沉重的船闸。船的桅顶和烟囱顶端从波纹铁皮屋顶群上方探出头来。这就是蒂尔伯里码头（Tilbury Dock）的入口，伦敦所有码头中离海最近、年代最近的一个。

===Original===
Between the crowded houses of Gravesend and the monstrous redbrick pile on the Essex shore the ship is surrendered fairly to the grasp of the river. That hint of loneliness, that soul of the sea which had accompanied her as far as the Lower Hope Reach, abandons her at the turn of the first bend above. The salt, acrid flavour is gone out of the air, together with a sense of unlimited space opening free beyond the threshold of sandbanks below the Nore. The waters of the sea rush on past Gravesend, tumbling the big mooring buoys laid along the face of the town; but the sea-freedom stops short there, surrendering the salt tide to the needs, the artifices, the contrivances of toiling men. Wharves, landing-places, dock-gates, waterside stairs, follow each other continuously right up to London Bridge, and the hum of men's work fills the river with a menacing, muttering note as of a breathless, ever-driving gale. The waterway, so fair above and wide below, flows oppressed by bricks and mortar and stone, by blackened timber and grimed glass and rusty iron, covered with black barges, whipped up by paddles and screws, overburdened with craft, overhung with chains, overshadowed by walls making a steep gorge for its bed, filled with a haze of smoke and dust.

===Chinese===
在格雷夫森德拥挤的房屋与埃塞克斯岸上那座庞大的红色砖楼之间，船已完全交到了河流的掌控之中。那一丝孤独的暗示，那伴随她直到下霍普河段的海洋之魂，在上游第一个转弯处便弃她而去了。空气中咸涩刺鼻的味道消散了，连同诺尔沙洲下方那无限开阔的空间感也一并消失。海水从格雷夫森德身旁奔涌而过，冲撞着沿城岸排列的巨大系泊浮标；但海洋的自由到此为止，咸涩的潮水让位给了劳作者的需求、机巧和手段。码头、登陆点、船闸、水边台阶一个接一个绵延不断，直抵伦敦桥，人们劳作的嗡鸣声充斥着河面，带着一种令人不安的低沉咕哝，仿佛一场令人窒息的、永不停歇的大风。这条水道，上游如此美好、下游如此宽阔，却在砖石砂浆、焦黑的木材、污秽的玻璃和锈蚀的铁器的压迫下流淌，黑压压的驳船覆盖水面，被明轮和螺旋桨搅得浪花翻涌，船舶不堪重负，铁链高悬于头顶，两壁的高墙投下阴影，将河床变成一道陡峭的峡谷，弥漫着烟尘雾霭。

===Original===
This stretch of the Thames from London Bridge to the Albert Docks is to other watersides of river ports what a virgin forest would be to a garden. It is a thing grown up, not made. It recalls a jungle by the confused, varied, and impenetrable aspect of the buildings that line the shore, not according to a planned purpose, but as if sprung up by accident from scattered seeds. Like the matted growth of bushes and creepers veiling the silent depths of an unexplored wilderness, they hide the depths of London's infinitely varied, vigorous, seething life. In other river ports it is not so. They lie open to their stream, with quays like broad clearings, with streets like avenues cut through thick timber for the convenience of trade. I am thinking now of river ports I have seen—of Antwerp, for instance; of Nantes or Bordeaux, or even old Rouen, where the night-watchmen of ships, elbows on rail, gaze at shopwindows and brilliant cafés, and see the audience go in and come out of the opera-house. But London, the oldest and greatest of river ports, does not possess as much as a hundred yards of open quays upon its river front. Dark and impenetrable at night, like the face of a forest, is the London waterside. It is the waterside of watersides, where only one aspect of the world's life can be seen, and only one kind of men toils on the edge of the stream. The lightless walls seem to spring from the very mud upon which the stranded barges lie; and the narrow lanes coming down to the foreshore resemble the paths of smashed bushes and crumbled earth where big game comes to drink on the banks of tropical streams.

Behind the growth of the London waterside the docks of London spread out unsuspected, smooth, and placid, lost amongst the buildings like dark lagoons hidden in a thick forest. They lie concealed in the intricate growth of houses with a few stalks of mastheads here and there overtopping the roof of some four-story warehouse.

===Chinese===
从伦敦桥到阿尔伯特码头的这段泰晤士河，相对于其他河港的滨水区，就好比原始森林之于花园。它是自然生长的，而非人工建造的。岸边建筑群那纷杂多样、无法穿透的面貌令人联想到丛林——它们不是按照规划的目的排列的，而是仿佛从散落的种子中偶然萌生。如同密密匝匝的灌木与藤蔓遮蔽着未被探索的荒野的幽深腹地，它们隐藏着伦敦那无穷多样、蓬勃热烈、沸腾翻涌的生活深处。其他河港不是这样。它们向河流敞开，码头如同宽阔的林中空地，街道如同为贸易便利而在密林中开辟的大道。我此刻想的是我见过的那些河港——比如安特卫普（Antwerp）；南特（Nantes）或波尔多（Bordeaux），甚至古老的鲁昂（Rouen），在那里，船上的夜班水手们肘搁栏杆，凝望商店橱窗和灯火辉煌的咖啡馆，看着观众进出歌剧院。但伦敦——最古老、最伟大的河港——在其河畔拥有的露天码头连一百码都不到。伦敦的滨水区在夜间黑暗而不可穿透，如同森林的面孔。它是滨水区中的滨水区，世间生活只在此呈现一种面貌，只有一类人在河岸边劳作。那些没有灯光的墙垣似乎从搁浅驳船所在的泥滩上直接拔地而起；通往滩涂的狭窄巷道，宛如热带溪流岸边被踏碎的灌木和泥土中的兽道，大型野兽来此饮水的地方。

在伦敦滨水区的层层建筑背后，伦敦的码头出人意料地铺展开来，平滑而宁静，隐没在建筑群中，如同密林深处的黑色潟湖。它们藏匿在错综复杂的房屋之间，只有几根桅杆顶端偶尔从某座四层仓库的屋顶上方探出头来。

===Original===
It is a strange conjunction this of roofs and mastheads, of walls and yardarms. I remember once having the incongruity of the relationship brought home to me in a practical way. I was the chief officer of a fine ship, just docked with a cargo of wool from Sydney, after a ninety days' passage. In fact, we had not been in more than half an hour and I was still busy making her fast to the stone posts of a very narrow quay in front of a lofty warehouse. An old man with a grey whisker under the chin and brass buttons on his pilot-cloth jacket, hurried up along the quay hailing my ship by name. He was one of those officials called berthing-masters—not the one who had berthed us, but another, who, apparently, had been busy securing a steamer at the other end of the dock. I could see from afar his hard blue eyes staring at us, as if fascinated, with a queer sort of absorption. I wondered what that worthy sea-dog had found to criticise in my ship's rigging. And I, too, glanced aloft anxiously. I could see nothing wrong there. But perhaps that superannuated fellow-craftsman was simply admiring the ship's perfect order aloft, I thought, with some secret pride; for the chief officer is responsible for his ship's appearance, and as to her outward condition, he is the man open to praise or blame. Meantime the old salt ("ex-coasting skipper" was writ large all over his person) had hobbled up alongside in his bumpy, shiny boots, and, waving an arm, short and thick like the flipper of a seal, terminated by a paw red as an uncooked beefsteak, addressed the poop in a muffled, faint, roaring voice, as if a sample of every North-Sea fog of his life had been permanently lodged in his throat: "Haul 'em round, Mr. Mate!" were his words. "If you don't look sharp, you'll have your topgallant yards through the windows of that 'ere warehouse presently!" This was the only cause of his interest in the ship's beautiful spars. I own that for a time I was struck dumb by the bizarre associations of yardarms and windowpanes. To break windows is the last thing one would think of in connection with a ship's topgallant yard, unless, indeed, one were an experienced berthing-master in one of the London docks. This old chap was doing his little share of the world's work with proper efficiency. His little blue eyes had made out the danger many hundred yards off. His rheumaticky feet, tired with balancing that squat body for many years upon the decks of small coasters, and made sore by miles of tramping upon the flagstones of the dock side, had hurried up in time to avert a ridiculous catastrophe. I answered him pettishly, I fear, and as if I had known all about it before.

"All right, all right! can't do everything at once."

===Chinese===
屋顶与桅杆、墙壁与桁端——这种搭配着实奇怪。我记得有一次，这种不协调的关系以一种切身的方式让我领教了。我是一条好船的大副，刚刚带着一批从悉尼运来的羊毛进港靠泊，航程九十天。事实上，我们进港不过半小时，我还在忙着将船系在一座高大仓库前方一条极窄的码头的石柱上。一个下巴底下灰白连鬓胡、粗呢夹克上铜纽扣闪亮的老人沿着码头急匆匆赶来，喊着我的船名。他是那种被称为"泊位管理员"（berthing-master）的官员——不是给我们安排泊位的那位，而是另一个，显然一直在码头另一端忙着给一条蒸汽船系泊。我远远就看见他那双坚硬的蓝眼睛盯着我们，仿佛着了魔，带着一种古怪的专注。我纳闷这位可敬的老水手在我船的索具上挑出了什么毛病。我也紧张地朝桅杆上望去。什么毛病也没看出来。但也许这位退了休的同行只是在欣赏船上索具的完美整齐，我暗自得意地想；因为大副对船的外观负全责，至于船的外部状态，他是那个接受褒贬的人。与此同时，那位老水手（"前沿海船长"几个大字写满了他全身）穿着坑坑洼洼、锃光瓦亮的靴子一瘸一拐地走到近旁，挥着一只短粗如海豹鳍肢的手臂——末端那只爪子红得像一块生牛排——用一种沉闷、微弱、仿佛在咆哮的声音朝船尾喊道，仿佛他一生中所有北海大雾的样本都永远沉积在他的喉咙里："把桁转过去，大副先生！"这是他的原话。"你要是不麻利点儿，你的顶桅帆桁马上就要捅进那边仓库的窗户了！"这就是他对船上美丽的圆材感兴趣的唯一原因。我承认，那一刻我被桁端与窗户之间那荒诞的联想震住了，一时说不出话来。打碎窗户是人们最不可能与一艘船的顶桅帆桁联系在一起的事情，除非你恰好是伦敦某个码头里经验丰富的泊位管理员。这位老伙计正在以他应有的效率完成他对世界的一份微薄贡献。他的小蓝眼在几百码外就看出了危险。他的风湿脚——在小型沿海船甲板上保持矮胖身躯的平衡多年而疲惫不堪，在码头石板路上跋涉数英里而疼痛不已——及时赶来避免了一场荒唐的灾难。我大概没好气地回答了他，而且装作自己早已了然于心。

"好的好的！又不能一下子把所有事都办了。"

===Original===
He remained near by, muttering to himself till the yards had been hauled round at my order, and then raised again his foggy, thick voice:

"None too soon," he observed, with a critical glance up at the towering side of the warehouse. "That's a half-sovereign in your pocket, Mr. Mate. You should always look first how you are for them windows before you begin to breast in your ship to the quay."

It was good advice. But one cannot think of everything or foresee contacts of things apparently as remote as stars and hop-poles.

===Chinese===
他留在近旁，自言自语地嘟囔着，直到横桁在我命令下转了过去，然后他再次举起那雾气沉沉的粗嗓门：

"再晚可就来不及了，"他瞥了一眼高耸的仓库墙壁，评论道，"这半镑金币（half-sovereign）你可省下了，大副先生。你以后靠码头之前，得先看看那些窗户的位置。"

这是好建议。但人不可能面面俱到，也不可能预见到那些看起来如星星与啤酒花架杆一般遥远的事物之间竟会产生接触。

### XXXII / 第三十二篇

===Original===
The view of ships lying moored in some of the older docks of London has always suggested to my mind the image of a flock of swans kept in the flooded backyard of grim tenement houses. The flatness of the walls surrounding the dark pool on which they float brings out wonderfully the flowing grace of the lines on which a ship's hull is built. The lightness of these forms, devised to meet the winds and the seas, makes, by contrast with the great piles of bricks, the chains and cables of their moorings appear very necessary, as if nothing less could prevent them from soaring upwards and over the roofs. The least puff of wind stealing round the corners of the dock buildings stirs these captives fettered to rigid shores. It is as if the soul of a ship were impatient of confinement.

===Chinese===
每当看到停泊在伦敦某些老码头里的船只，我脑海里总会浮现出这样一幅画面：一群天鹅被圈养在阴森公寓楼后院的一汪积水里。围合着它们漂浮的暗池的那些平直墙壁，反而奇妙地衬托出船体线条那种流畅的优雅。这些为迎接风与海而生的轻盈形体，与巨大的砖砌建筑群形成了鲜明对比，使得那些系泊的锚链和缆绳显得无比必要，仿佛唯有如此才能阻止它们腾空而起、越过屋顶。哪怕是最微弱的一股风悄悄绕过码头建筑的角落，也会搅动这些被铁链锁在僵硬岸壁上的囚徒。似乎船的灵魂天生不甘于囚禁。

===Original===
Those masted hulls, relieved of their cargo, become restless at the slightest hint of the wind's freedom. However tightly moored, they range a little at their berths, swaying imperceptibly the spire-like assemblages of cordage and spars. You can detect their impatience by watching the sway of the mastheads against the motionless, the soulless gravity of mortar and stones. As you pass alongside each hopeless prisoner chained to the quay, the slight grinding noise of the wooden fenders makes a sound of angry muttering.

===Chinese===
那些卸空了货物的有桅船体，一旦嗅到一丝风的自由气息，便会躁动不安。无论系得多紧，它们总会在泊位上小幅移动，使那尖塔般耸立的索具和帆桁微微摇晃。你只需观察桅顶相对于那静止的、毫无灵魂的砖石之庄严的摆动，便能察觉它们的不耐烦。当你从每一艘被锁在码头边的绝望囚徒身旁经过时，木质护舷轻微的摩擦声，听起来像是一阵愤怒的低语。

===Original===
But, after all, it may be good for ships to go through a period of restraint and repose, as the restraint and self-communion of inactivity may be good for an unruly soul—not, indeed, that I mean to say that ships are unruly; on the contrary, they are faithful creatures, as so many men can testify. And faithfulness is a great restraint, the strongest bond laid upon the self-will of men and ships on this globe of land and sea.

===Chinese===
然而说到底，让船经历一段约束和休憩也许是有益的，正如不作为的约束与自省对于一个桀骜的灵魂也许是好的——当然，我并不是说船是桀骜的；恰恰相反，它们是忠诚的造物，无数人都可以为它们作证。而忠诚本身就是一种巨大的约束，是施加于陆地与海洋这颗星球上人与船之自我意志的最强纽带。

===Original===
This interval of bondage in the docks rounds each period of a ship's life with the sense of accomplished duty, of an effectively played part in the work of the world. The dock is the scene of what the world would think the most serious part in the light, bounding, swaying life of a ship. But there are docks and docks. The ugliness of some docks is appalling.

===Chinese===
在码头里的这段羁绊，为船的每一段生命画上了句号，带来一种尽职尽责之感，一种在世间劳作中扮演了有效角色的满足。码头，在世人看来，是船那轻盈、弹跳、摇曳的生命中最严肃一幕的场景。但码头也分三六九等。有些码头的丑陋触目惊心。

===Original===
Wild horses would not drag from me the name of a certain river in the north whose narrow estuary is inhospitable and dangerous, and whose docks are like a nightmare of dreariness and misery. Their dismal shores are studded thickly with scaffold-like, enormous timber structures, whose lofty heads are veiled periodically by the infernal gritty night of a cloud of coal-dust. The most important ingredient for getting the world's work along is distributed there under the circumstances of the greatest cruelty meted out to helpless ships. Shut up in the desolate circuit of these basins, you would think a free ship would droop and die like a wild bird put into a dirty cage.

===Chinese===
野马也休想从我口中套出北方某条河流的名字——它那狭窄的河口荒凉而危险，它的码头简直是凄凉与苦难的噩梦。它们那阴沉的岸边密集地矗立着脚手架般的巨大木构建筑，高耸的顶端不时被煤尘云团遮蔽——那是地狱般充满沙砾的黑夜。推动世间运转的最重要原料，就在这种对无助船只施以最残酷待遇的环境中被分发。被囚禁在这些水池的荒凉围合中，你会以为一条自由的船会像一只被关进脏笼子的野鸟般枯萎而死。

===Original===
But a ship, perhaps because of her faithfulness to men, will endure an extraordinary lot of ill-usage. Still, I have seen ships issue from certain docks like half-dead prisoners from a dungeon, bedraggled, overcome, wholly disguised in dirt, and with their men rolling white eyeballs in black and worried faces raised to a heaven which, in its smoky and soiled aspect, seemed to reflect the sordidness of the earth below.

===Chinese===
但船——也许是因为对人类的忠诚——能够忍受非同一般的虐待。不过，我确实见过船从某些码头驶出时，活像半死的囚犯从地牢中走出来：邋遢不堪、形容憔悴、全身裹满污垢，船上的水手们抬起焦虑的、被煤灰染黑的脸庞，向上翻着白色的眼球仰望天空——而那天空烟尘弥漫、污浊不堪，似乎映照着下方尘世的肮脏。

===Original===
One thing, however, may be said for the docks of the Port of London on both sides of the river: for all the complaints of their insufficient equipment, of their obsolete rules, of failure (they say) in the matter of quick despatch, no ship need ever issue from their gates in a half-fainting condition. London is a general cargo port, as is only proper for the greatest capital of the world to be. General cargo ports belong to the aristocracy of the earth's trading places, and in that aristocracy London, as it is its way, has a unique physiognomy.

===Chinese===
不过，关于泰晤士河两岸的伦敦港码头，有一点值得一提：尽管人们抱怨设备不足、规则过时、在快速装卸方面有所欠缺（据说如此），但没有一艘船需要从它们的大门驶出时处于半昏迷状态。伦敦是一座杂货港——这对于世界最伟大的首都来说恰如其分。杂货港属于地球贸易场所中的贵族阶层，而在那个贵族阶层中，伦敦一如既往地保持着自己独特的面貌。

===Original===
The absence of picturesqueness cannot be laid to the charge of the docks opening into the Thames. For all my unkind comparisons to swans and backyards, it cannot be denied that each dock or group of docks along the north side of the river has its own individual attractiveness. Beginning with the cosy little St. Katherine's Dock, lying overshadowed and black like a quiet pool amongst rocky crags, through the venerable and sympathetic London Docks, with not a single line of rails in the whole of their area and the aroma of spices lingering between its warehouses, with their far-famed wine-cellars—down through the interesting group of West India Docks, the fine docks at Blackwall, on past the Galleons Reach entrance of the Victoria and Albert Docks, right down to the vast gloom of the great basins in Tilbury, each of those places of restraint for ships has its own peculiar physiognomy, its own expression. And what makes them unique and attractive is their common trait of being romantic in their usefulness.

===Chinese===
缺乏如画之美，这罪名可扣不到泰晤士河沿岸的码头头上。尽管我把它们比作天鹅和后院，但不可否认，河北岸的每一座或每一组码头都有其独特的魅力。从温馨小巧的圣凯瑟琳码头（St. Katherine's Dock）开始——它幽暗如岩石峭壁间一汪静谧的池塘——到古老而富有同情心的伦敦码头（London Docks）——整个区域没有一寸铁轨，香料的芬芳在仓库之间缭绕，还有远近闻名的酒窖——再向下经过西印度码头（West India Docks）那引人入胜的一组、布莱克沃尔（Blackwall）的优良码头，掠过维多利亚与阿尔伯特码头（Victoria and Albert Docks）加利恩水道（Galleons Reach）的入口，直抵蒂尔伯里（Tilbury）巨型水池那深沉的幽暗——这些囚禁船只的地方，每一处都有自己独特的面貌、自己的表情。而使它们独一无二且迷人的，是它们共同的特质：在实用性中蕴含着浪漫。

===Original===
In their way they are as romantic as the river they serve is unlike all the other commercial streams of the world. The cosiness of the St. Katherine's Dock, the old-world air of the London Docks, remain impressed upon the memory. The docks down the river, abreast of Woolwich, are imposing by their proportions and the vast scale of the ugliness that forms their surroundings—ugliness so picturesque as to become a delight to the eye. When one talks of the Thames docks, "beauty" is a vain word, but romance has lived too long upon this river not to have thrown a mantle of glamour upon its banks.

===Chinese===
在各自的方式上，它们与它们所服务的河流一样浪漫——而那条河不同于世界上所有其他商业河流。圣凯瑟琳码头的温馨、伦敦码头的旧日风情，都深深印刻在记忆中。河下游伍利奇（Woolwich）对面的码头，以其宏大的规模和周围丑陋景观的磅礴气势而令人肃然起敬——那种丑陋如此富有画面感，反倒成了赏心悦目之事。当人们谈论泰晤士河的码头时，"美"是一个空洞的词，但浪漫在这条河上住了太久，早已将一层魅力的斗篷披在了它的两岸。

===Original===
The antiquity of the port appeals to the imagination by the long chain of adventurous enterprises that had their inception in the town and floated out into the world on the waters of the river. Even the newest of the docks, the Tilbury Dock, shares in the glamour conferred by historical associations. Queen Elizabeth has made one of her progresses down there, not one of her journeys of pomp and ceremony, but an anxious business progress at a crisis of national history. The menace of that time has passed away, and now Tilbury is known by its docks. These are very modern, but their remoteness and isolation upon the Essex marsh, the days of failure attending their creation, invested them with a romantic air.

===Chinese===
这座港口的古老，通过一长串始于此城、乘着河水漂向世界的冒险事业，激发着人们的想象力。即使是最新的码头——蒂尔伯特码头（Tilbury Dock）——也分享着历史联想所赋予的魅力。伊丽莎白女王曾驾临此地，不是她那些盛装铺张的巡游，而是在国家历史危急关头一次焦灼的公务之行。那个时代的威胁早已消散，如今蒂尔伯里以其码头闻名。这些码头非常现代，但它们在埃塞克斯（Essex）沼泽地上的偏远与孤立，建造过程中那些失败的日子，赋予了它们一种浪漫的气息。

===Original===
Nothing in those days could have been more striking than the vast, empty basins, surrounded by miles of bare quays and the ranges of cargo-sheds, where two or three ships seemed lost like bewitched children in a forest of gaunt, hydraulic cranes. One received a wonderful impression of utter abandonment, of wasted efficiency. From the first the Tilbury Docks were very efficient and ready for their task, but they had come, perhaps, too soon into the field. A great future lies before Tilbury Docks. They shall never fill a long-felt want (in the sacramental phrase that is applied to railways, tunnels, newspapers, and new editions of books). They were too early in the field. The want shall never be felt because, free of the trammels of the tide, easy of access, magnificent and desolate, they are already there, prepared to take and keep the biggest ships that float upon the sea. They are worthy of the oldest river port in the world.

===Chinese===
在那些日子里，没有什么比这些巨大的、空荡荡的水池更令人震撼的了——它们被数英里的空旷码头和一排排货棚环绕着，两三艘船仿佛迷失的孩子，在一片瘦骨嶙峋的液压起重机的森林中不知所措。人会获得一种彻底荒废、效率被虚耗的奇异印象。蒂尔伯特码头从一开始就非常高效、准备就绪，但也许它们进入这个领域太早了。蒂尔伯特码头有着伟大的未来。它们永远不会填上一个"由来已久的需要"（这是人们用来形容铁路、隧道、报纸和书籍新版的那句庄严用语）。它们进场太早了。这个需要永远不会被感受到，因为它们不受潮汐的羁绊、进出便利、宏伟而荒凉，已经在那里了，准备承接并留住漂浮在海上的最大型船只。它们配得上世界上最古老的河港。

===Original===
And truth to say, for all the criticisms flung upon the heads of the dock companies, the other docks of the Thames are no disgrace to the town with a population greater than that of some commonwealths. The growth of London as a well-equipped port has been slow, while not unworthy of a great capital, of a great centre of distribution. It must not be forgotten that London has not the backing of great industrial districts or great fields of natural exploitation. In this it differs from Liverpool, from Cardiff, from Newcastle, from Glasgow; and therein the Thames differs from the Mersey, from the Tyne, from the Clyde. It is an historical river; it is a romantic stream flowing through the centre of great affairs, and for all the criticism of the river's administration, my contention is that its development has been worthy of its dignity.

===Chinese===
说句公道话，尽管人们把批评的矛头对准了码头公司，但泰晤士河的其他码头并没有给这座人口超过某些联邦国家的城市丢脸。伦敦作为一个设备完善的港口，其成长是缓慢的，但对于一个伟大的首都、一个伟大的分销中心来说，并不算辱没身份。不可忘记的是，伦敦背后没有大工业区或大片天然资源的支撑。在这一点上，它不同于利物浦、加的夫、纽卡斯尔和格拉斯哥；泰晤士河也因此有别于默西河（Mersey）、泰恩河（Tyne）和克莱德河（Clyde）。它是一条历史之河；它是一条浪漫的溪流，流经伟大事业的核心。尽管人们对这条河的管理有种种批评，但我坚持认为，它的发展与其尊严相称。

===Original===
For a long time the stream itself could accommodate quite easily the oversea and coasting traffic. That was in the days when, in the part called the Pool, just below London Bridge, the vessels moored stem and stern in the very strength of the tide formed one solid mass like an island covered with a forest of gaunt, leafless trees; and when the trade had grown too big for the river there came the St. Katherine's Docks and the London Docks, magnificent undertakings answering to the need of their time. The same may be said of the other artificial lakes full of ships that go in and out upon this high road to all parts of the world. The labour of the imperial waterway goes on from generation to generation, goes on day and night. Nothing ever arrests its sleepless industry but the coming of a heavy fog, which clothes the teeming stream in a mantle of impenetrable stillness.

===Chinese===
很长一段时间里，河水本身就能轻松容纳远洋和沿岸贸易。那是在所谓的池塘区（the Pool）——伦敦桥正下方——船只首尾相连、在潮汐最强处锚泊的日子，它们聚成一个坚实的整体，像一座覆盖着瘦骨嶙峋、光秃秃的树林的岛屿。当贸易增长到河流无法承载时，圣凯瑟琳码头和伦敦码头便应运而生——那些宏伟的工程回应了它们那个时代的需求。泰晤士河上其他那些满载着船只、在这条通往世界各地的通衢大道上进出往还的人工湖泊，也可以说同样如此。这条帝国水道的劳作代代相传，日日夜夜不曾停歇。唯有大雾降临才能让它的不眠劳作暂停——雾气为这条繁忙的河流披上一袭密不透风的寂静斗篷。

===Original===
After the gradual cessation of all sound and movement on the faithful river, only the ringing of ships' bells is heard, mysterious and muffled in the white vapour from London Bridge right down to the Nore, for miles and miles in a decrescendo tinkling, to where the estuary broadens out into the North Sea, and the anchored ships lie scattered thinly in the shrouded channels between the sandbanks of the Thames' mouth. Through the long and glorious tale of years of the river's strenuous service to its people these are its only breathing times.

===Chinese===
当这条忠诚的河流上所有的声响和活动渐渐止息之后，唯一能听见的，是船钟的鸣响——从伦敦桥一路到诺尔（the Nore），在白茫茫的雾气中神秘而沉闷，一英里又一英里地渐渐减弱，叮叮当当，直到河口开阔处汇入北海（the North Sea），锚泊的船只稀稀落落地散布在泰晤士河口沙洲之间被遮蔽的航道上。在这条河流为它的人民提供漫长而辉煌的服务的岁月里，这些便是它唯一的喘息时光。

## In Captivity / 囚困之中

### XXXIII / 第三十三篇

===Original===
A ship in dock, surrounded by quays and the walls of warehouses, has the appearance of a prisoner meditating upon freedom in the sadness of a free spirit put under restraint. Chain cables and stout ropes keep her bound to stone posts at the edge of a paved shore, and a berthing-master, with brass buttons on his coat, walks about like a weather-beaten and ruddy gaoler, casting jealous, watchful glances upon the moorings that fetter a ship lying passive and still and safe, as if lost in deep regrets of her days of liberty and danger on the sea.

===Chinese===
一艘停泊在船坞里的船，四周是码头和仓库的围墙，看上去像一个被束缚的自由灵魂，在忧伤中冥想着自由。锚链和粗缆将她牢牢系在铺砌岸壁边缘的石桩上，一个衣襟上钉着黄铜纽扣的带缆工（berthing-master），像个风吹日晒、面色红润的狱卒一样踱来踱去，用嫉妒而警觉的目光审视着那些束缚住这条船的系泊缆——她被动地、静止地、安全地躺着，仿佛深深陷入对她往昔自由与海上历险的追悔之中。

===Original===
The swarm of renegades—dockmasters, berthing-masters, gatemen, and suchlike—appear to nurse an immense distrust of the captive ship's resignation. There never seem chains and ropes enough to satisfy their minds concerned with the safe binding of free ships to the strong, muddy, enslaved earth. "You had better put another bight of a hawser astern, Mr. Mate," is the usual phrase in their mouth. I brand them for renegades, because most of them have been sailors in their time. As if the infirmities of old age—the grey hair, the wrinkles at the corners of the eyes, and the knotted veins of the hands—were the symptoms of moral poison, they prowl about the quays with an underhand air of gloating over the broken spirit of noble captives.

===Chinese===
那群叛徒——船坞长、带缆工、门卫之流——似乎对被囚之船的顺从心怀莫大的不信任。他们总觉得铁链和缆绳还不够多，不够满足他们要把自由的船只牢牢绑缚在坚实、泥泞、被奴役的大地上的念头。"大副先生，您最好在船尾再加一道粗缆圈，"是他们嘴里的口头禅。我称他们为叛徒，因为他们中的大多数当年都是水手。仿佛衰老的征兆——灰白的头发、眼角的皱纹、手上暴突的青筋——是道德中毒的症状，他们在码头上四处游荡，带着一种幸灾乐祸的阴郁气，觊觎着高贵囚徒那已被折服的灵魂。

===Original===
They want more fenders, more breasting-ropes; they want more springs, more shackles, more fetters; they want to make ships with volatile souls as motionless as square blocks of stone. They stand on the mud of pavements, these degraded sea-dogs, with long lines of railway-trucks clanking their couplings behind their backs, and run malevolent glances over your ship from headgear to taffrail, only wishing to tyrannize over the poor creature under the hypocritical cloak of benevolence and care.

===Chinese===
他们要更多的碰垫、更多的护舷缆；他们要更多的弹簧缆、更多的锁链、更多的脚镣；他们要把那些灵魂不安分的船变成纹丝不动的方石块。这些堕落的老水手站在泥泞的铺砌地面上，身后是哐当作响的铁路货车长龙，用恶意的目光从船首到船尾扫视你的船，一心只想披着仁慈关怀的伪善外衣，对这可怜的生灵施行暴政。

===Original===
Here and there cargo cranes looking like instruments of torture for ships swing cruel hooks at the end of long chains. Gangs of dock-labourers swarm with muddy feet over the gangways. It is a moving sight this, of so many men of the earth, earthy, who never cared anything for a ship, trampling unconcerned, brutal and hobnailed upon her helpless body.

===Chinese===
各处可见货运起重机——看上去像专门折磨船只的刑具——在长链的末端晃荡着残酷的吊钩。成群的码头工人，泥脚踩在舷梯上蜂拥而过。这是一幅令人动容的景象：如此众多尘世之人，满身尘土，对船从无半分关切，却毫不在意地、粗暴地、穿着钉靴践踏在她无助的躯体之上。

===Original===
Fortunately, nothing can deface the beauty of a ship. That sense of a dungeon, that sense of a horrible and degrading misfortune overtaking a creature fair to see and safe to trust, attaches only to ships moored in the docks of great European ports. You feel that they are dishonestly locked up, to be hunted about from wharf to wharf on a dark, greasy, square pool of black water as a brutal reward at the end of a faithful voyage.

===Chinese===
所幸，没有什么能够损毁一艘船的美丽。那种地牢的感觉，那种可怕的、有辱尊严的不幸降临在一个外观美丽、值得信赖的生灵身上的感觉，只属于那些停泊在欧洲大港船坞里的船。你会觉得它们是被不公正地锁起来的，在一方油腻发黑的方形水塘里被从一个码头驱赶到另一个码头，作为对一次忠诚航行的残酷奖赏。

===Original===
A ship anchored in an open roadstead, with cargo-lighters alongside and her own tackle swinging the burden over the rail, is accomplishing in freedom a function of her life. There is no restraint; there is space: clear water around her, and a clear sky above her mastheads, with a landscape of green hills and charming bays opening around her anchorage. She is not abandoned by her own men to the tender mercies of shore people. She still shelters, and is looked after by her own little devoted band, and you feel that presently she will glide between the headlands and disappear.

===Chinese===
一艘在开阔锚地抛锚的船，身旁紧靠着货驳（cargo-lighter），用自己的吊索将货物从舷侧吊起，是在自由中完成她生命的一项功能。没有束缚；有的是空间：四周是清澈的海水，桅顶上方是明净的天空，锚地周围展开着青山与迷人海湾的风景。她没有被自己的人抛弃，交给岸上人的"温柔"处置。她仍然有庇护，仍然由她自己那支忠心耿耿的小队伍照看着，你会觉得她不久便会滑过两座海岬之间，消失在远方。

===Original===
It is only at home, in dock, that she lies abandoned, shut off from freedom by all the artifices of men that think of quick despatch and profitable freights. It is only then that the odious, rectangular shadows of walls and roofs fall upon her decks, with showers of soot.

===Chinese===
只有在故乡——在船坞里——她才被遗弃，被那些只想着快速装卸和有利可图的运费的人们所设计的一切机关，隔绝于自由之外。也只有在那时，那些可憎的、方形的墙壁与屋顶的阴影才会投落在她的甲板上，伴随着阵阵煤灰飘落。

===Original===
To a man who has never seen the extraordinary nobility, strength, and grace that the devoted generations of shipbuilders have evolved from some pure nooks of their simple souls, the sight that could be seen five-and-twenty years ago of a large fleet of clippers moored along the north side of the New South Dock was an inspiring spectacle. Then there was a quarter of a mile of them, from the iron dockyard-gates guarded by policemen, in a long, forest-like perspective of masts, moored two and two to many stout wooden jetties. Their spars dwarfed with their loftiness the corrugated-iron sheds, their jibbooms extended far over the shore, their white-and-gold figureheads, almost dazzling in their purity, overhung the straight, long quay above the mud and dirt of the wharfside, with the busy figures of groups and single men moving to and fro, restless and grimy under their soaring immobility.

===Chinese===
对于一个从未见过世代献身的造船匠从他们淳朴灵魂的某个纯净角落里锤炼出的那种非凡高贵、力量与优雅的人来说，二十五年前在新南码头（New South Dock）北侧所见的那支庞大的快帆船（clipper）船队，是一幅令人振奋的景象。从警察守卫的铁质船坞大门望去，足足有四分之一英里的船只，在一道森林般绵延的桅杆透视线中，两两系泊在许多坚固的木质突堤码头上。它们高耸的桅桁使波纹铁皮棚屋相形见绌，它们的艏斜桅（jibboom）远远伸向岸上，它们白底描金的船首像——纯净得几乎令人目眩——悬在码头边上泥泞污垢之上的笔直长堤上方，忙碌的人影三五成群或孑然独行，在它们高耸的静止之下穿梭不息，满身污垢。

===Original===
At tide-time you would see one of the loaded ships with battened-down hatches drop out of the ranks and float in the clear space of the dock, held by lines dark and slender, like the first threads of a spider's web, extending from her bows and her quarters to the mooring-posts on shore. There, graceful and still, like a bird ready to spread its wings, she waited till, at the opening of the gates, a tug or two would hurry in noisily, hovering round her with an air of fuss and solicitude, and take her out into the river, tending, shepherding her through open bridges, through dam-like gates between the flat pier-heads, with a bit of green lawn surrounded by gravel and a white signal-mast with yard and gaff, flying a couple of dingy blue, red, or white flags.

===Chinese===
涨潮时分，你会看到一艘满载的、舱盖压条封紧的船从队列中悄然脱离，漂浮在船坞的开阔水域中，被几根细长黝黑的缆绳牵引着——如同蛛网最初的几根丝线——从船首和船尾延伸到岸上的系泊柱。在那里，她优雅而静止，像一只准备展翅的鸟儿，等待着大门开启，一两艘拖轮喧闹地驶入，带着大惊小怪和殷勤关切的样子在她身边盘旋，将她领入河中，护送她穿过开启的桥梁，穿过平坦码头之间的坝式闸门——闸门旁是一小片砾石环绕的绿地，一根白色的信号桅杆上挂着桁和斜桁（gaff），飘着两三面灰暗的蓝旗、红旗或白旗。

===Original===
This New South Dock (it was its official name), round which my earlier professional memories are centred, belongs to the group of West India Docks, together with two smaller and much older basins called Import and Export respectively, both with the greatness of their trade departed from them already. Picturesque and clean as docks go, these twin basins spread side by side the dark lustre of their glassy water sparsely peopled by a few ships laid up on buoys or tucked far away from each other at the end of sheds in the corners of empty quays, where they seemed to slumber quietly remote, untouched by the bustle of men's affairs—in retreat rather than in captivity.

===Chinese===
这新南码头（New South Dock）——它的正式名称——是我早年职业记忆的核心所在，属于西印度码头（West India Docks）群，另外还有两个更小、更古老的船坞，分别叫作进口码头和出口码头，两者的贸易辉煌都已成往事。作为船坞来说，这两座孪生的池塘算得上风景如画、干净整洁，并排铺展着幽暗而如镜的水面，零星停泊着几条系泊在浮筒上的船，或者远远地藏在空旷码头角落的棚屋尽头，彼此相隔甚远，仿佛在远离尘嚣的静谧中沉睡——与其说是囚禁，不如说是隐遁。

===Original===
They were quaint and sympathetic, those two homely basins, unfurnished and silent, with no aggressive display of cranes, no apparatus of hurry and work on their narrow shores. No railway-lines cumbered them. The knots of labourers trooping in clumsily round the corners of cargo-sheds to eat their food in peace out of red cotton handkerchiefs had the air of picnicking by the side of a lonely mountain pool. They were restful (and I should say very unprofitable), those basins, where the chief officer of one of the ships involved in the harassing, strenuous, noisy activity of the New South Dock only a few yards away could escape in the dinner-hour to stroll, unhampered by men and affairs, meditating (if he chose) on the vanity of all things human.

===Chinese===
那两座质朴的池塘是古雅而令人亲近的，没有任何摆设，寂静无声，没有咄咄逼人的起重机阵列，狭窄的岸上没有忙碌工作的器械。没有铁路线在那里盘踞。几群工人笨拙地绕过货棚的角落，在那里安安静静地从红色棉手帕里取出食物来吃，那样子像是在一座孤寂的山塘边野餐。那些池塘是令人安心的（我得说也非常无利可图），在那儿，新南码头那令人疲惫、繁重、喧闹的活动就在几码之外，而某条船的大副可以在午饭时间逃到这里来，不受人事干扰地散步，（如果他愿意的话）冥想人世间一切事物的虚妄。

===Original===
At one time they must have been full of good old slow West Indiamen of the square-stern type, that took their captivity, one imagines, as stolidly as they had faced the buffeting of the waves with their blunt, honest bows, and disgorged sugar, rum, molasses, coffee, or logwood sedately with their own winch and tackle. But when I knew them, of exports there was never a sign that one could detect; and all the imports I have ever seen were some rare cargoes of tropical timber, enormous baulks roughed out of iron trunks grown in the woods about the Gulf of Mexico. They lay piled up in stacks of mighty boles, and it was hard to believe that all this mass of dead and stripped trees had come out of the flanks of a slender, innocent-looking little barque with, as likely as not, a homely woman's name—Ellen this or Annie that—upon her fine bows. But this is generally the case with a discharged cargo. Once spread at large over the quay, it looks the most impossible bulk to have all come there out of that ship alongside.

===Chinese===
曾几何时，这里一定停满了那些缓慢老实的方尾西印度商船（West Indiamen），它们面对囚禁想必和它们当年以钝直诚实的船首迎击浪涛一样木然，用自带的绞盘和索具从容不迫地卸下蔗糖、朗姆酒、糖蜜、咖啡或苏木。但当我认识它们的时候，出口货物已经连一点迹象都看不到了；而我所见过的进口货物，不过是几批稀有的热带木材——从墨西哥湾周围森林中铁树干上粗粗劈出的巨大方木。它们堆叠在一起，原木粗壮如柱，令人难以相信，这整堆死去了的、剥了皮的树木，竟全是从一条外观纤弱无辜的小三桅帆船（barque）的船腹里吐出来的——那船上很可能还漆着某个家常女人的名字——这个艾伦、那个安妮。但这对卸下的货物来说是常态。一旦在码头上铺展开来，看上去就完全不可能是从旁边那条船里出来的了。

===Original===
They were quiet, serene nooks in the busy world of docks, these basins where it has never been my good luck to get a berth after some more or less arduous passage. But one could see at a glance that men and ships were never hustled there. They were so quiet that, remembering them well, one comes to doubt that they ever existed—places of repose for tired ships to dream in, places of meditation rather than work, where wicked ships—the cranky, the lazy, the wet, the bad sea boats, the wild steerers, the capricious, the pigheaded, the generally ungovernable—would have full leisure to take count and repent of their sins, sorrowful and naked, with their rent garments of sailcloth stripped off them, and with the dust and ashes of the London atmosphere upon their mastheads. For that the worst of ships would repent if she were ever given time I make no doubt. I have known too many of them. No ship is wholly bad; and now that their bodies that had braved so many tempests have been blown off the face of the sea by a puff of steam, the evil and the good together into the limbo of things that have served their time, there can be no harm in affirming that in these vanished generations of willing servants there never has been one utterly unredeemable soul.

===Chinese===
在繁忙的船坞世界里，它们是安静、宁静的角落，这些池塘——我从未有过在某次或苦或累的航行之后有幸在这里泊位的运气。但一眼就能看出，人和船在那里从不受催逼。它们安静得如此彻底，以至于当你清晰地回忆起它们时，反而开始怀疑它们是否真的存在过——那是疲惫的船做梦的休憩之所，是冥想而非劳作的所在，在那里，那些邪恶的船——易倾覆的、懒惰的、漏水的、航海性能差的、难以操纵的、反复无常的、固执的、一般而言无法管束的——将有充分的闲暇来清点并忏悔它们的罪孽，悲伤而赤裸，身上撕裂的帆布衣裳已被剥去，桅顶上蒙着伦敦大气的灰尘与灰烬。我毫不怀疑，即使是最糟糕的船，只要给她时间，她也会忏悔。我认识的船太多了。没有一条船是全然邪恶的；而如今，它们那曾经历过无数风暴的躯体，已被一缕蒸汽吹离了海面，善与恶一同坠入了那些已尽其使命的事物的虚无之境，在此声明，这些消逝了的世代忠仆之中，从未有过一个全然无可救赎的灵魂。

===Original===
In the New South Dock there was certainly no time for remorse, introspection, repentance, or any phenomena of inner life either for the captive ships or for their officers. From six in the morning till six at night the hard labour of the prison-house, which rewards the valiance of ships that win the harbour, went on steadily, great slings of general cargo swinging over the rail, to drop plumb into the hatchways at the sign of the gangway-tender's hand. The New South Dock was especially a loading dock for the Colonies in those great (and last) days of smart wool-clippers, good to look at and—well—exciting to handle.

===Chinese===
在新南码头，无论是被囚的船还是它们的船员，确实都没有时间来反省、内省、忏悔或进行任何内心生活的现象。从早晨六点到夜里六点，监狱般的苦役——那是对赢得港口的船只英勇航行的奖赏——稳步进行，巨大的杂货吊索从舷侧摆过，随着舷梯看守的手势笔直落入舱口。在那些运羊毛快帆船（wool-clipper）的伟大（也是最后的）岁月里，新南码头尤其是一个为殖民地装货的码头，那些船看上去漂亮极了——嗯——驾驭起来也令人兴奋。

===Original===
Some of them were more fair to see than the others; many were (to put it mildly) somewhat overmasted; all were expected to make good passages; and of all that line of ships, whose rigging made a thick, enormous network against the sky, whose brasses flashed almost as far as the eye of the policeman at the gates could reach, there was hardly one that knew of any other port amongst all the ports on the wide earth but London and Sydney, or London and Melbourne, or London and Adelaide, perhaps with Hobart Town added for those of smaller tonnage. One could almost have believed, as her grey-whiskered second mate used to say of the old *Duke of S——*, that they knew the road to the Antipodes better than their own skippers, who, year in, year out, took them from London—the place of captivity—to some Australian port where, twenty-five years ago, though moored well and tight enough to the wooden wharves, they felt themselves no captives, but honoured guests.

===Chinese===
其中有些比其他的更漂亮；许多——说得委婉些——桅杆略嫌过多；所有的船都被期望创造良好的航速；在那整排船中，索具在天空下织成浓密巨大的网络，黄铜配件的闪光几乎能被大门处警察的目光触及，而它们之中几乎没有一条知道广阔地球上除了伦敦和悉尼、或伦敦和墨尔本、或伦敦和阿德莱德之外还有什么别的港口——也许吨位较小的还会加上霍巴特（Hobart Town）。你几乎可以相信，正如那条老船"萨——公爵号"（Duke of S——）的灰胡子二副常说的那样，它们认识通往对跖地（Antipodes）的航路比它们自己的船长更清楚——那些船长年复一年地将它们从伦敦——囚禁之地——带到某个澳大利亚港口，而在二十五年前，虽然同样紧紧系泊在木质码头上，它们却觉得自己不是囚徒，而是尊贵的客人。

### XXXIV / 第三十四篇

===Original===
These towns of the Antipodes, not so great then as they are now, took an interest in the shipping, the running links with “home,” whose numbers confirmed the sense of their growing importance. They made it part and parcel of their daily interests. This was especially the case in Sydney, where, from the heart of the fair city, down the vista of important streets, could be seen the wool-clippers lying at the Circular Quay﻿—no walled prison-house of a dock that, but the integral part of one of the finest, most beautiful, vast, and safe bays the sun ever shone upon. Now great steam-liners lie at these berks, always reserved for the sea aristocracy﻿—grand and imposing enough ships, but here today and gone next week; whereas the general cargo, emigrant, and passenger clippers of my time, rigged with heavy spars, and built on fine lines, used to remain for months together waiting for their load of wool. Their names attained the dignity of household words. On Sundays and holidays the citizens trooped down, on visiting bent, and the lonely officer on duty solaced himself by playing the cicerone﻿—especially to the citizenesses with engaging manners and a well-developed sense of the fun that may be got out of the inspection of a ship’s cabins and staterooms. The tinkle of more or less untuned cottage pianos floated out of open stern-ports till the gas-lamps began to twinkle in the streets, and the ship’s night-watchman, coming sleepily on duty after his unsatisfactory day slumbers, hauled down the flags and fastened a lighted lantern at the break of the gangway.

===Chinese===
这些对跖地（Antipodes）的城镇，当时远不似今日这般宏伟，却对航运——那些与"家乡"的流动纽带——怀有浓厚兴趣，船舶的数量印证着它们日益增长的重要感。航运成了日常关切的一部分。在悉尼尤为如此，从这座美丽城市的中心，沿着重要街道的纵深望去，便能看见那些运羊毛快帆船（wool-clippers）停泊在圆形码头（Circular Quay）——那不是一道围困的牢狱，而是阳光照耀下最优良、最壮美、最辽阔、最安全的港湾的天然组成部分。如今，巨大的蒸汽邮轮停靠在这些泊位——它们总是为海上的贵族保留——船舰宏伟壮观，但今日在此，下周便不见踪影；而我那个时代的杂货、移民与客船快帆船，挂着厚重的帆桁，线条优美，往往一等就是几个月，等待装载羊毛。它们的名字获得了家喻户晓的尊严。在周日和节假日，市民们成群结队前来参观，而独自值勤的军官则以充当导游来自慰——尤其是对那些仪态迷人、深知参观船舶舱室与特等舱之乐趣的女市民们。或多或少走了调的家庭钢琴的叮咚声，从敞开的艉部舷窗飘出，直到街上的煤气灯开始闪烁，船上的夜间看守人睡眼惺忪地值勤——白日里他那不如人意的睡眠刚刚结束——降下旗帜，在舷梯的折角处挂上一盏点亮的提灯。

===Original===
The night closed rapidly upon the silent ships with their crews on shore. Up a short, steep ascent by the King’s Head pub., patronized by the cooks and stewards of the fleet, the voice of a man crying “Hot saveloys!” at the end of George Street, where the cheap eating-houses (sixpence a meal) were kept by Chinamen (Sun-kum-on’s was not bad), is heard at regular intervals. I have listened for hours to this most pertinacious pedlar (I wonder whether he is dead or has made a fortune), while sitting on the rail of the old *Duke of S﻿—﻿—﻿* (she’s dead, poor thing! a violent death on the coast of New Zealand), fascinated by the monotony, the regularity, the abruptness of the recurring cry, and so exasperated at the absurd spell, that I wished the fellow would choke himself to death with a mouthful of his own infamous wares.

===Chinese===
夜晚迅速笼罩了这些静默的船舶，船员们已上岸。沿着国王酒馆（King's Head pub.）旁一段陡峭的短坡——那酒馆深受船队厨师与管事们的光顾——在乔治街（George Street）尽头，一个男人叫卖"热萨维罗肠！"（Hot saveloys!）的声音，以规律的间隔传来，那里有许多廉价餐馆（一餐六便士），由中国移民经营（孙金安的（Sun-kum-on's）还不错）。我曾一连数小时聆听这位最执着的商贩（我纳闷他是否已死或已发了财）的叫卖声，当时我正坐在老旧的《S公爵号》（*Duke of S—*）的栏杆上（她已死去，可怜的东西！在新西兰海岸上死于非命），被那单调、规律、突兀而反复的叫喊声所迷住，又因这荒谬的魔咒而恼火，直希望那家伙被自己那些声名狼藉的货品噎死。

===Original===
A stupid job, and fit only for an old man, my comrades used to tell me, to be the night-watchman of a captive (though honoured) ship. And generally the oldest of the able seamen in a ship’s crew does get it. But sometimes neither the oldest nor any other fairly steady seaman is forthcoming. Ships’ crews had the trick of melting away swiftly in those days. So, probably on account of my youth, innocence, and pensive habits (which made me sometimes dilatory in my work about the rigging), I was suddenly nominated, in our chief mate Mr. B﻿—﻿—’s most sardonic tones, to that enviable situation. I do not regret the experience. The night humours of the town descended from the street to the waterside in the still watches of the night: larrikins rushing down in bands to settle some quarrel by a stand-up fight, away from the police, in an indistinct ring half hidden by piles of cargo, with the sounds of blows, a groan now and then, the stamping of feet, and the cry of “Time!” rising suddenly above the sinister and excited murmurs; night-prowlers, pursued or pursuing, with a stifled shriek followed by a profound silence, or slinking stealthily alongside like ghosts, and addressing me from the quay below in mysterious tones with incomprehensible propositions.

===Chinese===
"一份愚蠢的差事，只配老头子干，"我的同伴们过去常对我说，当一个被俘获（尽管光荣）的船舶的夜间看守人。通常，船上一等水手中最年长的那个确实会得到这职位。但有时，最年长的或其他任何相当稳健的水手都找不到。船员们在那些日子里有迅速消失的本事。所以，很可能因为我的年轻、天真和忧郁的习性（这有时让我在索具方面的工作有些拖拉），我突然被大副B先生（Mr. B—）以最刻薄的语调提名担任了这个令人羡慕的职位。我不后悔这段经历。城市的夜间风情在夜深人静的守望时刻从街道降临到水边：一群群街头混混（larrikins）为了某个争执，远离警察，在一堆堆货物半遮半掩的模糊圈子里，以一场面对面的打斗来了结，传来击打声、偶尔一声呻吟、跺脚声，以及"时间到！"（Time!）的喊声突然从险恶而激动的低语中升起；夜游者，或追逐或被追逐，伴随着一声压抑的尖叫，随后是深沉的寂静，或像幽灵般鬼鬼祟祟地沿船侧溜达，从下方的码头用神秘的语调向我提出令人费解的提议。

===Original===
The cabmen, too, who twice a week, on the night when the A.S.N. Company’s passenger-boat was due to arrive, used to range a battalion of blazing lamps opposite the ship, were very amusing in their way. They got down from their perches and told each other impolite stories in racy language, every word of which reached me distinctly over the bulwarks as I sat smoking on the main-hatch. On one occasion I had an hour or so of a most intellectual conversation with a person whom I could not see distinctly, a gentleman from England, he said, with a cultivated voice, I on deck and he on the quay sitting on the case of a piano (landed out of our hold that very afternoon), and smoking a cigar which smelt very good. We touched, in our discourse, upon science, politics, natural history, and operatic singers. Then, after remarking abruptly, “You seem to be rather intelligent, my man,” he informed me pointedly that his name was Mr. Senior, and walked off﻿—to his hotel, I suppose. Shadows! Shadows! I think I saw a white whisker as he turned under the lamppost. It is a shock to think that in the natural course of nature he must be dead by now. There was nothing to object to in his intelligence but a little dogmatism maybe. And his name was Senior! Mr. Senior!

===Chinese===
那些马车夫，每周两次，在A.S.N.公司（A.S.N. Company）的客轮预计抵达的夜晚，会在船对面排列起一队耀眼的灯火，他们自有其风趣。他们从座位上下来，用粗俗的语言互相讲不礼貌的故事，每一个字都清晰地越过舱壁传到我耳中——当时我正坐在主舱口（main-hatch）上抽烟。有一次，我与一位我无法看清的人进行了大约一个小时极富智性的交谈，他说他是一位来自英格兰的绅士，嗓音有教养，我在甲板上，他在码头上，坐在一架钢琴箱上（那钢琴正是当天下午从我们底舱卸下的），抽着一支气味极好的雪茄。在我们的谈话中，我们触及了科学、政治、自然史和歌剧演员。然后，他突兀地评论道："你似乎相当聪明，伙计，"并明确地告诉我他叫塞尼尔先生（Mr. Senior），然后走开了——我猜是去了他的旅馆。幻影！幻影！我觉得当他转过灯柱时，我瞥见了一撮白髭。想到他在自然的进程中此刻必然已经去世，令人震惊。他的才智无可非议，或许只有一点点武断。而他的名字叫塞尼尔（Senior）！塞尼尔先生（Mr. Senior）！

===Original===
The position had its drawbacks, however. One wintry, blustering, dark night in July, as I stood sleepily out of the rain under the break of the poop something resembling an ostrich dashed up the gangway. I say ostrich because the creature, though it ran on two legs, appeared to help its progress by working a pair of short wings; it was a man, however, only his coat, ripped up the back and flapping in two halves above his shoulders, gave him that weird and fowl-like appearance. At least, I suppose it was his coat, for it was impossible to make him out distinctly. How he managed to come so straight upon me, at speed and without a stumble over a strange deck, I cannot imagine. He must have been able to see in the dark better than any cat. He overwhelmed me with panting entreaties to let him take shelter till morning in our forecastle. Following my strict orders, I refused his request, mildly at first, in a sterner tone as he insisted with growing impudence.

===Chinese===
然而，这个职位也有其弊端。七月一个冬夜，风狂雨骤，天色昏暗，当我睡眼惺忪地站在艉楼（poop）的遮雨处时，一个类似鸵鸟的东西冲上了舷梯（gangway）。我说鸵鸟是因为那生物虽然用两条腿奔跑，却似乎靠扑腾一对短翅来助力前进；然而，那是一个人，只不过他的外套，从背后裂开，在肩膀上方两片翻飞，给了他那种怪异而禽类般的外表。至少，我猜是他的外套，因为根本无法看清他。他如何能在一条陌生的甲板上，如此径直、快速且不跌撞地冲到我面前，我无法想象。他必定能在黑暗中看得比任何猫都清楚。他气喘吁吁地恳求我，让我在我们的艏楼（forecastle）里躲到天亮。遵循我严格的命令，我拒绝了他的请求，起初温和，随着他越发无礼地坚持，语气也严厉起来。

===Original===
“For God’s sake let me, matey! Some of ’em are after me﻿—and I’ve got hold of a ticker here.”

===Chinese===
"看在上帝的份上让我进去吧，伙计！他们中有几个人在追我——而且我这里弄到了一块表。"（I’ve got hold of a ticker here.）

===Original===
“You clear out of this!” I said.

===Chinese===
"你给我离开这里！"我说。

===Original===
“Don’t be hard on a chap, old man!” he whined pitifully.

===Chinese===
"别对一个可怜人这么狠心，老兄！"他可怜巴巴地哀求道。

===Original===
“Now, then, get ashore at once. Do you hear?”

===Chinese===
"好了，立刻上岸去。听见了吗？"

===Original===
Silence. He appeared to cringe, mute, as if words had failed him through grief; then﻿—bang! came a concussion and a great flash of light in which he vanished, leaving me prone on my back with the most abominable black eye that anybody ever got in the faithful discharge of duty. Shadows! Shadows! I hope he escaped the enemies he was fleeing from to live and flourish to this day. But his fist was uncommonly hard and his aim miraculously true in the dark.

===Chinese===
一片沉寂。他似乎畏缩着，默不作声，仿佛因悲伤而说不出话来；接着——砰！一声撞击和一道强光闪过，他消失了，留下我仰面倒下，眼圈青肿得可怕——这是任何人忠于职守所能得到的最可憎的黑眼圈。幻影！幻影！我希望他逃脱了那些追捕他的敌人，活到今日，兴旺发达。但他的拳头异常坚硬，在黑暗中准头好得惊人。

===Original===
There were other experiences, less painful and more funny for the most part, with one amongst them of a dramatic complexion; but the greatest experience of them all was Mr. B﻿—﻿—, our chief mate himself.

===Chinese===
还有其他经历，大多不那么痛苦，也更有趣，其中有一次带有戏剧色彩；但所有经历中最伟大的，是我们的大副B先生（Mr. B—）本人。

===Original===
He used to go ashore every night to foregather in some hotel’s parlour with his crony, the mate of the barque *Cicero*, lying on the other side of the Circular Quay. Late at night I would hear from afar their stumbling footsteps and their voices raised in endless argument. The mate of the *Cicero* was seeing his friend on board. They would continue their senseless and muddled discourse in tones of profound friendship for half an hour or so at the shore end of our gangway, and then I would hear Mr. B﻿—﻿— insisting that he must see the other on board his ship. And away they would go, their voices, still conversing with excessive amity, being heard moving all round the harbour. It happened more than once that they would thus perambulate three or four times the distance, each seeing the other on board his ship out of pure and disinterested affection. Then, through sheer weariness, or perhaps in a moment of forgetfulness, they would manage to part from each other somehow, and by-and-by the planks of our long gangway would bend and creak under the weight of Mr. B﻿—﻿— coming on board for good at last.

===Chinese===
他每晚都会上岸，去某家酒店的客厅与他的老友——停泊在圆形码头（Circular Quay）另一边的三桅帆船（barque）《西塞罗号》（*Cicero*）的大副——聚会。深夜，我会从远处听到他们蹒跚的脚步声和提高嗓门进行的无尽争论。《西塞罗号》的大副正送他的朋友上船。他们会在我们舷梯的岸端，以极其深厚的友谊，继续他们那毫无意义且混乱的交谈大约半小时，然后我会听到B先生坚持一定要送对方上他的船。于是他们走远了，他们的声音——依然极其友好地交谈着——在港口周围回荡。不止一次，他们就这样绕行了三四次距离，每人出于纯粹而无私的情谊，将对方送上自己的船。然后，或许纯粹因为疲惫，或许是一时遗忘，他们设法分了手，不久之后，我们长长舷梯的木板会在B先生的重压下弯曲、嘎吱作响——他终于要永远上船了。

===Original===
On the rail his burly form would stop and stand swaying.

===Chinese===
在栏杆处，他那魁梧的身影会停下，摇摇晃晃地站着。

===Original===
“Watchman!”

===Chinese===
"看守人！"

===Original===
“Sir.”

===Chinese===
"先生。"

===Original===
A pause.

===Chinese===
一阵停顿。

===Original===
He waited for a moment of steadiness before negotiating the three steps of the inside ladder from rail to deck; and the watchman, taught by experience, would forbear offering help which would be received as an insult at that particular stage of the mate’s return. But many times I trembled for his neck. He was a heavy man.

===Chinese===
他等待着片刻的稳定，才去应付从栏杆到甲板的三步内梯；而看守人，因经验教训，会克制住不去提供帮助——在大副归来的这一特定阶段，帮助会被视为侮辱。但我许多次为他的脖子捏把汗。他是个沉重的人。

===Original===
Then with a rush and a thump it would be done. He never had to pick himself up; but it took him a minute or so to pull himself together after the descent.

===Chinese===
接着便是一阵急冲和一声闷响，他便下来了。他从不需要自己爬起来；但在下梯后，他需要大约一分钟才能缓过劲来。

===Original===
“Watchman!”

===Chinese===
"看守人！"

===Original===
“Sir.”

===Chinese===
"先生。"

===Original===
“Captain aboard?”

===Chinese===
"船长在船上吗？"

===Original===
“Yes, sir.”

===Chinese===
"在，先生。"

===Original===
Pause.

===Chinese===
停顿。

===Original===
“Dog aboard?”

===Chinese===
"狗在船上吗？"

===Original===
“Yes, sir.”

===Chinese===
"在，先生。"

===Original===
Pause.

===Chinese===
停顿。

===Original===
Our dog was a gaunt and unpleasant beast, more like a wolf in poor health than a dog, and I never noticed Mr. B﻿—﻿— at any other time show the slightest interest in the doings of the animal. But that question never failed.

===Chinese===
我们的狗是一只瘦削而不讨喜的畜生，更像一只病态的狼而非狗，我从未在其他任何时候注意到B先生对这只动物的行径表现出丝毫兴趣。但这个问题从未错过。

===Original===
“Let’s have your arm to steady me along.”

===Chinese===
"借你的胳膊扶我一把。"

===Original===
I was always prepared for that request. He leaned on me heavily till near enough the cabin-door to catch hold of the handle. Then he would let go my arm at once.

===Chinese===
我总是为这个请求做好准备。他重重地靠在我身上，直到足够靠近舱门能抓住门把手。然后他会立刻放开我的胳膊。

===Original===
“That’ll do. I can manage now.”

===Chinese===
"够了。我现在能行了。"

===Original===
And he could manage. He could manage to find his way into his berth, light his lamp, get into his bed﻿—ay, and get out of it when I called him at half-past five, the first man on deck, lifting the cup of morning coffee to his lips with a steady hand, ready for duty as though he had virtuously slept ten solid hours﻿—a better chief officer than many a man who had never tasted grog in his life. He could manage all that, but could never manage to get on in life.

===Chinese===
而他确实能行。他能设法找到自己的铺位，点亮灯，上床——是的，当我五点半叫他时，他也能起床，是甲板上的第一个人，用稳稳的手将晨间咖啡举到唇边，准备值勤，仿佛他已诚实地睡足了整整十个小时——比许多从未尝过烈酒的大副都要出色。他能设法做到这一切，却永远无法设法在人生中有所成就。

===Original===
Only once he failed to seize the cabin-door handle at the first grab. He waited a little, tried again, and again failed. His weight was growing heavier on my arm. He sighed slowly.

===Chinese===
只有一次，他第一把没抓住舱门把手。他等了一会儿，又试了一次，还是没抓住。他压在我胳膊上的分量越来越重。他慢慢地叹了口气。

===Original===
“D﻿—﻿—﻿n that handle!”

===Chinese===
"该死的那把手！"

===Original===
Without letting go his hold of me he turned about, his face lit up bright as day by the full moon.

===Chinese===
他没有放开抓着我的手，转过身去，他的脸被满月照得如同白昼般明亮。

===Original===
“I wish she were out at sea,” he growled savagely.

===Chinese===
"我愿她此刻在海上，"他凶狠地咆哮道。

===Original===
“Yes, sir.”

===Chinese===
"是，先生。"

===Original===
I felt the need to say something, because he hung on to me as if lost, breathing heavily.

===Chinese===
我感到有必要说点什么，因为他抓住我，仿佛迷失了方向，沉重地喘息着。

===Original===
“Ports are no good﻿—ships rot, men go to the devil!”

===Chinese===
"港口毫无用处——船会腐烂，人会堕落！"

===Original===
I kept still, and after a while he repeated with a sigh.

===Chinese===
我保持沉默，过了一会儿，他叹了口气又重复道。

===Original===
“I wish she were at sea out of this.”

===Chinese===
"我愿她在海上，离开这里。"

===Original===
“So do I, sir,” I ventured.

===Chinese===
"我也是，先生，"我鼓起勇气说。

===Original===
Holding my shoulder, he turned upon me.

===Chinese===
他抓住我的肩膀，转向我。

===Original===
“You! What’s that to you where she is? You don﻿—﻿—drink.”

===Chinese===
"你！她在哪里与你何干？你又不——喝酒。"

===Original===
And even on that night he “managed it” at last. He got hold of the handle. But he did not manage to light his lamp (I don’t think he even tried), though in the morning as usual he was the first on deck, bull-necked, curly-headed, watching the hands turn-to with his sardonic expression and unflinching gaze.

===Chinese===
而就在那个夜晚，他最终还是"设法做到了"。他抓住了门把手。但他没能设法点亮灯（我想他甚至没有尝试），不过早晨他照例是第一个上甲板的人，牛颈般粗壮，卷发，带着他那冷嘲的表情和坚定的目光，注视着船员们开始干活。

===Original===
I met him ten years afterwards, casually, unexpectedly, in the street, on coming out of my consignee office. I was not likely to have forgotten him with his “I can manage now.” He recognized me at once, remembered my name, and in what ship I had served under his orders. He looked me over from head to foot.

===Chinese===
十年后，我偶然地、出乎意料地在街上遇见了他，当时我刚从我的收货人办公室出来。我本不太可能忘记他，还有他那句"我现在能行了"。他立刻认出了我，记得我的名字，以及我在他麾下服役的那条船。他将我从头到脚打量了一番。

===Original===
“What are you doing here?” he asked.

===Chinese===
"你在这里做什么？"他问。

===Original===
“I am commanding a little barque,” I said, “loading here for Mauritius.” Then, thoughtlessly, I added: “And what are you doing, Mr. B-?”

===Chinese===
"我在指挥一条小三桅帆船（barque），"我说，"在这里装货去毛里求斯。"接着，我漫不经心地补充道："您在做什么呢，B先生？"

===Original===
“I,” he said, looking at me unflinchingly, with his old sardonic grin﻿—“I am looking for something to do.”

===Chinese===
"我，"他看着我，毫不退缩，带着他那旧时冷嘲的笑容——"我在找点事做。"

===Original===
I felt I would rather have bitten out my tongue. His jet-black, curly hair had turned iron-grey; he was scrupulously neat as ever, but frightfully threadbare. His shiny boots were worn down at heel. But he forgave me, and we drove off together in a hansom to dine on board my ship. He went over her conscientiously, praised her heartily, congratulated me on my command with absolute sincerity. At dinner, as I offered him wine and beer he shook his head, and as I sat looking at him interrogatively, muttered in an undertone:

===Chinese===
我感到我宁愿咬掉自己的舌头。他那乌黑的卷发已变成铁灰色；他一如既往地一丝不苟，却破旧得可怕。他闪亮的靴子后跟已磨损。但他原谅了我，我们一同乘一辆双轮马车离开，在我的船上用餐。他认真地检查了她，由衷地赞美她，以绝对的真诚祝贺我的指挥权。晚餐时，我给他斟酒和啤酒，他摇了摇头，当我坐在那里询问地看着他时，他低声咕哝道：

===Original===
“I’ve given up all that.”

===Chinese===
"我已经戒掉那些了。"

===Original===
After dinner we came again on deck. It seemed as though he could not tear himself away from the ship. We were fitting some new lower rigging, and he hung about, approving, suggesting, giving me advice in his old manner. Twice he addressed me as “My boy,” and corrected himself quickly to “Captain.” My mate was about to leave me (to get married), but I concealed the fact from Mr. B﻿—﻿—. I was afraid he would ask me to give him the berth in some ghastly jocular hint that I could not refuse to take. I was afraid. It would have been impossible. I could not have given orders to Mr. B﻿—﻿—, and I am sure he would not have taken them from me very long. He could not have managed that, though he had managed to break himself from drink﻿—too late.

===Chinese===
晚餐后我们再次来到甲板上。仿佛他无法离开这条船。我们正在安装一些新的下部索具，他四处闲逛，赞许着，提议着，以他旧时的方式给我建议。两次他称我为"我的孩子"（My boy），随即又迅速改口为"船长"（Captain）。我的大副即将离开我（去结婚），但我对B先生隐瞒了此事。我怕他会用某种可怕的玩笑暗示，要求我将那个职位给他，而我无法拒绝接受。我怕。那本是不可能的。我无法对B先生发号施令，而且我确信他也不会从我这里接受太久。他本无法设法做到这一点，尽管他已设法戒了酒——但为时已晚。

===Original===
He said goodbye at last. As I watched his burly, bull-necked figure walk away up the street, I wondered with a sinking heart whether he had much more than the price of a night’s lodging in his pocket. And I understood that if that very minute I were to call out after him, he would not even turn his head. He, too, is no more than a shadow, but I seem to hear his words spoken on the moonlit deck of the old *Duke of S﻿—﻿—﻿*:

===Chinese===
他终于道别了。当我望着他那魁梧、牛颈般的身影走上街头时，我心情沉重地揣测，他口袋里是否除了住一晚客栈的钱外，还有更多。而且我明白，如果那一刻我在他身后呼喊，他甚至不会回头。他也已不再是别的，只是一个影子，但我似乎能听到他的话语在古老《S公爵号》（*Duke of S—*）月光下的甲板上响起：

===Original===
“Ports are no good﻿—ships rot, men go to the devil!”

===Chinese===
"港口毫无用处——船会腐烂，人会堕落！"

## Initiation / 入门

### XXXV / 第三十五篇

===Original===
"Ships!" exclaimed an elderly seaman in clean shore togs. "Ships"—and his keen glance, turning away from my face, ran along the vista of magnificent figureheads that in the late seventies used to overhang in a serried rank the muddy pavement by the side of the New South Dock—"ships are all right; it's the men in 'em…"

===Chinese===
"船啊！"一位穿着整洁上岸服装的老水手喊道。"船啊"——他犀利的目光从我脸上移开，沿着那一排宏伟的船头雕饰望去——那些在七十年代末，曾在新南船坞旁泥泞的人行道上密密麻麻地排列着——"船都没问题；是船上的人啊……"

===Original===
Fifty hulls, at least, moulded on lines of beauty and speed—hulls of wood, of iron, expressing in their forms the highest achievement of modern shipbuilding—lay moored all in a row, stem to quay, as if assembled there for an exhibition, not of a great industry, but of a great art. Their colours were grey, black, dark green, with a narrow strip of yellow moulding defining their sheer, or with a row of painted ports decking in warlike decoration their robust flanks of cargo-carriers that would know no triumph but of speed in carrying a burden, no glory other than of a long service, no victory but that of an endless, obscure contest with the sea. The great empty hulls with swept holds, just out of dry-dock, with their paint glistening freshly, sat high-sided with ponderous dignity alongside the wooden jetties, looking more like unmovable buildings than things meant to go afloat; others, half loaded, far on the way to recover the true sea-physiognomy of a ship brought down to her load-line, looked more accessible. Their less steeply slanting gangways seemed to invite the strolling sailors in search of a berth to walk on board and try "for a chance" with the chief mate, the guardian of a ship's efficiency. As if anxious to remain unperceived amongst their overtopping sisters, two or three "finished" ships floated low, with an air of straining at the leash of their level headfasts, exposing to view their cleared decks and covered hatches, prepared to drop stern first out of the labouring ranks, displaying the true comeliness of form which only her proper sea-trim gives to a ship. And for a good quarter of a mile, from the dockyard gate to the farthest corner, where the old housed-in hulk, the *President* (drill-ship, then, of the Naval Reserve), used to lie with her frigate side rubbing against the stone of the quay, above all these hulls, ready and unready, a hundred and fifty lofty masts, more or less, held out the web of their rigging like an immense net, in whose close mesh, black against the sky, the heavy yards seemed to be entangled and suspended.

===Chinese===
至少有五十条船壳，依着美与速度的线条塑成——木质的、铁质的，其形制代表着现代造船术的最高成就——首尾相衔，整整齐齐地系泊在那里，仿佛被召集来举办一场展览，展示的不是一项伟大产业，而是一门伟大艺术。它们的色彩有灰、黑、深绿，一线窄窄的黄色饰条勾勒出舷弧，或者一排漆成仿炮眼的装饰，披挂在那些货运船坚实饱满的舷侧——这些船除了驮载重物的速度之外不知何为凯旋，除了长久服役之外不知何为荣耀，除了与大海永无休止的隐秘较量之外不知何为胜利。那些刚出干船坞、舱内清扫一空的巨大空壳，油漆闪亮如新，高高地侧坐在木质码头旁，带着沉甸甸的庄严，看上去更像不可移动的建筑而非将要浮起来的器物；另一些半装载的船，已经大体恢复了一条船被压到载重线时真正的海上面貌，看上去更亲近些。它们那坡度较缓的舷梯，似乎在邀请那些找差事的闲逛水手走上船去，跟大副——一位船舶效率的守护者——碰碰运气。仿佛急于在那些高过自己的姊妹中不被察觉，两三条"装修完毕"的船低低地浮着，一副挣着水平系缆的架势，露出光洁的甲板和盖好的舱口，随时准备从劳碌的队列中掉头先走，展示出唯有合宜的海上装具才能赋予一条船的真正端庄。从船坞大门到最远处的角落——那条旧的、带棚壳的"总统号"（*President*，当时的海军预备队练舰），护卫舰的一侧摩挲着码头的石壁——长达整整四分之一英里，在这一大片就绪与未就绪的船壳上方，大约一百五十根高高的桅杆，以它们的索具之网向外伸展，如同一张巨网，在那密密的网眼里——映着天空黑黢黢的——沉重的帆桁仿佛被缠住、悬在半空。

===Original===
It was a sight. The humblest craft that floats makes its appeal to a seaman by the faithfulness of her life; and this was the place where one beheld the aristocracy of ships. It was a noble gathering of the fairest and the swiftest, each bearing at the bow the carved emblem of her name, as in a gallery of plaster-casts, figures of women with mural crowns, women with flowing robes, with gold fillets on their hair or blue scarves round their waists, stretching out rounded arms as if to point the way; heads of men helmeted or bare; full lengths of warriors, of kings, of statesmen, of lords and princesses, all white from top to toe; with here and there a dusky turbaned figure, bedizened in many colours, of some Eastern sultan or hero, all inclined forward under the slant of mighty bowsprits as if eager to begin another run of 11,000 miles in their leaning attitudes. These were the fine figureheads of the finest ships afloat. But why, unless for the love of the life those effigies shared with us in their wandering impassivity, should one try to reproduce in words an impression of whose fidelity there can be no critic and no judge, since such an exhibition of the art of shipbuilding and the art of figurehead carving as was seen from year's end to year's end in the open-air gallery of the New South Dock no man's eye shall behold again? All that patient, pale company of queens and princesses, of kings and warriors, of allegorical women, of heroines and statesmen and heathen gods, crowned, helmeted, bareheaded, has run for good off the sea stretching to the last above the tumbling foam their fair, rounded arms; holding out their spears, swords, shields, tridents in the same unwearied, striving forward pose. And nothing remains but lingering perhaps in the memory of a few men, the sound of their names, vanished a long time ago from the first page of the great London dailies; from big posters in railway-stations and the doors of shipping offices; from the minds of sailors, dockmasters, pilots, and tugmen; from the hail of gruff voices and the flutter of signal flags exchanged between ships closing upon each other and drawing apart in the open immensity of the sea.

===Chinese===
那是一幅景致。最卑微的漂浮之物，都以它忠实的一生打动水手的心；而这里是瞻仰船舶贵族的地方。这是一场美好而迅捷的船舶的盛大集会，每一条船首都承载着它名字的浮雕徽记，如同一座石膏模型画廊：戴着城冠的女性、衣袂飘飘的女性，金发带缠在发间或蓝色腰带束于腰际，伸出圆润的手臂仿佛在指引方向；头戴钢盔或赤首的男子；全身像的武士、国王、政要、领主与公主，通体洁白；其间散落着几尊深肤色、包头巾的东方苏丹或英雄像，披挂着五彩斑斓的饰物，全都向前倾身，在巨大的艏斜桅下，仿佛迫不及待地以这种前倾的姿态再次启程一万一千英里。这些是世间最优秀的船舶上最精美的船首雕饰。但若不是出于对这些雕像与我们共同分享的那种漫游中的冷寂生命的热爱，为何要用文字去复现一种印象——它的忠实度既无批评家可评判，也无裁判者可裁决？因为新南船坞露天画廊中那绵延不绝的造船艺术与船首雕刻艺术的展览，人间再无人能目睹了。那一长列沉静、苍白的王后与公主、国王与武士、寓言中的女性、女英雄、政要与异教诸神，戴冠的、戴盔的、赤首的，已经永远驶离了大海，直到最后依然在翻滚的浪花上方伸出她们美丽圆润的手臂；举着矛、剑、盾、三叉戟，以那永不懈怠的向前奋力姿态。如今什么都不剩了——或许在少数几个人的记忆中还隐约飘着它们名字的声响；那些名字早已从伦敦大报的头版上消失，从火车站的大广告牌和航运公司的门口消失，从水手、船坞长、引航员和拖船工的脑海中消失，从船舶在浩瀚汪洋中彼此靠近又分开时那粗嗓门的呼喊与信号旗的飘动中消失了。

===Original===
The elderly, respectable seaman, withdrawing his gaze from that multitude of spars, gave me a glance to make sure of our fellowship in the craft and mystery of the sea. We had met casually, and had got into contact as I had stopped near him, my attention being caught by the same peculiarity he was looking at in the rigging of an obviously new ship, a ship with her reputation all to make yet in the talk of the seamen who were to share their life with her. Her name was already on their lips. I had heard it uttered between two thick, red-necked fellows of the semi-nautical type at the Fenchurch Street Railway-station, where, in those days, the everyday male crowd was attired in jerseys and pilot-cloth mostly, and had the air of being more conversant with the times of high-water than with the times of the trains. I had noticed that new ship's name on the first page of my morning paper. I had stared at the unfamiliar grouping of its letters, blue on white ground, on the advertisement-boards, whenever the train came to a standstill alongside one of the shabby, wooden, wharf-like platforms of the dock railway-line. She had been named, with proper observances, on the day she came off the stocks, no doubt, but she was very far yet from "having a name." Untried, ignorant of the ways of the sea, she had been thrust amongst that renowned company of ships to load for her maiden voyage. There was nothing to vouch for her soundness and the worth of her character, but the reputation of the building-yard whence she was launched headlong into the world of waters. She looked modest to me. I imagined her diffident, lying very quiet, with her side nestling shyly against the wharf to which she was made fast with very new lines, intimidated by the company of her tried and experienced sisters already familiar with all the violences of the ocean and the exacting love of men. They had had more long voyages to make their names in than she had known weeks of carefully tended life, for a new ship receives as much attention as if she were a young bride. Even crabbed old dockmasters look at her with benevolent eyes. In her shyness at the threshold of a laborious and uncertain life, where so much is expected of a ship, she could not have been better heartened and comforted, had she only been able to hear and understand, than by the tone of deep conviction in which my elderly, respectable seaman repeated the first part of his saying, "Ships are all right…"

===Chinese===
那位体面的老水手将目光从那密密的桅杆群上收回，瞥了我一眼，确认我们在航海这门行当与奥秘中是同道中人。我们偶然相遇，我恰好在他身旁停下，被他正看着的那条崭新船只索具上的某个特征吸引了注意——这条船在那些将要与她共度海上生涯的水手们的谈论中，名声还全然未立。但她的名字已经在他们嘴边了。我曾在芬丘奇街（Fenchurch Street）火车站听两个半海员类型的粗脖子汉子念叨过这个名字；那时候，每天那里的男性人群大多穿着针织衫和领港呢，看上去对涨潮时间比对火车时刻更了然于胸。我也在晨报头版上注意到了那条新船的名字。每当火车停靠在码头铁路线上那些破旧的、木头的、像栈桥一样的站台旁时，我总盯着广告牌上那陌生的字母组合——白底蓝字。她下水那天，无疑举行了正式的命名仪式，但离"有了名声"还差得远。未经试航、不谙海路，她被塞进了这群赫赫有名的船队中，准备装载她处女航的货物。没有任何东西能担保她的坚固和品性——除了她被一股脑儿抛入汪洋世界的那个造船厂的声誉。在我眼里她显得谦逊。我想象她怯生生的，静静地躺着，船身紧贴着码头，用崭新的缆绳系泊，在那些久经沙场、深谙海洋一切暴虐与人类苛求的姊妹面前心生畏葸。她们在扬名立万的漫长航行中所积累的，比她所知道的几个月精心呵护的生命还要多，因为一条新船受到的照料不亚于一位年轻的新娘。就连脾气古怪的老船坞长也用慈祥的目光打量她。在这劳碌而前途未卜的人生门槛上，她那羞怯的模样——在这条船上被寄予如此厚望的门槛上——若她能听见并理解，没有什么比那位体面的老水手以深信不疑的口吻重复他的前半句话更能给她鼓劲和安慰的了："船都没问题……"

===Original===
His civility prevented him from repeating the other, the bitter part. It had occurred to him that it was perhaps indelicate to insist. He had recognized in me a ship's officer, very possibly looking for a berth like himself, and so far a comrade, but still a man belonging to that sparsely-peopled after-end of a ship, where a great part of her reputation as a "good ship," in seaman's parlance, is made or marred.

===Chinese===
他的礼貌使他没有重复后半句——那句辛酸的话。他想到，再往下说或许有失分寸。他认出我是一名船上的高级船员，很可能也和他一样在找差事，在这个层面上是同道；但他仍然是船尾那个人员稀疏的部门的人——一条船作为"好船"的名声，在水手行话里，有很大一部分就是在那个部门成就或败坏的。

===Original===
"Can you say that of all ships without exception?" I asked, being in an idle mood, because, if an obvious ship's officer, I was not, as a matter of fact, down at the docks to "look for a berth," an occupation as engrossing as gambling, and as little favourable to the free exchange of ideas, besides being destructive of the kindly temper needed for casual intercourse with one's fellow-creatures.

===Chinese===
"你能说每条船都毫无例外吗？"我以一种闲散的心境问道。因为，虽然明摆着是一名船员，我实际上到船坞来并不是"找差事"——那是一种和赌博一样令人全神贯注、一样不利于思想自由交流，而且还损毁与同行随意交谈所需的温和性情的营生。

===Original===
"You can always put up with 'em," opined the respectable seaman judicially.

===Chinese===
"你总能将就，"那位体面的水手以法官般的口吻说道。

===Original===
He was not averse from talking, either. If he had come down to the dock to look for a berth, he did not seem oppressed by anxiety as to his chances. He had the serenity of a man whose estimable character is fortunately expressed by his personal appearance in an unobtrusive, yet convincing, manner which no chief officer in want of hands could resist. And, true enough, I learned presently that the mate of the *Hyperion* had "taken down" his name for quartermaster. "We sign on Friday, and join next day for the morning tide," he remarked, in a deliberate, careless tone, which contrasted strongly with his evident readiness to stand there yarning for an hour or so with an utter stranger.

===Chinese===
他也不排斥聊天。若他来船坞是找差事，他对自己的前景似乎并无焦虑之色。他身上有一种安宁，这种令人敬重的品格幸运地通过他的外表以一种不事张扬却令人信服的方式流露出来，任何缺人手的大副都抵挡不住。果然，我很快得知许珀里翁号（*Hyperion*）的大副已经"记下"了他的名字，录用为舵手长。"我们周五签合同，第二天一早就赶上早潮上船，"他用一种从容而随意的口吻说道，这与他显然愿意站在这里和一个素不相识的人聊上一小时的架势形成了强烈的反差。

===Original===
"*Hyperion*," I said. "I don't remember ever seeing that ship anywhere. What sort of a name has she got?"

===Chinese===
"许珀里翁号，"我说。"我不记得在哪儿见过这条船。她名声怎么样？"

===Original===
It appeared from his discursive answer that she had not much of a name one way or another. She was not very fast. It took no fool, though, to steer her straight, he believed. Some years ago he had seen her in Calcutta, and he remembered being told by somebody then, that on her passage up the river she had carried away both her hawsepipes. But that might have been the pilot's fault. Just now, yarning with the apprentices on board, he had heard that this very voyage, brought up in the Downs, outward bound, she broke her sheer, struck adrift, and lost an anchor and chain. But that might have occurred through want of careful tending in a tideway. All the same, this looked as though she were pretty hard on her ground-tackle. Didn't it? She seemed a heavy ship to handle, anyway. For the rest, as she had a new captain and a new mate this voyage, he understood, one couldn't say how she would turn out.…

===Chinese===
