从他散漫的回答中看来，她好也罢坏也罢，名声都不太响。她不太快。不过要直着操舵也不是傻瓜干得了的活儿，他相信。几年前他在加尔各答见过她，记得当时有人告诉他，她上行河段时两个锚链管都被扯掉了。但那也许是引航员的错。刚才跟船上的学徒聊天，他听说就在这一趟——在当斯锚地抛锚，外航途中——她断了系缆，漂了锚，丢了一只锚和一条锚链。但那也许是在潮汐航道上没有仔细看锚的缘故。话虽如此，这看起来她对自己的锚具可是够狠的，不是吗？不管怎么说，她看起来是一条不好操控的重船。至于其他，据说她这一趟换了新船长和新大副，谁也说不准她会怎么样。……

===Original===
In such marine shore-talk as this is the name of a ship slowly established, her fame made for her, the tale of her qualities and of her defects kept, her idiosyncrasies commented upon with the zest of personal gossip, her achievements made much of, her faults glossed over as things that, being without remedy in our imperfect world, should not be dwelt upon too much by men who, with the help of ships, wrest out a bitter living from the rough grasp of the sea. All that talk makes up her "name," which is handed over from one crew to another without bitterness, without animosity, with the indulgence of mutual dependence, and with the feeling of close association in the exercise of her perfections and in the danger of her defects.

===Chinese===
正是在这类岸上的海员闲谈中，一条船的名字慢慢立起来了，名声造出来了，她的品性与缺陷的故事传下来了，她的特性被兴致勃勃地当作私房话议论着，她的功绩被大加宣扬，她的缺点被轻描淡写地带过——因为在一个不完美的世界里无法弥补的事情，那些靠船舶的帮助从大海粗糙的手中苦挣苦熬的人，不应该纠缠太多。所有这些议论构成了她的"名声"，它被从一代船员传给下一代，没有怨恨、没有敌意，带着相互依存的宽容，以及在运用她的长处和承受她的缺陷时那种亲密的连带感。

===Original===
This feeling explains men's pride in ships. "Ships are all right," as my middle-aged, respectable quartermaster said with much conviction and some irony; but they are not exactly what men make them. They have their own nature; they can of themselves minister to our self-esteem by the demand their qualities make upon our skill and their shortcomings upon our hardiness and endurance. Which is the more flattering exaction it is hard to say; but there is the fact that in listening for upwards of twenty years to the sea-talk that goes on afloat and ashore I have never detected the true note of animosity. I won't deny that at sea, sometimes, the note of profanity was audible enough in those chiding interpellations a wet, cold, weary seaman addresses to his ship, and in moments of exasperation is disposed to extend to all ships that ever were launched—to the whole everlastingly exacting brood that swims in deep waters. And I have heard curses launched at the unstable element itself, whose fascination, outlasting the accumulated experience of ages, had captured him as it had captured the generations of his forbears.

===Chinese===
这种感情解释了人对船的自豪。"船都没问题，"我那位中年的、体面的舵手长带着深信和几分反讽说道；但船并不完全是人使它们成为的样子。它们有自己的天性；它们能凭自身的品性满足我们的自尊——其优点对我们的技艺提出要求，其缺陷对我们的坚韧和耐力提出要求。哪一种要求更令人受用，很难说得清；但有一个事实：在听了二十多年船上和岸上的海员闲谈后，我从未听出过真正的敌意。我不否认在海上，有时候，在那些湿冷疲惫的水手对他船的责骂式追问中，亵渎之语确实不绝于耳，而在恼怒的时刻，那种情绪还会蔓延到所有下过水的船——蔓延到那整群永远苛刻地要求着的、在深水中游弋的后代。我也听到过诅咒被投向那不稳定的元素本身——它的迷惑力跨越世代积累的经验，俘获了他，正如俘获了他的祖辈一样。

===Original===
For all that has been said of the love that certain natures (on shore) have professed to feel for it, for all the celebrations it had been the object of in prose and song, the sea has never been friendly to man. At most it has been the accomplice of human restlessness, and playing the part of dangerous abettor of worldwide ambitions. Faithful to no race after the manner of the kindly earth, receiving no impress from valour and toil and self-sacrifice, recognising no finality of dominion, the sea has never adopted the cause of its masters like those lands where the victorious nations of humanity have taken root, rocking their cradles and setting up their gravestones. He—man or people—who, putting his trust in the friendship of the sea, neglects the strength and cunning of his right hand, is a fool! As if it were too great, too mighty for common virtues, the ocean has no compassion, no faith, no law, no memory. Its fickleness is to be held true to men's purposes only by an undaunted resolution and by a sleepless, armed, jealous vigilance, in which, perhaps, there has always been more hate than love. *Odi et amo* may well be the confession of those who consciously or blindly have surrendered their existence to the fascination of the sea. All the tempestuous passions of mankind's young days, the love of loot and the love of glory, the love of adventure and the love of danger, with the great love of the unknown and vast dreams of dominion and power, have passed like images reflected from a mirror, leaving no record upon the mysterious face of the sea. Impenetrable and heartless, the sea has given nothing of itself to the suitors for its precarious favours. Unlike the earth, it cannot be subjugated at any cost of patience and toil. For all its fascination that has lured so many to a violent death, its immensity has never been loved as the mountains, the plains, the desert itself, have been loved. Indeed, I suspect that, leaving aside the protestations and tributes of writers who, one is safe in saying, care for little else in the world than the rhythm of their lines and the cadence of their phrase, the love of the sea, to which some men and nations confess so readily, is a complex sentiment wherein pride enters for much, necessity for not a little, and the love of ships—the untiring servants of our hopes and our self-esteem—for the best and most genuine part. For the hundreds who have reviled the sea, beginning with Shakespeare in the line—

===Chinese===
尽管有人（在岸上）宣称自己热爱大海，尽管它被散文和诗歌讴歌过无数次，大海从未对人友善过。它充其量不过是人类不安分的同谋，充当着世界野心的危险帮凶。它不像仁慈的大地那样忠于任何种族，不留下英勇、辛劳与自我牺牲的印记，不承认任何终极的统治权；它从未像那些胜利民族扎根的陆地那样，采纳它主人的事业——那些陆地摇着主人的摇篮，也竖着主人的墓碑。把信念寄托在大海的友谊上，却忽视了右手的力量与机巧的人——无论是个人还是民族——是个傻瓜！仿佛它太过伟大、太过强悍，容不下寻常的德行，大海没有怜悯、没有信仰、没有法度、没有记忆。它的反复无常只能靠不屈不挠的决心和不眠的、武装的、嫉妒般的警惕来加以约束，而在这警惕中，或许恨一直多于爱。*Odi et amo*——"我又恨又爱"——或许是那些自觉或盲目地将生命交托给大海魅力的人的坦白。人类青年时代所有激荡的激情——对掠物的爱、对荣光的爱、对冒险的爱、对危险的爱，连同对未知的深爱和对统治与权力的宏大梦想——都像镜中映出的影像一样掠过，没有在大海神秘的面庞上留下任何痕迹。不可穿透而无情的大海，没有给予那些觊觎它那靠不住的恩宠的追求者任何东西。不同于大地，它不能以任何耐心与辛劳的代价来征服。尽管它的魅力引诱了那么多人走向暴死，它的辽阔却从未像山峦、平原乃至沙漠本身那样被爱过。确实，我猜想——且不说那些作家的表白和颂词（可以放心地说，他们对世上其他东西的兴趣还不如对他们自己句子的韵律和措辞的节奏那么大）——那种人们和民族轻易便承认的对大海的爱，是一种复杂的感情，其中自尊占了很大成分，必要性占了不小成分，而对船舶——对我们希望与自尊的不倦仆人——的爱，则是最好的、最真实的部分。因为从莎士比亚的这行诗开始——

===Original===
> "More fell than hunger, anguish, or the sea."

===Chinese===
> "比饥饿、苦痛或大海更凶残。"

===Original===
down to the last obscure sea-dog of the "old model," having but few words and still fewer thoughts, there could not be found, I believe, one sailor who has ever coupled a curse with the good or bad name of a ship. If ever his profanity, provoked by the hardships of the sea, went so far as to touch his ship, it would be lightly, as a hand may, without sin, be laid in the way of kindness on a woman.

===Chinese===
直到最后一个寡言少语、更少思想的"旧式"老水手——我相信你找不到一个水手，会把诅咒和一条船的好名声或坏名声绑在一起说。即便他的亵渎之语——被大海的艰辛所激——达到了涉及他的船的地步，那也只是轻轻的，正如一只手可以毫无罪过地以怜爱的姿态搭在一个女人的肩上。

### XXXVI / 第三十六篇

===Original===
The love that is given to ships is profoundly different from the love men feel for every other work of their hands—the love they bear to their houses, for instance—because it is untainted by the pride of possession. The pride of skill, the pride of responsibility, the pride of endurance there may be, but otherwise it is a disinterested sentiment. No seaman ever cherished a ship, even if she belonged to him, merely because of the profit she put in his pocket. No one, I think, ever did; for a shipowner, even of the best, has always been outside the pale of that sentiment embracing in a feeling of intimate, equal fellowship the ship and the man, backing each other against the implacable, if sometimes dissembled, hostility of their world of waters. The sea—this truth must be confessed—has no generosity. No display of manly qualities—courage, hardihood, endurance, faithfulness—has ever been known to touch its irresponsible consciousness of power. The ocean has the conscienceless temper of a savage autocrat spoiled by much adulation. He cannot brook the slightest appearance of defiance, and has remained the irreconcilable enemy of ships and men ever since ships and men had the unheard of audacity to go afloat together in the face of his frown. From that day he has gone on swallowing up fleets and men without his resentment being glutted by the number of victims—by so many wrecked ships and wrecked lives. Today, as ever, he is ready to beguile and betray, to smash and to drown the incorrigible optimism of men who, backed by the fidelity of ships, are trying to wrest from him the fortune of their house, the dominion of their world, or only a dole of food for their hunger. If not always in the hot mood to smash, he is always stealthily ready for a drowning. The most amazing wonder of the deep is its unfathomable cruelty.

===Chinese===
人们对船只所怀的爱，与人们对自己双手所造之物——比如房屋——所怀的爱，有着深刻的差异，因为它不沾染占有的骄傲。技艺的骄傲、责任的骄傲、忍耐的骄傲或许存在，但除此之外，这是一种无私的情感。没有一个水手曾经仅仅因为一艘船能往他口袋里装钱而珍爱它——即使那船属于他。我想，从来没有人这样做过；因为船东，即便是最好的船东，也始终在那种将船只与人融为一体的亲密平等情谊的范围之外，而这种情谊意味着双方彼此扶持，对抗着它们的水域世界那冷酷无情——尽管有时被掩饰——的敌意。大海——这一点必须承认——没有慷慨可言。男子汉的品质——勇气、刚毅、忍耐、忠诚——无论怎样展示，都从未触动过它那对自己力量的肆无忌惮的意识。海洋拥有一个被谄媚宠坏了的野蛮暴君的无良心性。它容不得最轻微的违抗迹象，而自从船只与人竟敢冒着它的怒容一同浮上海面以来，它就一直是它们不可和解的敌人。从那一天起，它不断吞噬着船队与人，受害者的数量——那么多沉船与丧生——从未填满它的怨恨。今日一如往昔，它随时准备诱骗与背叛、击碎与淹死那些不可救药的乐观主义者；这些人凭借船只的忠诚，试图从它那里夺取他们家园的财富、他们世界的统治权，或者仅仅是一份赖以充饥的食物。即使并非总是处于暴怒的击碎情绪中，它也始终暗中蓄势，随时准备溺亡。大海最惊人的奇迹，是其深不可测的残忍。

===Original===
I felt its dread for the first time in mid-Atlantic one day, many years ago, when we took off the crew of a Danish brig homeward bound from the West Indies. A thin, silvery mist softened the calm and majestic splendour of light without shadows—seemed to render the sky less remote and the ocean less immense. It was one of the days, when the might of the sea appears indeed lovable, like the nature of a strong man in moments of quiet intimacy. At sunrise we had made out a black speck to the westward, apparently suspended high up in the void behind a stirring, shimmering veil of silvery blue gauze that seemed at times to stir and float in the breeze which fanned us slowly along. The peace of that enchanting forenoon was so profound, so untroubled, that it seemed that every word pronounced loudly on our deck would penetrate to the very heart of that infinite mystery born from the conjunction of water and sky. We did not raise our voices. "A waterlogged derelict, I think, sir," said the second officer quietly, coming down from aloft with the binoculars in their case slung across his shoulders; and our captain, without a word, signed to the helmsman to steer for the black speck. Presently we made out a low, jagged stump sticking up forward—all that remained of her departed masts.

===Chinese===
许多年前的一天，我第一次在大西洋中部感受到这种恐惧；当时我们正在救援一艘从西印度群岛返航的丹麦双桅横帆船（brig）的船员。一层薄薄的银色薄雾柔化了那无影的、平静而庄严的光辉——似乎让天空不再那么遥远，让海洋不再那么浩瀚。那是大海的力量显得确实可爱的日子之一，如同一个强壮之人在安静亲密时刻的天性。日出时分，我们辨认出西方有一个黑色的小点，显然悬浮在虚空的高处，背后是一层颤动闪烁的银蓝色薄纱面纱，似乎时而在拂过我们徐徐前行的微风中摇曳飘荡。那个迷人的上午，其宁静如此深邃、如此不受搅扰，仿佛在我们甲板上说出的每一个响亮的字，都会穿透那由水与天结合而生的无尽神秘的核心。我们没有提高嗓门。"我想是一艘进水的废船，长官，"大副平静地说，从高处下来，望远镜连同皮套斜挎在肩上；我们的船长一言不发，向舵手指了指那个黑点。不一会儿，我们辨认出前方伸出一截低矮的、参差不齐的断桩——那是她那已逝的桅杆仅存的遗骸。

===Original===
The captain was expatiating in a low conversational tone to the chief mate upon the danger of these derelicts, and upon his dread of coming upon them at night, when suddenly a man forward screamed out, "There's people on board of her, sir! I see them!" in a most extraordinary voice—a voice never heard before in our ship; the amazing voice of a stranger. It gave the signal for a sudden tumult of shouts. The watch below ran up the forecastle head in a body, the cook dashed out of the galley. Everybody saw the poor fellows now. They were there! And all at once our ship, which had the well-earned name of being without a rival for speed in light winds, seemed to us to have lost the power of motion, as if the sea, becoming viscous, had clung to her sides. And yet she moved. Immensity, the inseparable companion of a ship's life, chose that day to breathe upon her as gently as a sleeping child. The clamour of our excitement had died out, and our living ship, famous for never losing steerage way as long as there was air enough to float a feather, stole, without a ripple, silent and white as a ghost, towards her mutilated and wounded sister, come upon at the point of death in the sunlit haze of a calm day at sea.

===Chinese===
船长正以低声的谈话口吻向大副详述这些废船的危险，以及他对于在夜间遭遇它们的恐惧，这时突然一个前方的人尖叫起来："船上有活人，长官！我看见他们了！"——用一种前所未闻的、极其异常的声音——一种我们在自己船上从未听过的声音；一个陌生人的惊异之声。它发出了突然爆发的一片喊叫的信号。值班水手们全体跑上船首楼，厨师从厨房里冲了出来。现在所有人都看见了那些可怜的家伙。他们就在那里！突然之间，我们这艘以轻风中无人能及的速度而享有盛名的船，在我们看来似乎失去了行动的能力，仿佛大海变得黏稠，粘附在她两侧。然而她还是在动。浩瀚——船之生命不可分离的伴侣——那天选择像对待一个熟睡的孩子般，温柔地向她呼吸。我们兴奋的喧嚣已经平息，我们这艘活生生的船——以只要有足以托起一根羽毛的空气便永不失舵效而闻名——悄然无波、寂静如幽灵般白色地滑向她那残损受伤的姊妹；那姊妹在一个风平浪静的日子、阳光照耀的薄雾中，被发现时已奄奄一息。

===Original===
With the binoculars glued to his eyes, the captain said in a quavering tone: "They are waving to us with something aft there." He put down the glasses on the skylight brusquely, and began to walk about the poop. "A shirt or a flag," he ejaculated irritably. "Can't make it out… Some damn rag or other!" He took a few more turns on the poop, glancing down over the rail now and then to see how fast we were moving. His nervous footsteps rang sharply in the quiet of the ship, where the other men, all looking the same way, had forgotten themselves in a staring immobility. "This will never do!" he cried out suddenly. "Lower the boats at once! Down with them!"

===Chinese===
船长把望远镜紧贴在眼睛上，用颤抖的声音说："他们在船尾那边用什么东西朝我们挥舞。"他粗暴地把望远镜放在天窗上，开始在艉楼上踱步。"一件衬衫，还是一面旗子，"他烦躁地脱口而出。"看不清……管它什么破布！"他在艉楼上又转了几圈，不时低头越过栏杆看看我们航行的速度。他紧张的脚步在船上的寂静中发出清脆的响声，其他人都望着同一个方向，陷入了一种凝视的静止之中，忘却了自身。"这可不行！"他突然喊道。"立刻放下艇去！放下去！"

===Original===
Before I jumped into mine he took me aside, as being an inexperienced junior, for a word of warning:
"You look out as you come alongside that she doesn't take you down with her. You understand?"
He murmured this confidentially, so that none of the men at the falls should overhear, and I was shocked. "Heavens! as if in such an emergency one stopped to think of danger!" I exclaimed to myself mentally, in scorn of such cold-blooded caution.
It takes many lessons to make a real seaman, and I got my rebuke at once. My experienced commander seemed in one searching glance to read my thoughts on my ingenuous face.
"What you're going for is to save life, not to drown your boat's crew for nothing," he growled severely in my ear. But as we shoved off he leaned over and cried out: "It all rests on the power of your arms, men. Give way for life!"

===Chinese===
在我跳进艇之前，他把我——作为一个缺乏经验的初级船员——拉到一边，叮嘱了一句：
"你靠上去的时候小心，别让她把你连人带艇拖下去。明白吗？"
他压低声音说出这番告诫，以免吊艇索边的水手们听见，我感到震惊。"天哪！在这种紧急关头，谁还有功夫去想危险！"我在心里对自己喊道，对这种冷血的谨慎嗤之以鼻。
要成为一个真正的水手，需要许多教训，而我立刻就领教了。我那经验丰富的指挥官仿佛仅凭一道审视的目光，便读懂了我天真脸庞上的想法。
"你去是为了救人，不是让你的艇员白白淹死，"他严厉地在我耳边低吼。但当我们离船时，他俯身喊道："全靠你们胳膊的力量了，伙计们。拼命划啊，为了活命！"

===Original===
We made a race of it, and I would never have believed that a common boat's crew of a merchantman could keep up so much determined fierceness in the regular swing of their stroke. What our captain had clearly perceived before we left had become plain to all of us since. The issue of our enterprise hung on a hair above that abyss of waters which will not give up its dead till the Day of Judgment. It was a race of two ship's boats matched against Death for a prize of nine men's lives, and Death had a long start. We saw the crew of the brig from afar working at the pumps—still pumping on that wreck, which already had settled so far down that the gentle, low swell, over which our boats rose and fell easily without a check to their speed, welling up almost level with her head-rails, plucked at the ends of broken gear swinging desolately under her naked bowsprit.

===Chinese===
我们展开了一场竞渡，我绝不会相信一艘商船上的普通艇员，竟能在他们规律的划桨节奏中保持如此坚定的凶猛。我们离船前船长已清晰感知的一切，此后对我们所有人也变得显而易见。我们事业的成败悬于一线，悬在那片绝不会在审判日之前交出死者的深渊之上。这是两艘艇与死亡的赛跑，赌注是九条人命，而死亡已遥遥领先。我们从远处看见那艘双桅横帆船的船员们在抽水——仍在那艘沉船上抽水；那船已经沉得如此之深，轻柔的低涌——我们的艇在上面轻松起伏，丝毫不影响速度——几乎涨到了与她的船首栏杆齐平，拍打着在她光秃秃的艏斜桅下凄凉摆荡的断索末端。

===Original===
We could not, in all conscience, have picked out a better day for our regatta had we had the free choice of all the days that ever dawned upon the lonely struggles and solitary agonies of ships since the Norse rovers first steered to the westward against the run of Atlantic waves. It was a very good race. At the finish there was not an oar's length between the first and second boat, with Death coming in a good third on the top of the very next smooth swell, for all one knew to the contrary. The scuppers of the brig gurgled softly all together when the water rising against her sides subsided sleepily with a low wash, as if playing about an immovable rock. Her bulwarks were gone fore and forth, and one saw her bare deck low-lying like a raft and swept clean of boats, spars, houses—of everything except the ringbolts and the heads of the pumps. I had one dismal glimpse of it as I braced myself up to receive upon my breast the last man to leave her, the captain, who literally let himself fall into my arms.

===Chinese===
说实话，即便我们能在自北欧海盗首次逆着大西洋浪涛向西航行以来所有照耀过船只孤独挣扎与独自苦痛的黎明中自由挑选，我们也选不出比这更好的日子来举行我们的赛艇了。这是一场非常精彩的比赛。终点时，第一名和第二名之间只差不到一桨之距，而死亡紧随其后，在下一个平滑的涌浪顶端稳居第三名，只要没人能证明相反。当拍击船侧的海水带着低沉的水声慵懒地退去时，双桅横帆船的排水孔一起发出轻柔的咕噜声，仿佛在拍击一块不可撼动的礁石。她的舷墙从船首到船尾都已消失，你可以看见她光秃秃的甲板低低地躺在那里，像一个木筏，被扫得干干净净——没有艇、没有桅桁、没有舱室——什么都没有了，只剩下系缆栓和水泵的顶端。我瞥见了那凄惨的一幕，随即挺起胸膛，准备迎接最后一个离开她的那个人——船长——他简直就像让自己倒在我怀里。

===Original===
It had been a weirdly silent rescue—a rescue without a hail, without a single uttered word, without a gesture or a sign, without a conscious exchange of glances. Up to the very last moment those on board stuck to their pumps, which spouted two clear streams of water upon their bare feet. Their brown skin showed through the rents of their shirts; and the two small bunches of half-naked, tattered men went on bowing from the waist to each other in their backbreaking labour, up and down, absorbed, with no time for a glance over the shoulder at the help that was coming to them. As we dashed, unregarded, alongside a voice let out one, only one hoarse howl of command, and then, just as they stood, without caps, with the salt drying grey in the wrinkles and folds of their hairy, haggard faces, blinking stupidly at us their red eyelids, they made a bolt away from the handles, tottering and jostling against each other, and positively flung themselves over upon our very heads. The clatter they made tumbling into the boats had an extraordinarily destructive effect upon the illusion of tragic dignity our self-esteem had thrown over the contests of mankind with the sea. On that exquisite day of gently breathing peace and veiled sunshine perished my romantic love to what men's imagination had proclaimed the most august aspect of Nature. The cynical indifference of the sea to the merits of human suffering and courage, laid bare in this ridiculous, panic-tainted performance extorted from the dire extremity of nine good and honourable seamen, revolted me. I saw the duplicity of the sea's most tender mood. It was so because it could not help itself, but the awed respect of the early days was gone. I felt ready to smile bitterly at its enchanting charm and glare viciously at its furies. In a moment, before we shoved off, I had looked coolly at the life of my choice. Its illusions were gone, but its fascination remained. I had become a seaman at all.

===Chinese===
那是一场诡异的寂静救援——没有一声呼喊，没有说出一个字，没有一个手势或示意，没有有意识的目光交流。直到最后一刻，船上的那些人仍坚守在水泵旁，两道清澈的水流喷溅在他们赤裸的脚上。他们褐色的皮肤透过衬衫的裂口显露出来；这两小群半裸的、衣衫褴褛的人继续弯着腰在他们累断腰的劳作中彼此鞠躬——上上下下，全神贯注，没有时间回头看一眼正向他们驶来的救援。当我们不被注意地冲到他们旁边时，一个声音发出了一声——仅仅一声——嘶哑的命令式嚎叫，然后，就在他们站着的地方，没戴帽子，盐霜在他们毛发蓬乱、憔悴的脸上皱纹和褶皱中风干成灰色，他们用发红的眼睑傻傻地眨巴着看我们，他们离开了水泵把手，跌跌撞撞地互相推搡，然后简直就像直接扑到了我们头上。他们跌进艇里时发出的哗啦声，对我们的自尊心投射在人与大海较量上的悲剧尊严的幻象，产生了极具破坏性的影响。在那微风轻拂、阳光朦胧的美妙日子里，我对人类想象力所宣告的自然最庄严面貌的浪漫之爱死去了。大海对人类苦难与勇气之价值的冷嘲般的漠视，在这场从九名善良正直的水手的绝境中逼出来的可笑、充满恐慌的表演中暴露无遗，令我憎恶。我看到了大海最温柔情绪中的虚伪。它如此，是因为它别无选择，但早年那种敬畏的尊重已经消失了。我感到准备好对它迷人的魅力报以苦涩的微笑，对它的暴怒投以恶狠狠的一瞥。片刻之间，在我们离艇之前，我冷冷地审视了我选择的生活。它的幻象已经消失，但它的魅力犹存。我终于成了一个水手。

===Original===
We pulled hard for a quarter of an hour, then laid on our oars waiting for our ship. She was coming down on us with swelling sails, looking delicately tall and exquisitely noble through the mist. The captain of the brig, who sat in the stern sheets by my side with his face in his hands, raised his head and began to speak with a sort of sombre volubility. They had lost their masts and sprung a leak in a hurricane; drifted for weeks, always at the pumps, met more bad weather; the ships they sighted failed to make them out, the leak gained upon them slowly, and the seas had left them nothing to make a raft of. It was very hard to see ship after ship pass by at a distance, "as if everybody had agreed that we must be left to drown," he added. But they went on trying to keep the brig afloat as long as possible, and working the pumps constantly on insufficient food, mostly raw, till "yesterday evening," he continued monotonously, "just as the sun went down, the men's hearts broke."

===Chinese===
我们奋力划了十五分钟，然后停桨等待我们的船。她张着鼓起的帆向我们驶来，在薄雾中显得修长精致、高贵绝伦。那艘双桅横帆船的船长坐在我旁边的艉座板上，双手捂着脸，抬起头来，以一种阴郁的流利开始讲述。他们在一场飓风中失去了桅杆，船身漏水；漂流了数周，一直守在水泵旁，又遭遇了更恶劣的天气；他们望见的船只没有发现他们，漏水慢慢地加剧，大海没有给他们留下任何可以制作木筏的东西。看着一艘又一艘船从远处驶过，"仿佛所有人都商定好了，要把我们丢下淹死，"他补充道。但他们继续尽力让双桅横帆船尽可能久地漂浮，在不足的——大多是生的——食物供给下不停地操作水泵，直到"昨天傍晚，"他单调地继续说，"就在太阳落山的时候，水手们的心碎了。"

===Original===
He made an almost imperceptible pause here, and went on again with exactly the same intonation:
"They told me the brig could not be saved, and they thought they had done enough for themselves. I said nothing to that. It was true. It was no mutiny. I had nothing to say to them. They lay about aft all night, as still as so many dead men. I did not lie down. I kept a lookout. When the first light came I saw your ship at once. I waited for more light; the breeze began to fail on my face. Then I shouted out as loud as I was able, 'Look at that ship!' but only two men got up very slowly and came to me. At first only we three stood alone, for a long time, watching you coming down to us, and feeling the breeze drop to a calm almost; but afterwards others, too, rose, one after another, and by-and-by I had all my crew behind me. I turned round and said to them that they could see the ship was coming our way, but in this small breeze she might come too late after all, unless we turned to and tried to keep the brig afloat long enough to give you time to save us all. I spoke like that to them, and then I gave the command to man the pumps."

===Chinese===
他在这里做了一个几乎无法察觉的停顿，然后以完全相同的语调继续说下去：
"他们告诉我这艘双桅横帆船救不了了，他们觉得自己已经为自己做了够多的事了。对此我什么也没说。这是事实。这不是兵变。我对他们无话可说。他们整个晚上都躺在船尾，像死人一样一动不动。我没有躺下。我保持着瞭望。当第一道光出现时，我立刻看见了你们的船。我等待更多的光亮；我脸上的微风开始减弱。然后我尽可能大声地喊：'看那艘船！'但只有两个人非常缓慢地站起来，走到我身边。起初只有我们三个孤独地站着，站了很长时间，看着你们向我们驶来，感觉风几乎要停成无风；但后来其他人也一个接一个地站起来，渐渐地，我所有的船员都站在我身后了。我转身对他们说，他们可以看到那艘船正朝我们这边来，但在这微风中，她可能终究来得太迟了，除非我们开始努力让双桅横帆船尽可能久地漂浮，给你们争取时间来救我们所有人。我那样对他们说了，然后我下令到水泵边去。"

===Original===
He gave the command, and gave the example, too, by going himself to the handles, but it seems that these men did actually hang back for a moment, looking at each other dubiously before they followed him. "He! he! he!" He broke out into a most unexpected, imbecile, pathetic, nervous little giggle. "Their hearts were broken so! They had been played with too long," he explained, apologetically, lowering his eyes, and became silent.

===Chinese===
他下了命令，也给出了榜样——亲自走到水泵把手前——但这些人似乎确实犹豫了片刻，彼此怀疑地看了看，才跟上他。"嘿！嘿！嘿！"他突然发出一阵极其出人意料的、低能的、可怜的、神经质的小声窃笑。"他们的心就是这样碎的！他们被捉弄得太久了，"他抱歉地解释道，垂下眼睛，沉默了。

===Original===
Twenty-five years is a long time—a quarter of a century is a dim and distant past; but to this day I remember the dark-brown feet, hands, and faces of two of these men whose hearts had been broken by the sea. They were lying very still on their sides on the bottom boards between the thwarts, curled up like dogs. My boat's crew, leaning over the looms of their oars, stared and listened as if at the play. The master of the brig looked up suddenly to ask me what day it was.
They had lost the date. When I told him it was Sunday, the 22nd, he frowned, making some mental calculation, then nodded twice sadly to himself, staring at nothing.
His aspect was miserably unkempt and wildly sorrowful. Had it not been for the unquenchable candour of his blue eyes, whose unhappy, tired glance every moment sought his abandoned, sinking brig, as if it could find rest nowhere else, he would have appeared mad. But he was too simple to go mad, too simple with that manly simplicity which alone can bear men unscathed in mind and body through an encounter with the deadly playfulness of the sea or with its less abominable fury.

===Chinese===
二十五年是很长的时间——四分之一个世纪是一个模糊而遥远的过去；但直到今天，我还记得那两个被大海碎了心的人的深褐色的脚、手和脸。他们侧卧在座板之间的船底板上，一动不动，像狗一样蜷缩着。我的艇员们俯身倚在桨柄上，像看戏一样盯着听。双桅横帆船的船长突然抬起头来，问我今天是几号。
他们已经不知道日期了。我告诉他今天是星期天，22号，他皱起眉头，心里做了些计算，然后悲伤地对自己点了点头，茫然地凝视着什么。
他的外表极其邋遢，极度哀伤。若非他那蓝色眼睛里不可熄灭的坦诚——那双不快乐的、疲惫的目光每时每刻都在寻找他那被遗弃的、正在下沉的双桅横帆船，仿佛它在别处找不到安宁——他看起来会像个疯子。但他太单纯了，不会发疯，单纯到带着那种唯有男子汉的单纯才能让人心智与身体毫发无伤地穿越大海致命的戏耍或它那不那么可憎的暴怒的单纯。

===Original===
Neither angry, nor playful, nor smiling, it enveloped our distant ship growing bigger as she neared us, our boats with the rescued men and the dismantled hull of the brig we were leaving behind, in the large and placid embrace of its quietness, half lost in the fair haze, as if in a dream of infinite and tender clemency. There was no frown, no wrinkle on its face, not a ripple. And the run of the slight swell was so smooth that it resembled the graceful undulation of a piece of shimmering grey silk shot with gleams of green. We pulled an easy stroke; but when the master of the brig, after a glance over his shoulder, stood up with a low exclamation, my men feathered their oars instinctively, without an order, and the boat lost her way.

===Chinese===
大海既不愤怒，也不戏耍，也不微笑；它将我们远处正在靠近的船——越来越近、越来越大——我们的艇，载着获救的人们，以及我们留在身后的那艘解体的双桅横帆船的残骸，一并包裹在它宁静的宽广安详的拥抱中，半隐于美好的薄雾里，仿佛在一个无限温柔的仁慈之梦中。它的脸上没有皱眉，没有皱纹，没有一丝涟漪。轻微的涌浪起伏如此平滑，宛如一块闪烁的灰色丝绸上泛着绿光的优雅波动。我们划着轻松的桨；但当双桅横帆船的船长在瞥了一眼身后，低声惊呼着站起来时，我的水手们本能地将桨叶立起，无需命令，艇便停了下来。

===Original===
He was steadying himself on my shoulder with a strong grip, while his other arm, flung up rigidly, pointed a denunciatory finger at the immense tranquillity of the ocean. After his first exclamation, which stopped the swing of our oars, he made no sound, but his whole attitude seemed to cry out an indignant "Behold!"… I could not imagine what vision of evil had come to him. I was startled, and the amazing energy of his immobilized gesture made my heart beat faster with the anticipation of something monstrous and unsuspected. The stillness around us became crushing.

===Chinese===
他用一只手紧紧抓住我的肩膀以稳住自己，另一只手臂僵直地举起，用一根控诉般的手指指向大海广袤的宁静。在他那止住了我们划桨节奏的第一声惊呼之后，他没有再发出声音，但他整个姿态似乎在喊出一声愤慨的"看哪！"……我无法想象他眼前出现了什么恐怖的景象。我吃了一惊，他那僵定手势中惊人的能量让我的心跳加速，预感到某种巨大而未料之事。我们周围的寂静变得令人窒息。

===Original===
For a moment the succession of silky undulations ran on innocently. I saw each of them swell up the misty line of the horizon, far, far away beyond the derelict brig, and the next moment, with a slight friendly toss of our boat, it had passed under us and was gone. The lulling cadence of the rise and fall, the invariable gentleness of this irresistible force, the great charm of the deep waters, warmed my breast deliciously, like the subtle poison of a love-potion. But all this lasted only a few soothing seconds before I jumped up too, making the boat roll like the veriest landlubber.

===Chinese===
有一阵子，那一连串丝绸般的涌浪继续天真地奔涌。我看见每一朵涌浪在远方朦胧的地平线上隆起——远远地，在那艘废船之外——然后下一刻，伴随着我们艇的一次友善的轻颠，它从我们身下通过，消失了。起伏的抚慰节奏，这不可抗拒之力一成不变的温柔，深水的巨大魅力，愉悦地温暖着我的胸膛，如同爱情魔药的微妙毒素。但这一切只持续了几个舒缓的秒，然后我也跳了起来，让艇摇晃得像个十足的旱鸭子。

===Original===
Something startling, mysterious, hastily confused, was taking place. I watched it with incredulous and fascinated awe, as one watches the confused, swift movements of some deed of violence done in the dark. As if at a given signal, the run of the smooth undulations seemed checked suddenly around the brig. By a strange optical delusion the whole sea appeared to rise upon her in one overwhelming heave of its silky surface, where in one spot a smother of foam broke out ferociously. And then the effort subsided. It was all over, and the smooth swell ran on as before from the horizon in uninterrupted cadence of motion, passing under us with a slight friendly toss of our boat. Far away, where the brig had been, an angry white stain undulating on the surface of steely-grey waters, shot with gleams of green, diminished swiftly, without a hiss, like a patch of pure snow melting in the sun. And the great stillness after this initiation into the sea's implacable hate seemed full of dread thoughts and shadows of disaster.

===Chinese===
一些惊人的、神秘的、匆忙混乱的事情正在发生。我以难以置信又着迷的敬畏注视着它，就像一个人注视着黑暗中某种暴行的混乱、迅疾的动作。仿佛得到一个暗号，那平滑涌浪的奔涌似乎在双桅横帆船周围突然被遏制。一种奇异的视觉错觉让整片大海似乎以它丝绸般表面的一次压倒性的隆起向她涌来，在一处地方，一团泡沫猛烈地喷涌而出。然后，这番努力平息了。一切结束了，平滑的涌浪一如从前从地平线上以不间断的节奏奔涌而来，伴随着我们艇的一次友善的轻颠从我们身下通过。远方，那艘双桅横帆船曾所在的地方，一道愤怒的白色污迹在钢灰色的海面上起伏，泛着绿光，迅速缩小，没有嘶嘶声，像一块纯白的雪在阳光下融化。在这初次领教了大海无情仇恨之后的巨大的寂静中，似乎充满了恐怖的念头和灾难的阴影。

===Original===
"Gone!" ejaculated from the depths of his chest my bowman in a final tone. He spat in his hands, and took a better grip on his oar. The captain of the brig lowered his rigid arm slowly, and looked at our faces in a solemnly conscious silence, which called upon us to share in his simple-minded, marvelling awe. All at once he sat down by my side, and leaned forward earnestly at my boat's crew, who, swinging together in a long, easy stroke, keep their eyes fixed upon him faithfully.

===Chinese===
"沉了！"我的艇首桨手从胸腔深处以终结的语气喊了一声。他往手掌上吐了口唾沫，更紧地握住了桨。双桅横帆船的船长缓缓放下他那僵直的手臂，在庄严而自觉的沉默中注视着我们的脸庞，那沉默要求我们分享他那单纯的、惊愕的敬畏。突然间，他在我身边坐下，向前倾身，诚恳地看着我的艇员们；他们以整齐的长划共同荡桨，忠实地将目光锁定在他身上。

===Original===
"No ship could have done so well," he addressed them firmly, after a moment of strained silence, during which he seemed with trembling lips to seek for words fit to bear such high testimony. "She was small, but she was good. I had no anxiety. She was strong. Last voyage I had my wife and two children in her. No other ship could have stood so long the weather she had to live through for days and days before we got dismasted a fortnight ago. She was fairly worn out, and that's all. You may believe me. She lasted under us for days and days, but she could not last forever. It was long enough. I am glad it is over. No better ship was ever left to sink at sea on such a day as this."

===Chinese===
"没有船能做得比她更好了，"他在一阵紧张的沉默后坚定地对他们说，在那沉默中，他似乎用颤抖的嘴唇寻找着足以承载如此崇高证词的言辞。"她很小，但她很好。我曾毫不担忧。她很结实。上一趟航程，我带着我的妻子和两个孩子在她上面。没有别的船能像她那样经受住我们必须承受的那么多天的恶劣天气——直到两周前我们失去桅杆。她只是彻底磨损了，仅此而已。你们可以相信我。她在我们身下撑了又撑，但不可能永远撑下去。够久了。我很高兴这一切结束了。在这样一个日子里，没有比她更好的船被留在海上沉没了。"

===Original===
He was competent to pronounce the funereal oration of a ship, this son of ancient sea-folk, whose national existence, so little stained by the excesses of manly virtues, had demanded nothing but the merest foothold from the earth. By the merits of his sea-wise forefathers and by the artlessness of his heart, he was made fit to deliver this excellent discourse. There was nothing wanting in its orderly arrangement—neither piety nor faith, nor the tribute of praise due to the worthy dead, with the edifying recital of their achievement. She had lived, he had loved her; she had suffered, and he was glad she was at rest. It was an excellent discourse. And it was orthodox, too, in its fidelity to the cardinal article of a seaman's faith, of which it was a single-minded confession. "Ships are all right." They are. They who live with the sea have got to hold by that creed first and last; and it came to me, as I glanced at him sideways, that some men were not altogether unworthy in honour and conscience to pronounce the funereal eulogium of a ship's constancy in life and death.

===Chinese===
这个古老海洋民族的后裔——其民族生存极少被男子汉美德的过度行为所玷污，从大地那里所要求的不过是最微小的立足之处——完全有能力为一艘船宣读葬礼悼词。凭借他那通晓海洋的先祖的功德，以及他那质朴心灵的纯真，他适于发表这篇精彩的演说。在其有序的安排中，什么都不缺——既有虔诚，也有信仰，还有对可敬死者的应有赞颂，以及对其成就的令人感奋的叙述。她曾存活过，他曾爱过她；她曾受苦，而他欣慰于她的安息。这是一篇精彩的演说。而且它也是正统的，因为它忠于水手信仰的核心信条——这演说便是对其真诚的告白。"船是靠得住的。"它们确实靠得住。那些与大海为生的人，必须自始至终恪守这一信条；当我侧目看他时，我想到，有些人——以荣誉和良知而言——并非全然不配宣读对一艘船在生与死中之忠诚的葬礼颂词。

===Original===
After this, sitting by my side with his loosely-clasped hands hanging between his knees, he uttered no word, made no movement till the shadow of our ship's sails fell on the boat, when, at the loud cheer greeting the return of the victors with their prize, he lifted up his troubled face with a faint smile of pathetic indulgence. This smile of the worthy descendant of the most ancient sea-folk whose audacity and hardihood had left no trace of greatness and glory upon the waters, completed the cycle of my initiation. There was an infinite depth of hereditary wisdom in its pitying sadness. It made the hearty bursts of cheering sound like a childish noise of triumph. Our crew shouted with immense confidence—honest souls! As if anybody could ever make sure of having prevailed against the sea, which has betrayed so many ships of great "name," so many proud men, so many towering ambitions of fame, power, wealth, greatness!

===Chinese===
此后，他坐在我身边，双手松松地交握垂在两膝之间，没有再说一个字，也没有再做一个动作，直到我们的船帆的阴影落到艇上；在迎接凯旋者带着他们的战利品归来的热烈欢呼声中，他抬起忧伤的脸庞，带着一丝可怜的宽容的淡淡微笑。这位最古老海洋民族的可敬后裔的微笑——其先祖的胆识与刚毅在水面上未曾留下任何伟大与光荣的痕迹——完成了我入门的轮回。那怜悯的哀伤中蕴含着无限深邃的世代智慧。它让那发自肺腑的阵阵欢呼听起来像孩童的胜利喧哗。我们的船员们以巨大的信心呼喊着——诚实的灵魂！好像真有人能确信自己已经战胜了大海——那个背叛了那么多声名显赫的船只、那么多骄傲的人、那么多凌云壮志的名望、权力、财富与伟大的大海！

===Original===
As I brought the boat under the falls my captain, in high good-humour, leaned over, spreading his red and freckled elbows on the rail, and called down to me sarcastically, out of the depths of his cynic philosopher's beard:
"So you have brought the boat back after all, have you?"
Sarcasm was "his way," and the most that can be said for it is that it was natural. This did not make it lovable. But it is decorous and expedient to fall in with one's commander's way. "Yes. I brought the boat back all right, sir," I answered. And the good man believed me. It was not for him to discern upon me the marks of my recent initiation. And yet I was not exactly the same youngster who had taken the boat away—all impatience for a race against death, with the prize of nine men's lives at the end.

===Chinese===
当我把艇划到吊艇索下方时，我的船长兴高采烈地俯身趴在栏杆上，将他那发红而多斑点的胳膊肘张开，从他那愤世嫉俗的哲学家般的胡须深处向我讽刺地喊道：
"所以你到底还是把艇划回来了，是吗？"
讽刺是"他的风格"，对其最好的评价不过是：这是自然的。这并未使它变得可爱。但遵从指挥官的作风，是得体而明智的。"是的。我把艇好好地带回来了，长官，"我回答。这个好人相信了我。他看不出我身上最近经历入门仪式留下的印记。然而，我已不再是当初划走那艘艇时的那个毛头小子了——当时一心想与死亡赛跑，赌注是九条人命。

===Original===
Already I looked with other eyes upon the sea. I knew it capable of betraying the generous ardour of youth as implacably as, indifferent to evil and good, it would have betrayed the basest greed or the noblest heroism. My conception of its magnanimous greatness was gone. And I looked upon the true sea—the sea that plays with men till their hearts are broken, and wears stout ships to death. Nothing can touch the brooding bitterness of its heart. Open to all and faithful to none, it exercises its fascination for the undoing of the best. To love it is not well. It knows no bond of plighted troth, no fidelity to misfortune, to long companionship, to long devotion. The promise it holds out perpetually is very great; but the only secret of its possession is strength, strength—the jealous, sleepless strength of a man guarding a coveted treasure within his gates.

===Chinese===
我已经用另一双眼睛审视大海。我知道它能够毫不留情地背叛年轻人慷慨的热情，正如它漠然于善恶，也会背叛最卑劣的贪婪或最高贵的英雄主义。我对其宽宏伟大的构想已经破灭。我看到了真正的大海——那个戏弄人们直到他们心碎，将坚固的船舶磨损致死的大海。没有什么能触及它那沉思的苦涩之心。对所有人敞开却对任何人不忠，它施展着它的魅力，旨在毁掉最优秀者。爱它是不好的。它不懂誓言的约束，不懂对不幸、对长久陪伴、对长久奉献的忠诚。它永远许诺的前景极为宏大；但拥有它的唯一秘诀是力量，力量——一个男人守护着门内觊觎之财的那嫉妒的、不眠的力量。

## The Nursery of the Craft / 这门行当的苗圃

### XXXVII / 第三十七篇

===Original===
The cradle of oversea traffic and of the art of naval combats, the Mediterranean, apart from all the associations of adventure and glory, the common heritage of all mankind, makes a tender appeal to a seaman. It has sheltered the infancy of his craft. He looks upon it as a man may look at a vast nursery in an old, old mansion where innumerable generations of his own people have learned to walk. I say his own people because, in a sense, all sailors belong to one family: all are descended from that adventurous and shaggy ancestor who, bestriding a shapeless log and paddling with a crooked branch, accomplished the first coasting-trip in a sheltered bay ringing with the admiring howls of his tribe. It is a matter of regret that all those brothers in craft and feeling, whose generations have learned to walk a ship's deck in that nursery, have been also more than once fiercely engaged in cutting each other's throats there. But life, apparently, has such exigencies. Without human propensity to murder and other sorts of unrighteousness there would have been no historical heroism. It is a consoling reflection.

===Chinese===
地中海——远洋贸易与海战艺术的摇篮，冒险与荣耀的共同遗产、全人类的共有之物——除了这一切联想之外，它还向水手发出温柔的呼唤。它庇护过他这门行当的幼年。他注视它，如同注视一座古老宅邸中广袤的育儿室，无数代他自己的族人曾在那里学会行走。我说"他自己的族人"，因为在某种意义上，所有水手同属一家：他们都源于那位毛发蓬乱的冒险祖先——他骑跨一段不成形的圆木，以一根弯曲的树枝作桨，在一片受庇护的海湾中完成了第一次沿岸航行，耳边回荡着部落中仰慕的嚎叫。令人遗憾的是，所有那些在行当与情感上同为兄弟的人——他们的世代在那间育儿室里学会在船甲板上行走——在那里也曾不止一次凶猛地互相割喉。但生活显然有这种苛求。若无人类嗜杀及种种不义的倾向，便不会有历史上的英雄壮举。这是一个令人宽慰的反思。

===Original===
And then, if one examines impartially the deeds of violence, they appear of but small consequence. From Salamis to Actium, through Lepanto and the Nile to the naval massacre of Navarino, not to mention other armed encounters of lesser interest, all the blood heroically spilt into the Mediterranean has not stained with a single trail of purple the deep azure of its classic waters.

===Chinese===
而且，若公正地审视那些暴行，它们似乎无足轻重。从萨拉米斯（Salamis）到阿克提乌姆（Actium），经莱潘托（Lepanto）和尼罗河到纳瓦里诺（Navarino）的海军屠杀——更遑论其他次要的武装遭遇——所有英勇倾入地中海的鲜血，都未能在它那经典的深蓝色水面上留下一道紫色的痕迹。

===Original===
Of course, it may be argued that battles have shaped the destiny of mankind. The question whether they have shaped it well would remain open, however. But it would be hardly worth discussing. It is very probable that, had the Battle of Salamis never been fought, the face of the world would have been much as we behold it now, fashioned by the mediocre inspiration and the shortsighted labours of men. From a long and miserable experience of suffering, injustice, disgrace and aggression the nations of the earth are mostly swayed by fear—fear of the sort that a little cheap oratory turns easily to rage, hate, and violence. Innocent, guileless fear has been the cause of many wars. Not, of course, the fear of war itself, which, in the evolution of sentiments and ideas, has come to be regarded at last as a half-mystic and glorious ceremony with certain fashionable rites and preliminary incantations, wherein the conception of its true nature has been lost.

===Chinese===
当然，有人可能会争辩说战役塑造了人类的命运。但它是否塑造得好，这个问题仍然悬而未决。不过这几乎不值得讨论。极有可能的是，倘若萨拉米斯之战从未打响，世界的面貌仍会与我们今天所见大同小异——由人类平庸的灵感和短视的劳作塑造而成。在经历了漫长而悲惨的苦难、不公、耻辱与侵略之后，地球上的民族大多被恐惧所支配——那种只需一点廉价修辞便能轻易转化为愤怒、仇恨与暴力的恐惧。无辜的、毫无心机的恐惧一直是诸多战争的起因。当然不是对战争本身的恐惧——在情感与观念的演进中，战争最终被视为一种半神秘的、光荣的仪式，配有种种时髦的仪轨与预备的咒语，而其真正本质的概念已然失落。

===Original===
To apprehend the true aspect, force, and morality of war as a natural function of mankind one requires a feather in the hair and a ring in the nose, or, better still, teeth filed to a point and a tattooed breast. Unfortunately, a return to such simple ornamentation is impossible. We are bound to the chariot of progress. There is no going back; and, as bad luck would have it, our civilization, which has done so much for the comfort and adornment of our bodies and the elevation of our minds, has made lawful killing frightfully and needlessly expensive.

===Chinese===
要理解战争作为人类自然功能的真实面貌、力量与道德，你须在头发上插一根羽毛、在鼻子上穿一枚环——或更好的是，把牙齿锉成尖的、在胸膛上刺青。不幸的是，回归这种简朴的装饰已不可能。我们被绑缚在进步的战车上，无法回头；而糟糕的是，我们的文明——为身体的舒适与装饰、心智的提升做了如此之多——却让合法的杀戮变得骇人听闻且毫无必要地昂贵。

===Original===
The whole question of improved armaments has been approached by the governments of the earth in a spirit of nervous and unreflecting haste, whereas the right way was lying plainly before them, and had only to be pursued with calm determination. The learned vigils and labours of a certain class of inventors should have been rewarded with honourable liberality as justice demanded; and the bodies of the inventors should have been blown to pieces by means of their own perfected explosives and improved weapons with extreme publicity as the commonest prudence dictated. By this method the ardour of research in that direction would have been restrained without infringing the sacred privileges of science. For the lack of a little cool thinking in our guides and masters this course has not been followed, and a beautiful simplicity has been sacrificed for no real advantage.

===Chinese===
整个改进军备的问题，地球上各政府一直以紧张而未经反思的匆忙态度来应对，而正确的道路明明就在眼前，只需以冷静的决心沿其前行即可。某一类发明家的博学守夜与辛劳理应得到公正要求的体面奖赏；而这些发明家的身体则应以最公开的方式被他们自己完善的炸药和改进的武器炸成碎片——这是最基本的谨慎所要求的。用这种方法，那一方向的研究热情本可被遏制，而不侵犯科学的神圣特权。正因我们的导师和主宰缺乏一点冷静的思考，这条路未被遵循，一种美丽的简洁被牺牲了，却未带来任何实际的好处。

===Original===
A frugal mind cannot defend itself from considerable bitterness when reflecting that at the Battle of Actium (which was fought for no less a stake than the dominion of the world) the fleet of Octavianus Caesar and the fleet of Antonius, including the Egyptian division and Cleopatra's galley with purple sails, probably cost less than two modern battleships, or, as the modern naval book-jargon has it, two capital units. But no amount of lubberly book-jargon can disguise a fact well calculated to afflict the soul of every sound economist. It is not likely that the Mediterranean will ever behold a battle with a greater issue; but when the time comes for another historical fight its bottom will be enriched as never before by a quantity of jagged scrap-iron, paid for at pretty nearly its weight of gold by the deluded populations inhabiting the isles and continents of this planet.

===Chinese===
一个俭省的人在反思时无法不感到相当的苦涩：在阿克提乌姆之战中（那场战役的赌注不亚于世界霸权），屋大维（Octavianus Caesar）的舰队与安东尼（Antonius）的舰队——包括埃及分舰队和克利奥帕特拉（Cleopatra）那紫帆 galley——的总造价大概还不及两艘现代战列舰，或者说用现代海军书本术语来说，两艘主力舰。但无论多么笨拙的书本术语，都无法掩盖一个令每一位健全经济学家灵魂痛苦的事实。地中海不太可能再目睹一场结果更为重大的战役了；但当又一场历史性战斗到来时，它的海底将比以往任何时候都更丰富地堆满锯齿状的废铁——由这颗星球上岛屿和大陆上受骗的民众以近乎等重的黄金代价购得。

### XXXVIII / 第三十八篇

===Original===
Happy he who, like Ulysses, has made an adventurous voyage; and there is no such sea for adventurous voyages as the Mediterranean—the inland sea which the ancients looked upon as so vast and so full of wonders. And, indeed, it was terrible and wonderful; for it is we alone who, swayed by the audacity of our minds and the tremors of our hearts, are the sole artisans of all the wonder and romance of the world.

===Chinese===
像尤利西斯（Ulysses）那样做过一次冒险航行的人是幸福的；而说到冒险航行，没有哪片海比得上地中海（Mediterranean）——那片古人视为浩瀚无垠、充满奇迹的内海。它的确既可怖又神奇；因为唯有我们——受着我们心智的胆魄和心灵的悸动所驱使——才是世间一切奇迹与传奇的唯一匠人。

===Original===
It was for the Mediterranean sailors that fair-haired sirens sang among the black rocks seething in white foam and mysterious voices spoke in the darkness above the moving wave—voices menacing, seductive, or prophetic, like that voice heard at the beginning of the Christian era by the master of an African vessel in the Gulf of Syrta, whose calm nights are full of strange murmurs and flitting shadows. It called him by name, bidding him go and tell all men that the great god Pan was dead. But the great legend of the Mediterranean, the legend of traditional song and grave history, lives, fascinating and immortal, in our minds.

===Chinese===
正是地中海的水手们，听到了金发的塞壬（sirens）在翻滚着白沫的黑色礁石间歌唱，听到了神秘的声音在涌动的波浪上方的黑暗中说话——那些声音或带威胁、或具诱惑、或含预言，正如基督教纪元之初，一位非洲船只的船长在锡尔特湾（Gulf of Syrta）听到的那个声音——那里宁静的夜晚充满了奇异的低语和掠过的暗影。那声音唤着他的名字，命他去告诉所有人，伟大的潘神（Pan）已死。但地中海的伟大传说——传统歌谣与庄严历史的传说——在我们的脑海中生动而永恒地存活着。

===Original===
The dark and fearful sea of the subtle Ulysses' wanderings, agitated by the wrath of Olympian gods, harbouring on its isles the fury of strange monsters and the wiles of strange women; the highway of heroes and sages, of warriors, pirates, and saints; the workaday sea of Carthaginian merchants and the pleasure lake of the Roman Caesars, claims the veneration of every seaman as the historical home of that spirit of open defiance against the great waters of the earth which is the very soul of his calling. Issuing thence to the west and south, as a youth leaves the shelter of his parental house, this spirit found the way to the Indies, discovered the coasts of a new continent, and traversed at last the immensity of the great Pacific, rich in groups of islands remote and mysterious like the constellations of the sky.

===Chinese===
狡黠的尤利西斯流浪其间、被奥林匹斯众神的怒意搅扰的那片幽暗可怖之海，它的岛屿上藏着凶暴的异兽和诡诈的女妖；英雄与哲人的通道，武士、海盗与圣徒的通途；迦太基商人的作业场和罗马凯撒们的游乐湖——它要求每一位水手的敬仰，因为它是那种公然反抗大地汪洋的精神的历史故里——而这精神正是他这一行的灵魂。如同一个青年离开父母屋檐的庇护，这精神从地中海向西向南出发，找到了通往印度的路，发现了新大陆的海岸，最终穿越了浩瀚无际的太平洋——那里散布着星罗棋布的岛屿，遥远而神秘，宛若天穹中的星座。

===Original===
The first impulse of navigation took its visible form in that tideless basin freed from hidden shoals and treacherous currents, as if in tender regard for the infancy of the art. The steep shores of the Mediterranean favoured the beginners in one of humanity's most daring enterprises, and the enchanting inland sea of classic adventure has led mankind gently from headland to headland, from bay to bay, from island to island, out into the promise of worldwide oceans beyond the Pillars of Hercules.

===Chinese===
航海最初的冲动在这片无潮汐的水域中获得了可见的形体——那里没有暗藏的浅滩和凶险的潮流，仿佛是对这门艺术的幼年怀有温柔的眷顾。地中海陡峭的海岸庇护着人类这一最大胆事业的初学者，而这片充满经典冒险的迷人内海，引领着人类从一个海角到另一个海角，从一个海湾到另一个海湾，从一座岛屿到另一座岛屿，走向赫拉克勒斯之柱（Pillars of Hercules）之外那环抱全球的海洋的应许之地。

### XXXIX / 第三十九篇

===Original===
The charm of the Mediterranean dwells in the unforgettable flavour of my early days, and to this hour this sea, upon which the Romans alone ruled without dispute, has kept for me the fascination of youthful romance. The very first Christmas night I ever spent away from land was employed in running before a Gulf of Lyons gale, which made the old ship groan in every timber as she skipped before it over the short seas until we brought her to, battered and out of breath, under the lee of Majorca, where the smooth water was torn by fierce cat's-paws under a very stormy sky.

===Chinese===
地中海（the Mediterranean）的魅力寄寓在我早年岁月那难忘的余韵之中，时至今日，这片唯有罗马人曾无可争议地统治过的海洋，对我依然保有青春浪漫的魅惑。我离陆地度过的第一个圣诞夜，就是在利昂湾（Gulf of Lyons）一场大风中顺风疾驶中度过的。那风让老船每根肋骨都在呻吟，她在短促的浪头上蹦跳着前行，直到我们把她带到马略卡岛（Majorca）的背风处，才总算停了下来——伤痕累累、气喘吁吁。在那里，平滑的水面被狂暴的猫爪风撕裂，头顶是一片极为阴沉的暴风雨天空。

===Original===
We—or, rather, they, for I had hardly had two glimpses of salt water in my life till then—kept her standing off and on all that day, while I listened for the first time with the curiosity of my tender years to the song of the wind in a ship's rigging. The monotonous and vibrating note was destined to grow into the intimacy of the heart, pass into blood and bone, accompany the thoughts and acts of two full decades, remain to haunt like a reproach the peace of the quiet fireside, and enter into the very texture of respectable dreams dreamed safely under a roof of rafters and tiles. The wind was fair, but that day we ran no more.

===Chinese===
我们——或者更准确地说是他们，因为在此之前我这辈子几乎没有见过两次海水——让她在那里来回迎风停泊了整整一天。而我，以少年特有的好奇，生平第一次倾听着风在船的索具间歌唱。那单调而震颤的音符，注定要深入心灵的亲密处，渗入血脉与骨骼，伴随我整整二十年的思绪与行动，像一种责备般萦绕在宁静的炉火旁，并进入那些在屋檐瓦片下安然做的体面梦境的肌理。风向有利，但那天我们再没有启航。

===Original===
The thing (I will not call her a ship twice in the same half-hour) leaked. She leaked fully, generously, overflowingly, all over—like a basket. I took an enthusiastic part in the excitement caused by that last infirmity of noble ships, without concerning myself much with the why or the wherefore. The surmise of my maturer years is that, bored by her interminable life, the venerable antiquity was simply yawning with ennui at every seam. But at the time I did not know; I knew generally very little, and least of all what I was doing in that galère.

===Chinese===
那玩意儿——我不会在同一个半小时里叫它两次"船"——漏水。她漏得很彻底、很慷慨、很汹涌，到处都漏——像一只篮子。我热情洋溢地参与了由高贵船只这最后的痼疾所引起的骚动，并不太关心缘由。以我后来更成熟的推测来看，大概是那条老古董厌倦了她漫长的一生，在每一道接缝处打着无聊的哈欠。但当时我什么都不知道；我总体上知之甚少，尤其不知道自己在那条破船（galère）上干什么。

===Original===
I remember that, exactly as in the comedy of Molière, my uncle asked the precise question in the very words—not of my confidential valet, however, but across great distances of land, in a letter whose mocking but indulgent turn ill concealed his almost paternal anxiety. I fancy I tried to convey to him my (utterly unfounded) impression that the West Indies awaited my coming. I had to go there. It was a sort of mystic conviction—something in the nature of a call. But it was difficult to state intelligibly the grounds of this belief to that man of rigorous logic, if of infinite charity.

===Chinese===
我记得，完全像莫里哀（Molière）的喜剧里那样，我的叔叔问了一模一样的问题——不过不是问他的心腹仆人，而是隔着遥远的陆地，在一封信中。那信的嘲弄而又纵容的语气，几乎掩饰不住他那近乎父亲般的焦虑。我想我曾试图向他传达我的（完全毫无根据的）印象：西印度群岛（the West Indies）在等待我的到来。我必须去那里。这是一种神秘的信念——某种近乎召唤的东西。但要对那个逻辑严谨、虽有无限仁慈的人说清楚这种信念的根据，实在难以言传。

===Original===
The truth must have been that, all unversed in the arts of the wily Greek, the deceiver of gods, the lover of strange women, the evoker of bloodthirsty shades, I yet longed for the beginning of my own obscure Odyssey, which, as was proper for a modern, should unroll its wonders and terrors beyond the Pillars of Hercules. The disdainful ocean did not open wide to swallow up my audacity, though the ship, the ridiculous and ancient galère of my folly, the old, weary, disenchanted sugar-wagon, seemed extremely disposed to open out and swallow up as much salt water as she could hold. This, if less grandiose, would have been as final a catastrophe.

===Chinese===
真相一定是这样的：尽管我全然不通那位狡猾的希腊人——欺瞒众神的骗子、陌生女人的情人、嗜血亡魂的召魂者——的种种技艺，我却渴望着自己那场隐晦的奥德赛（Odyssey）的开端。对于一个现代人来说，它理应在赫拉克勒斯之柱（Pillars of Hercules）以外展开它的奇迹与恐怖。那骄傲的大海并没有张开大口吞噬我的胆量，尽管那条船——我那愚蠢岁月中可笑的古老破船、那条疲惫的、幻灭的糖罐车（sugar-wagon）——却似乎极乐意裂开肚子，灌入尽可能多的海水。这虽说没那么壮丽，但也同样是一场终结性的灾难。

===Original===
But no catastrophe occurred. I lived to watch on a strange shore a black and youthful Nausicaa, with a joyous train of attendant maidens, carrying baskets of linen to a clear stream overhung by the heads of slender palm-trees. The vivid colours of their draped raiment and the gold of their earrings invested with a barbaric and regal magnificence their figures, stepping out freely in a shower of broken sunshine. The whiteness of their teeth was still more dazzling than the splendour of jewels at their ears. The shaded side of the ravine gleamed with their smiles. They were as unabashed as so many princesses, but, alas! not one of them was the daughter of a jet-black sovereign. Such was my abominable luck in being born by the mere hair's breadth of twenty-five centuries too late into a world where kings have been growing scarce with scandalous rapidity, while the few who remain have adopted the uninteresting manners and customs of simple millionaires.

===Chinese===
但灾难并未发生。我活着，在一个陌生的海岸上目睹了一位黝黑而年轻的瑙西卡亚（Nausicaa），带着一群欢快的侍女，头顶亚麻布篮子，走向一条清澈的溪流，溪流上方是纤细棕榈树的树冠。她们披裹的衣物色彩鲜艳，耳环的金光赋予她们的身姿以蛮野而王者般的辉煌——她们在斑驳的阳光中自由地迈步。她们洁白的牙齿比耳畔的宝石更加耀眼。峡谷的阴凉面因她们的笑容而熠熠生辉。她们像许多公主一样毫无羞怯，但唉！她们中没有一个是黑得发亮的君主的女儿。这就是我可悲的运气——仅仅差了区区二十五个世纪，就生得太晚了，降生在一个国王以令人震惊的速度日益稀少的世界，而那硕果仅存的几位，已经采用了乏味的百万富翁的做派和习俗。

===Original===
Obviously it was a vain hope in 187— to see the ladies of a royal household walk in chequered sunshine, with baskets of linen on their heads, to the banks of a clear stream overhung by the starry fronds of palm-trees. It was a vain hope. If I did not ask myself whether, limited by such discouraging impossibilities, life were still worth living, it was only because I had then before me several other pressing questions, some of which have remained unanswered to this day. The resonant, laughing voices of these gorgeous maidens scared away the multitude of hummingbirds, whose delicate wings wreathed with the mist of their vibration the tops of flowering bushes.

===Chinese===
显然，在187某年，期盼看到一个王室家庭的女眷们头顶亚麻布篮子、在斑驳的阳光中走向清澈溪流的岸边——溪流上方是星芒般的棕榈叶——不过是一个虚妄的幻想。那确实是虚妄的幻想。如果说我没有自问——在如此令人气馁的不可能面前，生活是否还值得一过，那只是因为我当时面前还有其他几个紧迫的问题，其中一些至今悬而未决。那些华丽少女们响亮而欢快的笑声吓跑了成群的蜂鸟，它们精致的翅膀扇动的薄雾缭绕在开花灌木的顶端。

===Original===
No, they were not princesses. Their unrestrained laughter filling the hot, fern-clad ravine had a soulless limpidity, as of wild, inhuman dwellers in tropical woodlands. Following the example of certain prudent travellers, I withdrew unseen—and returned, not much wiser, to the Mediterranean, the sea of classic adventures.

===Chinese===
不，她们不是公主。她们那无拘无束的笑声充满了闷热的、蕨类覆盖的峡谷，带着一种没有灵魂的澄澈——像热带丛林中那些野性的、非人间的居民。效仿某些谨慎的旅行者，我悄然退去——不太明智地，又回到了地中海，那片经典冒险的海洋。

## The *Tremolino* / 特雷莫利诺号

### XL / 第四十篇

===Original===
It was written that there, in the nursery of our navigating ancestors, I should learn to walk in the ways of my craft and grow in the love of the sea, blind as young love often is, but absorbing and disinterested as all true love must be. I demanded nothing from it—not even adventure. In this I showed, perhaps, more intuitive wisdom than high self-denial. No adventure ever came to one for the asking. He who starts on a deliberate quest of adventure goes forth but to gather dead-sea fruit, unless, indeed, he be beloved of the gods and great amongst heroes, like that most excellent cavalier Don Quixote de la Mancha. By us ordinary mortals of a mediocre animus that is only too anxious to pass by wicked giants for so many honest windmills, adventures are entertained like visiting angels. They come upon our complacency unawares. As unbidden guests are apt to do, they often come at inconvenient times. And we are glad to let them go unrecognized, without any acknowledgment of so high a favour. After many years, on looking back from the middle turn of life's way at the events of the past, which, like a friendly crowd, seem to gaze sadly after us hastening towards the Cimmerian shore, we may see here and there, in the grey throng, some figure glowing with a faint radiance, as though it had caught all the light of our already crepuscular sky. And by this glow we may recognize the faces of our true adventures, of the once unbidden guests entertained unawares in our young days.

===Chinese===
命运注定，就在那里——我们航海先祖的摇篮之地——我将学会行走在这门行当的道路上，在对大海的热爱中成长，像年轻的爱情常常那样盲目，却也像一切真正的爱情必然那样投入而无私。我从大海中什么也不索取——甚至不求冒险。在这一点上，我表现出的或许不是崇高的克己，而是比这更高明的直觉智慧。冒险从不因你的索取而来。那些蓄意追寻冒险的人出发去摘取的，不过是死海的苦果，除非他真如众神所钟爱、英雄中的豪杰——比如那位最出色的骑士堂吉诃德·德·拉·曼恰（Don Quixote de la Mancha）。而我们这些平庸的凡人，天性不过尔尔，过于急切地绕开邪恶的巨人去敬奉那些老实的风车，冒险却如同来访的天使般被我们迎进门来。它们在我们猝不及防时降临于我们的自满。正如不速之客惯常所做的那样，它们常常在不方便的时候到来。而我们倒乐得让它们悄然离去，不去承认这份莫大的恩惠。多年以后，从人生路途的中途回望过往的事件——它们如同一群友善的人群，似乎悲伤地目送我们匆匆走向钦默里安海岸（the Cimmerian shore）——我们或许会在灰色的人群中看到这里那里有一个人影闪烁着微弱的光芒，仿佛它已捕获了我们那已近黄昏的天空中的全部光亮。借着这微光，我们或许能辨认出那些真正冒险的面孔——我们年轻时不期而遇的不速之客。

===Original===
If the Mediterranean, the venerable (and sometimes atrociously ill-tempered) nurse of all navigators, was to rock my youth, the providing of the cradle necessary for that operation was entrusted by Fate to the most casual assemblage of irresponsible young men (all, however, older than myself) who, as if drunk with Provençal sunshine, frittered life away in joyous levity on the model of Balzac's *Histoire des Treize* qualified by a dash of romance *de cape et d'épée*.

She who was my cradle in those years had been built on the River of Savona by a famous builder of boats, was rigged in Corsica by another good man, and was described on her papers as a "tartane" of sixty tons. In reality, she was a true balancelle, with two short masts raking forward and two curved yards, each as long as her hull; a true child of the Latin lake, with a spread of two enormous sails resembling the pointed wings on a seabird's slender body, and herself, like a bird indeed, skimming rather than sailing the seas.

===Chinese===
如果地中海——所有航海者那令人敬畏（有时脾气坏得可怕）的乳母——注定要摇晃我的青春，那么为这摇篮提供必要的襁褓，命运便将它交给了最随意的一群不靠谱的年轻人（尽管他们个个比我年长），这些人仿佛被普罗旺斯的阳光灌醉了，以巴尔扎克（Balzac）《十三人故事》（*Histoire des Treize*）为范本，掺着几分披风与剑（*de cape et d'épée*）的浪漫，挥霍着生活，轻浮而快活。

那些年里充当我摇篮的那条船，由一位著名的造船匠在萨沃纳河（River of Savona）上建造，由另一位好心人在科西嘉装配索具，证件上登记为一艘六十吨的"塔尔塔纳帆船"（tartane）。实际上，她是一艘地道的巴朗瑟勒帆船（balancelle），两根短桅前倾，两根弯曲的横桁各与船体等长；一个真正的拉丁之湖的孩子，两面巨大的帆展开来像海鸟纤细身躯上尖尖的翅膀，而她自己——像一只鸟——与其说在航行，不如说在海面上掠过。

===Original===
Her name was the *Tremolino*. How is this to be translated? The *Quiverer*? What a name to give the pluckiest little craft that ever dipped her sides in angry foam! I had felt her, it is true, trembling for nights and days together under my feet, but it was with the high-strung tenseness of her faithful courage. In her short, but brilliant, career she has taught me nothing, but she has given me everything. I owe to her the awakened love for the sea that, with the quivering of her swift little body and the humming of the wind under the foot of her lateen sails, stole into my heart with a sort of gentle violence, and brought my imagination under its despotic sway. The *Tremolino*! To this day I cannot utter or even write that name without a strange tightening of the breast and the gasp of mingled delight and dread of one's first passionate experience.

===Chinese===
她的名字叫*特雷莫利诺号*（*Tremolino*）。这个名字该怎么译？*颤动号*（*The Quiverer*）？把这样的名字赐给一条曾在怒涛的白色泡沫中侧身破浪的最勇敢的小船，未免太不像话了！诚然，我感受过她在我的脚下日日夜夜地颤抖，但那是她忠实勇气的紧绷与亢奋。在她短暂而辉煌的生涯中，她没有教给我什么，却给了我一切。我对大海被唤醒的热爱，正是拜她所赐——伴随着她那灵巧小船身的震颤和拉丁帆脚下风的嗡鸣，这热爱以一种温柔的暴力潜入了我的心房，将我的想象力置于它专横的统治之下。*特雷莫利诺号*！直到今天，每当我念出或写下这个名字，胸口总会涌起一种奇异的紧缩感，夹杂着初次激情体验的欣喜与畏惧，令人屏息。

### XLI / 第四十一篇

===Original===
We four formed (to use a term well understood nowadays in every social sphere) a “syndicate” owning the *Tremolino*: an international and astonishing syndicate. And we were all ardent Royalists of the snow-white Legitimist complexion—Heaven only knows why! In all associations of men there is generally one who, by the authority of age and of a more experienced wisdom, imparts a collective character to the whole set. If I mention that the oldest of us was very old, extremely old—nearly thirty years old—and that he used to declare with gallant carelessness, “I live by my sword,” I think I have given enough information on the score of our collective wisdom. He was a North Carolinian gentleman, J. M. K. B. were the initials of his name, and he really did live by the sword, as far as I know. He died by it, too, later on, in a Balkanian squabble, in the cause of some Serbs or else Bulgarians, who were neither Catholics nor gentlemen—at least, not in the exalted but narrow sense he attached to that last word.

Poor J. M. K. B., *Américain, Catholique, et gentilhomme*, as he was disposed to describe himself in moments of lofty expansion! Are there still to be found in Europe gentlemen keen of face and elegantly slight of body, of distinguished aspect, with a fascinating drawing-room manner and with a dark, fatal glance, who live by their swords, I wonder? His family had been ruined in the Civil War, I fancy, and seems for a decade or so to have led a wandering life in the Old World. As to Henry C—, the next in age and wisdom of our band, he had broken loose from the unyielding rigidity of his family, solidly rooted, if I remember rightly, in a well-to-do London suburb. On their respectable authority he introduced himself meekly to strangers as a “black sheep.” I have never seen a more guileless specimen of an outcast. Never.

===Chinese===
我们四个人组成了（借用当今每个社交圈都熟知的术语）一个拥有特雷莫利诺号（Tremolino）的“辛迪加”：一个国际性的、令人惊异的辛迪加。而且我们全都是热忱的保皇党，纯白正统派的拥护者——天知道为什么！在所有人类的结盟中，总有一个人，凭借年长的权威与更老练的智慧，赋予整个群体一种集体性格。如果我提及我们中最年长的那位非常年迈，极其年迈——将近三十岁了——并且他惯于带着豪侠的漫不经心宣称“我以剑为生”，我想关于我们的集体智慧，我已提供了足够的信息。他是一位北卡罗来纳绅士，名字的首字母是 J. M. K. B.，据我所知，他确实以剑为生。后来，他也死于剑下，在一场巴尔干的小规模争端中，为一些塞尔维亚人或保加利亚人——他们既非天主教徒，也算不上绅士，至少，在他赋予后一词那种崇高而狭隘的意义上——的事业而死。

可怜的 J. M. K. B.，*Américain, Catholique, et gentilhomme*（美国人、天主教徒、绅士），这是他喜欢在慷慨陈词时如此自称！欧洲还能找到面容犀利、身材优雅苗条、仪表不凡、有着迷人沙龙风度以及一双深邃、致命眼眸，并且以剑为生的绅士吗？我很好奇。我想，他的家族在内战中破产，似乎有十来年在旧世界过着漂泊的生活。至于亨利·C—，我们中年龄与智慧居次的那一位，他挣脱了家族的严苛束缚——如果我记得没错，那家族牢固地扎根于伦敦一个富裕的郊区。他谦卑地以“害群之马”自称，这符合他们家族的体面权威。我从未见过比他更天真无邪的浪子样本。从未。

===Original===
However, his people had the grace to send him a little money now and then. Enamoured of the South, of Provence, of its people, its life, its sunshine and its poetry, narrow-chested, tall and shortsighted, he strode along the streets and the lanes, his long feet projecting far in advance of his body, and his white nose and gingery moustache buried in an open book: for he had the habit of reading as he walked. How he avoided falling into precipices, off the quays, or down staircases is a great mystery. The sides of his overcoat bulged out with pocket editions of various poets. When not engaged in reading Virgil, Homer, or Mistral, in parks, restaurants, streets, and suchlike public places, he indited sonnets (in French) to the eyes, ears, chin, hair, and other visible perfections of a nymph called Thérèse, the daughter, honesty compels me to state, of a certain Madame Leonore who kept a small café for sailors in one of the narrowest streets of the old town.

No more charming face, clear-cut like an antique gem, and delicate in colouring like the petal of a flower, had ever been set on, alas! a somewhat squat body. He read his verses aloud to her in the very café with the innocence of a little child and the vanity of a poet. We followed him there willingly enough, if only to watch the divine Thérèse laugh, under the vigilant black eyes of Madame Leonore, her母亲. She laughed very prettily, not so much at the sonnets, which she could not but esteem, as at poor Henry’s French accent, which was unique, resembling the warbling of birds, if birds ever warbled with a stuttering, nasal intonation.

Our third partner was Roger P. de la S—, the most Scandinavian-looking of Provençal squires, fair, and six feet high, as became a descendant of sea-roving Northmen, authoritative, incisive, wittily scornful, with a comedy in three acts in his pocket, and in his breast a heart blighted by a hopeless passion for his beautiful cousin, married to a wealthy hide and tallow merchant. He used to take us to lunch at their house without ceremony. I admired the good lady’s sweet patience. The husband was a conciliatory soul, with a great fund of resignation, which he expended on “Roger’s friends.” I suspect he was secretly horrified at these invasions. But it was a Carlist salon, and as such we were made welcome. The possibility of raising Catalonia in the interest of the *Rey netto*, who had just then crossed the Pyrenees, was much discussed there.

===Chinese===
不过，他的家人时不时会给他寄点钱来。他深爱南方，爱普罗旺斯（Provence）、爱那里的人、那里的生活、阳光与诗歌，他胸廓狭长，身材高挑，近视得厉害，他在大街小巷里阔步前行，他的长腿远远地伸在身体前面，他那白色的鼻子和姜黄色的胡须埋在一本摊开的书里：因为他有边走边读书的习惯。他如何能避开掉下悬崖、跌出码头、或滚下楼梯，这真是个天大的谜。他大衣两侧的口袋鼓鼓囊囊，装满了各种诗人的袖珍本。当不在公园、餐馆、街道这类公共场所读维吉尔、荷马或米斯特拉尔的书时，他便吟咏十四行诗（用法语），献给一位名叫泰蕾兹（Thérèse）的仙女的眼睛、耳朵、下巴、头发以及所有其他可见的完美之处——老实说，她是莱奥诺尔夫人（Madame Leonore）的女儿，后者在老城一条最狭窄的街道上开了一家小咖啡馆，专供水手光顾。

没有哪张更迷人的脸，如古宝石般轮廓分明，又如花瓣般色泽娇嫩，曾安在——唉！——一个有些矮胖的身躯上。他在咖啡馆里，以孩童的天真和诗人的虚荣，当面为她朗读自己的诗作。我们很乐意跟他去那里，哪怕只是为了看仙女泰蕾兹在母亲莱奥诺尔夫人警惕的黑眼睛注视下开怀大笑。她笑得非常可爱，与其说是被十四行诗——她不可能不欣赏它们——逗笑，不如说是被可怜的亨利那独特的法国口音逗乐，那口音如同鸟儿的啭鸣，如果鸟儿会以一种结结巴巴、鼻音浓重的调子鸣叫的话。

我们的第三位合伙人是罗杰·P·德拉·S—（Roger P. de la S—），普罗旺斯乡绅中最具北欧风情的一位，金发，身高六英尺，无愧于以海为家的北方人的后裔身份，他威严、敏锐、带着机智的嘲讽，口袋里装着一部三幕喜剧，心中则因对美丽表妹——她已嫁与一位富有的皮毛与油脂商——无望的激情而憔悴。他常不请自来地带我们去他们家吃午饭。我钦佩那位贤淑夫人的甜美耐心。那位丈夫是个随和的灵魂，有着取之不尽的逆来顺受，他将这份耐心都倾注在了“罗杰的朋友们”身上。我怀疑他内心对这些造访是惊恐的。但那是一个卡尔派沙龙，因此我们受到了欢迎。在那个沙龙里，人们广泛讨论着为刚刚越过比利牛斯山脉的*Rey netto*（正统国王）的利益而策动加泰罗尼亚起义的可能性。

===Original===
Don Carlos, no doubt, must have had many queer friends (it is the common lot of all Pretenders), but amongst them none more extravagantly fantastic than the *Tremolino* Syndicate, which used to meet in a tavern on the quays of the old port. The antique city of Massilia had surely never, since the days of the earliest Phoenicians, known an odder set of shipowners. We met to discuss and settle the plan of operations for each voyage of the *Tremolino*. In these operations a banking-house, too, was concerned—a very respectable banking-house. But I am afraid I shall end by saying too much. Ladies, too, were concerned (I am really afraid I am saying too much)—all sorts of ladies, some old enough to know better than to put their trust in princes, others young and full of illusions.

One of these last was extremely amusing in the imitations, she gave us in confidence, of various highly-placed personages she was perpetually rushing off to Paris to interview in the interests of the cause—*Por el Rey*! For she was a Carlist, and of Basque blood at that, with something of a lioness in the expression of her courageous face (especially when she let her hair down), and with the volatile little soul of a sparrow dressed in fine Parisian feathers, which had the trick of coming off disconcertingly at unexpected moments.

But her imitations of a Parisian personage, very highly placed indeed, as she represented him standing in the corner of a room with his face to the wall, rubbing the back of his head and moaning helplessly, “Rita, you are the death of me!” were enough to make one (if young and free from cares) split one’s sides laughing. She had an uncle still living, a very effective Carlist, too, the priest of a little mountain parish in Guipuzcoa. As the seagoing member of the syndicate (whose plans depended greatly on Doña Rita’s information), I used to be charged with humbly affectionate messages for the old man. These messages I was supposed to deliver to the Arragonese muleteers (who were sure to await at certain times the *Tremolino* in the neighbourhood of the Gulf of Rosas), for faithful transportation inland, together with the various unlawful goods landed secretly from under the *Tremolino*’s hatches.

===Chinese===
唐·卡洛斯（Don Carlos）无疑必定有许多古怪的朋友（这是所有觊觎王位者的共同命运），但在他们之中，没有比特雷莫利诺辛迪加更异想天开的了，这群人常在老港口码头上的一家酒馆碰面。这座古老的马赛利亚城（Massilia），自最早的腓尼基人时代以来，肯定从未见过如此古怪的一群船东。我们碰面是为了讨论并确定特雷莫利诺号每次航行的行动计划。在这些行动中，一家银行——一家非常体面的银行——也参与其中。但我恐怕会说得太多。女士们也参与其中（我真担心说得太多了）——形形色色的女士，有些年纪大到足以明白不该相信王子的承诺，另一些则年轻而充满幻想。

后一类人中有一位极为有趣，她私下里为我们模仿她常常为之奔走巴黎、为事业采访的各位身居高位的人物——*Por el Rey*（为了国王）！因为她是一位卡尔派，而且是巴斯克血统，她那勇敢的面容中——尤其是在她披散头发时——流露出几分母狮般的神气，而她那只穿着华丽巴黎羽毛的麻雀般变幻莫测的小灵魂，总在出其不意的时刻令人窘迫地脱落。

但她模仿那位巴黎要人——确实身居高位——的样子，堪称一绝：她把他描绘成站在房间角落，面朝墙壁，揉着后脑勺，无助地呻吟着，“丽塔（Rita），你简直要了我的命！”——这足以让一个（年轻而无忧无虑的）人笑得前仰后合。她还有一位在世的叔叔，也是一位相当活跃的卡尔派，是吉普斯夸省（Guipuzcoa）一个小山区教区的神父。作为辛迪加中负责出海的成员（我们的计划很大程度上依赖丽塔夫人（Doña Rita）的情报），我常被托付给这位老人传递谦卑而深情的问候。这些问候，我理应转交给阿拉贡的骡夫们（他们总会在特定时间在罗萨斯湾（Gulf of Rosas）附近等候特雷莫利诺号），由他们忠实地运往内地，同时运送的还有从特雷莫利诺号舱盖下秘密卸下的各种非法货物。

===Original===
Well, now, I have really let out too much (as I feared I should in the end) as to the usual contents of my sea-cradle. But let it stand. And if anybody remarks cynically that I must have been a promising infant in those days, let that stand, too. I am concerned but for the good name of the *Tremolino*, and I affirm that a ship is ever guiltless of the sins, transgressions, and follies of her men.

===Chinese===
好吧，现在，我确实泄露得太多了（正如我担心的那样），关于我这海上摇篮的日常内容。但就这样吧。如果有人讥讽地说我当年想必是个有出息的婴儿，那也由他去吧。我关心的只是特雷莫利诺号的好名声，我断言，一艘船永远无需为她的船员们的罪过、过失与愚蠢负责。

### XLII / 第四十二篇

===Original===
It was not *Tremolino*'s fault that the syndicate depended so much on the wit and wisdom and the information of Doña Rita. She had taken a little furnished house on the Prado for the good of the cause—*Por el Rey*! She was always taking little houses for somebody's good, for the sick or the sorry, for broken-down artists, cleaned-out gamblers, temporarily unlucky speculators—*vieux amis*—old friends, as she used to explain apologetically, with a shrug of her fine shoulders.

===Chinese===
辛迪加如此依赖丽塔夫人（Doña Rita）的智慧、见识与信息，并非特雷莫利诺号（*Tremolino*）的过错。她为了事业的益处——*Por el Rey*（为了国王）！——在普拉多大道租了一幢带家具的小房子。她总是为别人的好处租小房子，为那些病弱的或潦倒的人，为落魄的艺术家、输光的赌徒、暂时不走运的投机者——*vieux amis*（老朋友），她过去总是带着歉意地耸耸她那优美的肩膀这样解释。

===Original===
Whether Don Carlos was one of the "old friends," too, it's hard to say. More unlikely things have been heard of in smoking-rooms. All I know is that one evening, entering incautiously the salon of the little house just after the news of a considerable Carlist success had reached the faithful, I was seized round the neck and waist and whirled recklessly three times round the room, to the crash of upsetting furniture and the humming of a valse tune in a warm contralto voice.

===Chinese===
唐·卡洛斯（Don Carlos）是否也是"老朋友"之一，这很难说。在吸烟室里听到过更不可思议的事。我所知道的只是，一天晚上，就在卡利斯特（Carlist）取得重大胜利的消息传到忠臣们耳中之后，我不小心走进了那幢小房子的客厅，便被搂住脖子和腰，鲁莽地在房间里旋转了三圈，伴随着家具翻倒的碰撞声和一个温暖的女中音哼唱的华尔兹旋律。

===Original===
When released from the dizzy embrace, I sat down on the carpet—suddenly, without affectation. In this unpretentious attitude I became aware that J. M. K. B. had followed me into the room, elegant, fatal, correct and severe in a white tie and large shirtfront. In answer to his politely sinister, prolonged glance of inquiry, I overheard Doña Rita murmuring, with some confusion and annoyance, "*Vous êtes bête mon cher. Voyons! Ça n'a aucune conséquence.*" Well content in this case to be of no particular consequence, I had already about me the elements of some worldly sense.

===Chinese===
从那令人眩晕的拥抱中解脱出来后，我一屁股坐在了地毯上——突然的，毫不做作。在这不事张扬的姿态中，我意识到J. M. K. B.跟着我进了房间，他优雅、致命、得体而严肃，系着白领结，穿着大衬胸。为了回应他那礼貌而不祥的、探究的凝视，我无意中听见丽塔夫人带着几分困惑和烦恼喃喃道："*Vous êtes bête mon cher. Voyons! Ça n'a aucune conséquence.*（你真傻，亲爱的。瞧！这毫无关系。）"我很高兴在这种情况下自己确实无关紧要，我身上已有些许世故的端倪。

===Original===
Rearranging my collar, which, truth to say, ought to have been a round one above a short jacket, but was not, I observed felicitously that I had come to say goodbye, being ready to go off to sea that very night with the *Tremolino*. Our hostess, slightly panting yet, and just a shade dishevelled, turned tartly upon J. M. K. B., desiring to know when *he* would be ready to go off by the *Tremolino*, or in any other way, in order to join the royal headquarters. Did he intend, she asked ironically, to wait for the very eve of the entry into Madrid? Thus by a judicious exercise of tact and asperity we reestablished the atmospheric equilibrium of the room long before I left them a little before midnight, now tenderly reconciled, to walk down to the harbour and hail the *Tremolino* by the usual soft whistle from the edge of the quay. It was our signal, invariably heard by the ever-watchful Dominic, the padrone.

===Chinese===
我整了整领子——说实话，它本该是一件短夹克上的圆领，但并非如此——我机智地说我是来告别的，当晚就准备随特雷莫利诺号出海。我们的女主人，仍微微气喘，衣衫稍稍凌乱，尖刻地转向J. M. K. B.，想知道他什么时候准备好乘特雷莫利诺号，或以任何其他方式出发，以便加入王室大本营。她讽刺地问他，是否打算等到进入马德里的前夜才动身？就这样，通过机智地运用圆滑与尖刻，我们恢复了房间的气氛平衡；午夜前不久，我离开了他们——他们已温柔地和解了——走到港口，从码头边缘以惯常的轻柔口哨呼唤特雷莫利诺号。这是我们的信号，总是被时刻警觉的多米尼克（Dominic），我们的老板（padrone），听到。

===Original===
He would raise a lantern silently to light my steps along the narrow, springy plank of our primitive gangway. "And so we are going off," he would murmur directly my foot touched the deck. I was the harbinger of sudden departures, but there was nothing in the world sudden enough to take Dominic unawares. His thick black moustaches, curled every morning with hot tongs by the barber at the corner of the quay, seemed to hide a perpetual smile. But nobody, I believe, had ever seen the true shape of his lips. From the slow, imperturbable gravity of that broad-chested man you would think he had never smiled in his life. In his eyes lurked a look of perfectly remorseless irony, as though he had been provided with an extremely experienced soul; and the slightest distension of his nostrils would give to his bronzed face a look of extraordinary boldness. This was the only play of feature of which he seemed capable, being a Southerner of a concentrated, deliberate type. His ebony hair curled slightly on the temples. He may have been forty years old, and he was a great voyager on the inland sea.

===Chinese===
他会默默地举起一盏灯笼，照亮我沿着我们原始舷梯那狭窄而有弹性的跳板行走的脚步。"所以我们要出发了，"我的脚一踏上甲板，他便会低声说。我是突然启程的先兆，但世上没有任何事突然得能让多米尼克措手不及。他那浓密的黑色髭须——每天早上由码头拐角的理发师用热钳卷过——似乎隐藏着一种永恒的微笑。但我相信，没有人见过他嘴唇的真实形状。从这个宽胸男人缓慢、镇定的庄重，你会以为他这辈子从未笑过。他的眼中潜伏着一种完全无情的讽刺神情，仿佛他被赋予了一个极其老练的灵魂；而他鼻翼最轻微的翕动，便会让他那古铜色的脸庞显出非凡的勇猛。这是他似乎唯一能表现的面部活动，他是一个沉着、审慎类型的南方人。他乌黑的头发在太阳穴处微微卷曲。他大约四十岁，是内海上的伟大航行者。

===Original===
Astute and ruthless, he could have rivalled in resource the unfortunate son of Laertes and Anticlea. If he did not pit his craft and audacity against the very gods, it is only because the Olympian gods are dead. Certainly no woman could frighten him. A one-eyed giant would not have had the ghost of a chance against Dominic Cervoni, of Corsica, not Ithaca; and no king, son of kings, but of very respectable family—authentic Caporali, he affirmed. But that is as it may be. The Caporali families date back to the twelfth century.

===Chinese===
精明而无情，他的智谋足以与拉厄耳忒斯（Laertes）和安提克勒亚（Anticlea）那不幸的儿子匹敌。如果他没有将自己的狡诈与胆量与众神本身抗衡，那只是因为奥林匹斯诸神已死。当然，没有哪个女人能吓倒他。一个独眼巨人对科西嘉的多米尼克·切尔沃尼（Dominic Cervoni）也毫无胜算——不是伊萨卡（Ithaca）的；而且他并非国王之子，而是出身于非常体面的家族——正宗的卡波拉利（Caporali），他断言。但究竟如何不得而知。卡波拉利家族可追溯到十二世纪。

===Original===
For want of more exalted adversaries Dominic turned his audacity fertile in impious stratagems against the powers of the earth, as represented by the institution of Customhouses and every mortal belonging thereto—scribes, officers, and *guardacostas* afloat and ashore. He was the very man for us, this modern and unlawful wanderer with his own legend of loves, dangers, and bloodshed. He told us bits of it sometimes in measured, ironic tones. He spoke Catalonian, the Italian of Corsica and the French of Provence with the same easy naturalness. Dressed in shore-togs, a white starched shirt, black jacket, and round hat, as I took him once to see Doña Rita, he was extremely presentable. He could make himself interesting by a tactful and rugged reserve set off by a grim, almost imperceptible, playfulness of tone and manner.

===Chinese===
由于缺少更高贵的对手，多米尼克将他的胆量化作不敬的诡计，瞄准世俗的权力——以海关机构及其所有隶属人员为代表：文书、官员，以及水上和岸上的*guardacostas*（缉私巡逻员）。他正是我们需要的人，这个现代的、非法的流浪者，有着自己关于爱情、危险与流血的传奇。他有时以克制的、讽刺的语调向我们讲述其中的片段。他说加泰罗尼亚语、科西嘉的意大利语和普罗旺斯的法语，都同样自如自然。有一次我带他去见丽塔夫人，他穿着岸上的行头——一件浆过的白衬衫、黑色夹克和圆顶帽——极其体面。他能通过一种得体而粗犷的矜持——辅以一种冷峻的、几乎难以察觉的语调与举止中的俏皮——让自己显得有趣。

===Original===
He had the physical assurance of strong-hearted men. After half an hour's interview in the dining-room, during which they got in touch with each other in an amazing way, Rita told us in her best grande dame manner: "*Mais il est parfait, cet homme.*" He was perfect. On board the *Tremolino*, wrapped up in a black *caban*, the picturesque cloak of Mediterranean seamen, with those massive moustaches and his remorseless eyes set off by the shadow of the deep hood, he looked piratical and monkish and darkly initiated into the most awful mysteries of the sea.

===Chinese===
他拥有内心强大之人特有的身体上的自信。在餐厅里会谈了半小时——其间他们以惊人的速度相互了解——丽塔夫人以她最佳的贵妇姿态告诉我们："*Mais il est parfait, cet homme.*（这个人真是完美。）"他是完美的。在特雷莫利诺号上，裹在一件黑色*caban*（地中海水手标志性的斗篷）里，带着那浓密的髭须和无情的眼睛，在深兜帽的阴影映衬下，他看起来既像海盗，又像修士，仿佛深谙大海最可怖的奥秘。

### XLIII / 第四十三篇

===Original===
Anyway, he was perfect, as Doña Rita had declared. The only thing unsatisfactory (and even inexplicable) about our Dominic was his nephew, Cesar. It was startling to see a desolate expression of shame veil the remorseless audacity in the eyes of that man superior to all scruples and terrors.

“I would never have dared to bring him on board your balancelle,” he once apologized to me. “But what am I to do? His mother is dead, and my brother has gone into the bush.”

In this way I learned that our Dominic had a brother. As to “going into the bush,” this only means that a man has done his duty successfully in the pursuit of a hereditary vendetta. The feud which had existed for ages between the families of Cervoni and Brunaschi was so old that it seemed to have smouldered out at last. One evening Pietro Brunaschi, after a laborious day amongst his olive-trees, sat on a chair against the wall of his house with a bowl of broth on his knees and a piece of bread in his hand. Dominic’s brother, going home with a gun on his shoulder, found a sudden offence in this picture of content and rest so obviously calculated to awaken the feelings of hatred and revenge. He and Pietro had never had any personal quarrel; but, as Dominic explained, “all our dead cried out to him.” He shouted from behind a wall of stones, “O Pietro! Behold what is coming!” And as the other looked up innocently he took aim at the forehead and squared the old vendetta account so neatly that, according to Dominic, the dead man continued to sit with the bowl of broth on his knees and the piece of bread in his hand.

This is why—because in Corsica your dead will not leave you alone—Dominic’s brother had to go into the maquis, into the bush on the wild mountainside, to dodge the gendarmes for the insignificant remainder of his life, and Dominic had charge of his nephew with a mission to make a man of him.

===Chinese===
无论如何，他是完美的，正如丽塔夫人（Doña Rita）所宣称的那样。关于我们的多米尼克（Dominic），唯一令人不满（甚至无法解释）之处，便是他的侄子切萨尔（Cesar）。看到一丝绝望的羞愧神情，掩盖了那双超越一切顾虑与恐惧的眼睛里无情的胆大妄为，着实令人惊愕。

“我绝不敢把他带上你的巴朗瑟勒帆船（balancelle），”他曾向我致歉。“但我有什么办法呢？他的母亲去世了，而我的兄弟进了丛林。”

由此我得知，我们的多米尼克还有一个兄弟。至于“进了丛林”，这仅仅意味着一个人成功地履行了追讨世仇的职责。切沃尼（Cervoni）与布鲁纳斯基（Brunaschi）两大家族间存续了数个世纪的世仇，久远到似乎终于渐渐平息了。一天傍晚，皮埃特罗·布鲁纳斯基（Pietro Brunaschi）在橄榄园劳作了一整天后，背靠着自家的墙坐在椅子上，膝上放着一碗肉汤，手里拿着一块面包。多米尼克的兄弟扛着枪回家，看到这幅满足与安歇的画面——它显然旨在唤醒仇恨与复仇的情感——顿时心生冒犯。他和皮埃特罗素无个人恩怨；但是，正如多米尼克解释的那样，“我们所有的死者都在向他呼喊。”他从一堵石墙后喊道：“噢，皮埃特罗！看哪，什么来了！”当对方天真地抬起头时，他瞄准额头，漂亮地清算了那笔旧日的仇账，以至于，据多米尼克说，那死者继续坐在那里，膝上放着那碗肉汤，手里拿着那块面包。

这就是为什么——因为在科西嘉（Corsica），你的死者不会让你安宁——多米尼克的兄弟不得不躲进灌木丛（maquis），躲进荒山野岭的丛林里，为余下那微不足道的生命躲避宪兵，而多米尼克则担起了照管侄子的责任，使命是将他培养成一个男子汉。

===Original===
No more unpromising undertaking could be imagined. The very material for the task seemed wanting. The Cervonis, if not handsome men, were good sturdy flesh and blood. But this extraordinarily lean and livid youth seemed to have no more blood in him than a snail.

“Some cursed witch must have stolen my brother’s child from the cradle and put that spawn of a starved devil in its place,” Dominic would say to me. “Look at him! Just look at him!”

To look at Cesar was not pleasant. His parchment skin, showing dead white on his cranium through the thin wisps of dirty brown hair, seemed to be glued directly and tightly upon his big bones, Without being in any way deformed, he was the nearest approach which I have ever seen or could imagine to what is commonly understood by the word “monster.” That the source of the effect produced was really moral I have no doubt. An utterly, hopelessly depraved nature was expressed in physical terms, that taken each separately had nothing positively startling. You imagined him clammily cold to the touch, like a snake. The slightest reproof, the most mild and justifiable remonstrance, would be met by a resentful glare and an evil shrinking of his thin dry upper lip, a snarl of hate to which he generally added the agreeable sound of grinding teeth.

It was for this venomous performance rather than for his lies, impudence, and laziness that his uncle used to knock him down. It must not be imagined that it was anything in the nature of a brutal assault. Dominic’s brawny arm would be seen describing deliberately an ample horizontal gesture, a dignified sweep, and Cesar would go over suddenly like a ninepin—which was funny to see. But, once down, he would writhe on the deck, gnashing his teeth in impotent rage—which was pretty horrible to behold. And it also happened more than once that he would disappear completely—which was startling to observe. This is the exact truth. Before some of these majestic cuffs Cesar would go down and vanish. He would vanish heels overhead into open hatchways, into scuttles, behind upended casks, according to the place where he happened to come into contact with his uncle’s mighty arm.

===Chinese===
再没有比这更没有指望的事业了。完成这项任务所需的材料似乎都付之阙如。切沃尼家族的人，即便算不上英俊，也都是结实健壮的血肉之躯。但这个异常消瘦、面色青灰的年轻人，体内的血液似乎还不如一只蜗牛多。

“某个该死的巫婆一定从摇篮里偷走了我兄弟的孩子，把一个饿瘪了的恶魔崽子塞了进去，”多米尼克会这样对我说。“看看他！你看看他！”

看着切萨尔并不愉快。他那羊皮纸般的皮肤，透过肮脏棕发的稀疏发绺，在头颅上显出死气沉沉的白色，仿佛直接而紧紧地粘在他的大骨头上。他并未畸形，却是我见过或能想象的最接近“怪物”这一通常含义的存在。我毫不怀疑，造成这种效果的根源实则是道德上的。一种彻底、无可救药的堕落天性，用肉体的语言表达出来，但若单独审视每个部分，却并无绝对惊悚之处。你想象他摸上去黏糊糊的，冰凉如蛇。最轻微的责备，最温和、最情有可原的规劝，都会招来一个怨恨的怒视，以及他那薄薄的、干枯上唇的邪恶收缩，一声充满恨意的狺狺低吼，通常还伴随着那令人愉快的磨牙声。

正是为了这恶毒的表演，而非他的谎言、无耻和懒惰，他的叔叔常把他打倒在地。切莫想象这是某种野蛮的殴打。多米尼克那肌肉发达的手臂会从容地划出一个幅度很大的水平手势，一个庄严的横扫，切萨尔便会像九柱戏的木柱般突然倒下——这景象颇为滑稽。但一旦倒下，他便会在甲板上扭曲翻滚，愤怒地咬牙切齿——这景象就相当可怕了。而且，不止一次，他会完全消失——这景象就令人惊愕了。这是千真万确的。在某些这般威严的耳光下，切萨尔会倒下并消失。他会头朝下倒栽进敞开的舱口，掉进舷窗，或者翻倒在竖起的木桶后面，这取决于他恰好在何处接触到他叔叔那条强有力的手臂。

===Original===
Once—it was in the old harbour, just before the *Tremolino*’s last voyage—he vanished thus overboard to my infinite consternation. Dominic and I had been talking business together aft, and Cesar had sneaked up behind us to listen, for, amongst his other perfections, he was a consummate eavesdropper and spy. At the sound of the heavy plop alongside horror held me rooted to the spot; but Dominic stepped quietly to the rail and leaned over, waiting for his nephew’s miserable head to bob up for the first time.
